### Filesystem watcher
- id: file-watcher
- feature: Save pipeline & file watching
- summary: A Rust `notify` watcher per window reports external changes under the workspace root (or the active document's folder) as `fs:changed` batches addressed to that window, which a coalescing event bus fans out to the file tree and the external-change handler.
- capabilities: per-`watch_id` (window label) recursive watchers, started off the IPC thread; changes collected for 75 ms and emitted as one batch of at most 512, in order, a repeat dropped only when nothing else touched its paths since; a bounded queue that blocks the watcher thread rather than drop; `emit_to` the owning window, and a listener registered on that window; a watcher error or the OS's overflow report surfaced as `rescan`, which re-lists the file tree; paths rebased onto the requested root rather than the OS realpath; `.git`, `.svn`, `.hg`, `node_modules`, `.obsidian`, `__pycache__`, `.DS_Store`, `.Trash` filtered while `.github`/`.vscode` pass; a bare Windows drive root refused as a watch root; start/stop serialized per window on the frontend; a destroyed window's watcher stopped natively in `app_setup.rs`, and a watcher that finishes starting after its window closed removed again; normalized, deduplicated, self-write-flagged events coalesced into batches
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/watcher.rs`, `src-tauri/src/watcher/batch.rs`, `src-tauri/src/watcher/paths.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, `src/hooks/useWindowFileWatcher.ts`, `src/utils/watchRoot.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/workspaceEventBus.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/normalizeFsEvents.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/subscribeWorkspaceEvents.ts`
- rust: `watcher::start_watching`, `watcher::stop_watching`
- docs: `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"External Changes"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/watcher.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/watcher/batch.test.rs`, `src/hooks/useWindowFileWatcher.test.ts`, `src/utils/watchRoot.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/workspaceEventBus.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/normalizeFsEvents.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/subscribeWorkspaceEvents.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaceEvents/suppressUnchanged.test.ts`, `src/components/Sidebar/FileExplorer/useFileTree.watcherRescan.test.tsx`
- notes: no glob or recursive ignore patterns (component-based filtering only). A `rescan` re-lists the tree, but the external-change handler does not yet re-verify open documents on it, so a change the OS dropped can still leave an open document one write behind. Watchers live in a process-global static rather than `.manage()` state. The `watchRoot.ts` header names a `useWorkspaceEventBus` hook that does not exist.

### Document version history (snapshots)
- id: document-history
- feature: Document history / snapshots
- summary: Each save of a file-backed document records a timestamped snapshot in app data, browsable, restorable and deletable from the History sidebar view.
- capabilities: snapshot per save with the save kind recorded; per-document folder `{appDataDir}/history/{hash}/` holding `index.json` plus one `{id}.md` file per snapshot; preview text; prune by count and by age; merge window consolidates consecutive auto-saves into one snapshot; file-size guard skips oversized files before any I/O; restore a version with a confirm dialog (current content snapshotted first); delete one snapshot; clear this document's history from the sidebar; clear workspace history and clear all history from the File menu (confirmed, re-entry guarded); one pinned warning toast per session when the history backend fails
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `general.historyEnabled = true`, `general.historyMaxSnapshots = 50`, `general.historyMaxAgeDays = 7`, `general.historyMergeWindow = 30`, `general.historyMaxFileSize = 512`

- code: `src/services/history/historyOperations.ts`, `src/services/history/historyRecovery.ts`, `src/services/persistence/saveHistorySnapshot.ts`, `src/utils/historyTypes.ts`, `src/components/Sidebar/HistoryView.tsx`, `src/services/commands/miscCommands.ts`

- tests: `src/services/history/historyOperations.test.ts`, `src/services/history/__tests__/historyOperations.test.ts`, `src/services/history/historyRecovery.test.ts`, `src/services/history/__tests__/historyRecovery.test.ts`, `src/utils/historyTypes.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/saveToPath.test.ts`; no dedicated test for `src/components/Sidebar/HistoryView.tsx` (mocked out in `src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx`)
- notes: untitled documents have no history. A snapshot failure never fails the save but is awaited, so a hung history backend holds the save promise open. The `historyOperations.ts` header still says it "Lives in hooks/".

### Unified undo/redo across WYSIWYG and Source
- id: unified-history
- feature: Document history / snapshots
- summary: Undo and redo cross the WYSIWYG ⇄ Source boundary — when the active editor's native history is exhausted, checkpoints recorded at each mode switch continue the chain without changing the current view mode.
- capabilities: checkpoint on every mode switch (`toggleSourceModeWithCheckpoint`); native undo/redo first, checkpoints only once native depth is 0; content restored, mode never switched; redo entries carry a `branchBase` so a redo whose branch was abandoned by a new edit is refused; per-tab stacks cleared on tab close; only active document tabs participate; stacks captured and restored by hot exit
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: Mod-Z / Mod-Shift-Z; Edit menu Undo / Redo
- code: `src/services/history/unifiedHistory.ts`, `src/services/history/unifiedUndoRedo.ts`, `src/stores/documentStore/unifiedHistory.ts`, `src/services/editor/unifiedHistoryDispatch.ts`
- rust: none (persisted through `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/session.rs`)
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Undo Across Modes"
- tests: `src/services/history/unifiedHistory.test.ts`, `src/services/history/checkpointRedoBranch.test.ts`, `src/stores/documentStore/__tests__/unifiedHistory.test.ts`, `src/stores/unifiedHistoryStore.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/06-undo-redo.mjs`
- notes: an ordinary edit never reaches the checkpoint store, which is why the redo stack cannot be discarded the way a native editor does — hence `branchBase`. The `performUnifiedUndo` doc comment in `unifiedUndoRedo.ts` still says a checkpoint "may trigger mode switch"; `restoreFromCheckpoint` never switches mode.

### Hot exit (session capture and restore)
- id: hot-exit
- feature: Hot exit / session recovery
- summary: The full multi-window session — tabs, unsaved content, cursors, undo/redo, UI state, geometry, workspace instances — can be captured to `session.json` and is restored on the next launch; capture currently runs only on an updater restart or from the developer tools.
- capabilities: coordinator broadcasts a capture request to every window with a 5 s timeout; partial captures merge with the previous session unless it is older than 1 h; `hot-exit:partial-capture` warning toast; atomic write with SHA-256 dedup against the last write; read ladder = strict parse → per-item salvage → `session.prev.json` backup; structural validation with auto-repair; schema v5 with Rust and TypeScript migrations; Zod salvage plus quarantine of unparseable fragments on the frontend; session file kept when the payload was salvaged or served from the backup; multi-window restore plan (N saved windows → N windows); pull-based per-window state retrieval; a restoring window re-grants its workspace roots before reading them, waiting at most 5 s (`RESTORED_GRANT_WAIT_MS`); sessions older than 7 days ignored
- status: partial
- gate: always on (no user-facing toggle)
- surfaces: automatic on launch (main window); auto-update "restart to install"; settings pane Advanced → Developer tools → Hot Exit Dev Tools
- code: `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/coordinator.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/storage.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/read_session.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/salvage.rs`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restartWithHotExit.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/sessionSalvage.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/useDocumentResilience.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_hotExitCapture.ts`, `src/services/persistence/resilience/_hotExitRestore.ts`, `src/hooks/useHotExitCaptureWarning.ts`
- rust: `hot_exit_capture`, `hot_exit_restore`, `hot_exit_restore_multi_window`, `hot_exit_inspect_session`, `hot_exit_clear_session`, `hot_exit_get_window_state`, `hot_exit_window_restore_complete`
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Session Recovery (Hot Exit)" (update-restart capture; an ordinary quit does not capture); `website/guide/workspace-rail.md` (hot exit restores every workspace of the window)
- tests: Rust `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/coordinator.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/coordinator_pins.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/merge.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/salvage.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/validation.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/session.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/state.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/restore_plan.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/read_session.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/migration_v3_tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/migration_v4_tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/migration_v5_tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/hot_exit/path_containment_tests.rs`; TS `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restartWithHotExit.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restartWithHotExit.salvage.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/multiWindowRestore.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restoreTabsDedup.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/schemaMigration.test.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_hotExitCapture.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/resilience/_hotExitRestore.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/resilience/_hotExitRestore.grant.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useHotExitCaptureWarning.test.ts`
- notes: `hot_exit_capture` is invoked only from `restartWithHotExit()` (called by `src/hooks/useUpdateChecker.ts`) and `src/pages/settings/HotExitDevTools.tsx`; nothing captures on quit or window close, which the guide now states. Rust's inspect payload reports `lossy_repair` / `repair_summary` and says the frontend must quarantine the main file before clearing it, but no frontend code reads either field, so a lossy-repaired session is cleared after a successful restore. The comment in `restartWithHotExit.ts` still calls the `recoveredFromBackup` arm unreachable pending a TODO that no longer exists (Rust reports the flag) and says "the next quit overwrites it", which no quit does.

### Crash recovery snapshots
- id: crash-recovery
- feature: Hot exit / session recovery
- summary: Dirty buffers are snapshotted to disk every 10 s so an unexpected exit can restore them as dirty tabs on the next launch.
- capabilities: per-window writer comparing content, path and title by value; atomic tmp + rename write under `{appDataDir}/recovery/`; startup scan runs after hot-exit restore on the main window only; recovered tabs created in the background with the active tab preserved; toast by outcome (all restored → info, partial → warning with counts, total failure → pinned error); snapshot removed when a tab closes, when a document goes clean, and on normal window unload; files older than 7 days deleted
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic; startup toast
- code: `src/services/persistence/crashRecovery.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryWriter.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryStartup.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryCleanup.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/useDocumentResilience.ts`, `src/hooks/lifecycle/MainWindowRunners.tsx`

- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Session Recovery (Hot Exit)" (crash-recovery and 7-day cleanup bullets)
- tests: `src/services/persistence/crashRecovery.test.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryWriter.test.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryStartup.test.ts`, `src/hooks/resilience/_crashRecoveryCleanup.test.ts`
- notes: composed with hot exit behind `useDocumentResilience()` (per window) and `useResilienceStartup()` (main window, hot-exit startup before the recovery scan). The `useDocumentResilience.ts` header still mentions "a documented state machine"; that module no longer exists.

### Workspace session tab restore
- id: session-tab-restore
- feature: Tabs & windows
- summary: Opening a workspace reopens the document tabs that were last open in it, in order, and its split layout.
- capabilities: reads the versioned `sessionTabs` field, falling back to legacy `lastOpenTabs: string[]`; downgrade-safe (both fields written; an older binary reads `lastOpenTabs`); forward-tolerant (unknown `kind` or version skipped); untrusted entries validated and skipped individually; skips paths already open (e.g. from hot exit); restored text tabs claim file ownership like every other open; a lone clean Untitled tab is closed once something is restored; restore activations are MRU-neutral; split layout restored after the tabs; `openWorkspaceByPath` does not grant; its caller settles access first: the picker grants the folder it returns, Open Recent and MCP `open_workspace` ask Rust through `resolveWorkspaceAccess`, and `src/contexts/WindowContext.tsx` re-grants a window's startup workspace before it is read (Rust grants only a folder the user chose; a refusal for a folder the static scope covers is the ordinary answer)

### Reload guard
- id: reload-guard

### Multi-window persistence plumbing
- id: persistence-plumbing
- feature: Hot exit / session recovery
- summary: Not user-facing: localStorage keys shared by several windows merge instead of overwriting, and a quota failure surfaces once per store instead of silently losing data.
- capabilities: `createSafeStorage` catches `QuotaExceededError` and toasts once per store key; `persistedListMerge` read-merge-writes list-shaped keys (recent files and workspaces, prompt history, genie recents); `persistedSectionMerge` writes only the sections this window changed against a hydration baseline (settings, shortcuts); `instanceRekeyBus` fans out loose-instance identity re-keys to stores that cannot import the owner (closed-tab scopes)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/services/persistence/safeStorage.ts`, `src/stores/persistedListMerge.ts`, `src/stores/persistedSectionMerge.ts`, `src/stores/instanceRekeyBus.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none
- tests: `src/services/persistence/safeStorage.test.ts`, `src/stores/persistedListMerge.test.ts`, `src/stores/persistedSectionMerge.test.ts`; no dedicated test for `src/stores/instanceRekeyBus.ts`
- notes: deliberate tradeoff (no tombstones) — a removal in one window can be resurrected by a later merge in another, and if two windows change the same section between reads the later write wins that section.

## Area 6 — Export and print

Verified: `2675ad132`

Every export command is registered in `src/services/commands/exportCommands.ts` (palette + menu events); the frontend operations live under `src/export/`, the native PDF and print pipeline under `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/`, and Pandoc under `src-tauri/src/pandoc/`. The Knowledge Base content server and Slidev deck export are Area 11 (*content-server-kb*, *slidev*), not part of this pipeline.

### Export HTML (document folder)
- id: export-html
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Writes the current document as a folder holding both a linked `index.html` and an all-embedded `standalone.html`, plus an `assets/` tree, rendered with the same Tiptap extensions and CSS the WYSIWYG editor uses.
- capabilities: always emits BOTH `index.html` (external CSS/JS/images/fonts) and `standalone.html` (local assets as data URIs; remote http(s) images stay remote); `assets/vmark-reader.css` + `assets/vmark-reader.js` always shipped; `assets/images/` and `assets/fonts/` when needed; theme snapshot embedded with the light theme forced (`forceLightTheme: true`); editor+plugin CSS bundle inlined; KaTeX shipped only when the document contains math (CDN stylesheet in `index.html`, inlined CSS and woff2 fonts in `standalone.html`); a curated subset of web fonts (Literata; JetBrains Mono, Fira Code, IBM Plex Mono) downloaded from Google Fonts and embedded when chosen in Settings; default folder name from the first H1 or the file basename; save panel with no `filters`, a trailing `.html` stripped to make the folder name but never down to the parent folder; missing-image and asset warning toasts with a real count; empty documents refused
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu id `export-html` (File → Export → HTML…); palette command `export.html` ("Export as HTML"); shortcut id `exportHTML`, default key empty (menu only)
- code: `src/export/exportToHtmlFolder.ts`, `src/export/htmlExport.ts`, `src/export/htmlExportAssets.ts`, `src/export/htmlTemplates.ts`, `src/export/htmlExportStyles.ts`, `src/export/fontEmbedder.ts`, `src/export/exportGuards.ts`, `src/utils/exportNaming.ts`, `src/services/commands/exportCommands.ts`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/export_menu.rs`
- rust: none (frontend + `@tauri-apps/plugin-fs`)
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"What an Export Produces", §"Export HTML", §"Offline Viewing"; `website/guide/features.md` §"HTML Export"; `website/guide/shortcuts.md` ("Export HTML | Menu only")
- tests: `src/export/__tests__/htmlExportCleanup.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/htmlExportAssets.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/htmlExportResources.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/htmlTemplatesKatex.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportFolderPath.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportWarningCount.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/fontEmbedder.test.ts`, `src/utils/exportNaming.test.ts`, `src/services/commands/exportCommands.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/38-export-html-to-disk.mjs` (calls the render and writer directly — the native save panel is not driven)
- notes: Only the Latin and mono settings are passed to the font step (`exportToHtml` sends `latinFont` and `monoFont`); the CJK font is never embedded, and a `custom:<family>` font (#1429) or any curated family outside the Google Fonts map falls back to the reader's system stack. An offline export silently gets no embedded web font beyond a warning.

### HTML export staging, rollback and destination lock
- id: export-html-staging
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: An HTML export is all-or-nothing: every file is written under a private staging folder inside the destination, published by rename, and rolled back from backups if a rename fails, with a lock file so two windows cannot export into one folder at once.
- capabilities: staging root `.vmark-export-<nonce>` inside the destination (rename never crosses a device); replaced files renamed aside into `.replaced/` and restored in reverse on failure (rename first, `copyFile` fallback, never remove-then-rename); an unrestorable backup is retained and its path named in the error; per-webview serialization (`runExclusive`) plus a cross-window lock file `.vmark-export.lock` taken with an exclusive create; 30 s wait for a held lock, stale takeover after 5 min by `rename`; per-acquisition owner nonce re-checked immediately before publish; reserved `.vmark-export` names refused as publish targets
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic; user-visible as the "Another export is already writing to …" error and a leftover `.vmark-export-…` folder when a restore failed
- code: `src/export/exportStaging.ts`, `src/export/exportPublish.ts`, `src/export/exportLock.ts`, `src/export/exportLockFile.ts`, `src/export/exportLockTakeover.ts`, `src/export/exportErrorMessages.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Re-exporting into a folder you have used before"
- tests: `src/export/__tests__/exportStaging.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportLock.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportErrorsLocalized.test.ts` (the first two over the in-memory `src/export/__tests__/exportFsHarness.ts`); no test runs against a real Windows filesystem
- notes: Every lock-contention, take-over, not-a-file, publish and rollback message is an i18n lookup (`dialog:exportError.*`, present in all ten `src/locales/*/dialog.json`) resolved in `exportErrorMessages.ts`, with paths passed as parameters; it reaches the user as the detail line of the export-failed dialog.

### Export PDF (settings window → native renderer)
- id: export-pdf
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Opens a dedicated 440×640 native window with a PDF settings sidebar, then renders the document to a paginated PDF through the platform's own webview print pipeline and opens the result in the OS viewer.
- capabilities: page size A4/Letter/A3/Legal; portrait/landscape (a width/height swap); margin presets Normal/Narrow/Wide/Custom plus a draggable four-side margin diagram (mm); font size 10–14pt; line height 1.4–2.0; CJK letter spacing off/0.02/0.05/0.08em; Latin and CJK font lists shared with Settings (`src/utils/fontOptions.ts`), with a `custom:<family>` value carried into the list; style presets Default/Academic/Compact/Elegant plus auto-detected "Custom"; "Use Editor Theme" toggle (otherwise light is forced); `<details>` forced open; oversized images and diagram previews bounded to the printable height; KaTeX woff2 fonts inlined as data URIs; progress stages loading/rendering/finishing/done from `pdf-export-progress`; save panel without `filters`, `.pdf` suffix guaranteed idempotently; partial-success pinned toast naming a failed post-processing step; one export at a time (`ExportGate`, a second is refused with `Conflict`); auto-open via `plugin-opener`; window built in Rust with an empty menu on Windows/Linux (#1377), centred over the calling window
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu id `export-pdf-native` (File → Export → PDF…); palette command `export.pdfNative` ("Export as PDF"); shortcut id `exportPdf`, default key empty; window label `pdf-export` at route `/pdf-export`
- code: `src/export/useExportOperations.ts` (`exportToPdfNative`), `src/services/navigation/pdfExportWindow.ts`, `src/pages/PdfExportPage.tsx`, `src/export/PdfExportDialog.tsx`, `src/export/PdfSettingsSidebar.tsx`, `src/export/PdfSidebarPrimitives.tsx`, `src/export/pdfHtmlTemplate.ts`, `src/export/pdfOptions.ts`, `src/export/pdfPresets.ts`, `src/export/pageSpec.ts`, `src-tauri/src/window_manager/pdf_export_window.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/export_gate.rs`, `src-tauri/capabilities/pdf-export.json`
- rust: `export_pdf`, `open_pdf_export_window`, `write_temp_html`; `pdf_export::export_gate::ExportGate` (managed state)
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Print / Export PDF"; `website/guide/features.md` §"PDF Export"; `website/guide/shortcuts.md` ("Export PDF | —")
- tests: `src/export/__tests__/PdfExportDialog.test.tsx`, `src/export/__tests__/PdfSettingsSidebar.test.tsx`, `src/export/__tests__/pdfHtmlTemplate.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/pdfPresets.test.ts`, `src/export/pdfExportError.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportDialogNamespaces.test.ts`, `src/export/pageSpec.test.ts`, `src/services/navigation/pdfExportWindow.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/window_manager/pdf_export_window.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/export_gate.test.rs`; `src/pages/PdfExportPage.tsx` has no dedicated test
- notes: Settings-only by design — no preview (`PdfExportDialog.tsx` header). The `pdf-export` window capability grants read of `$APPDATA/temp/**` only and no filesystem write; the PDF is written by the Rust `export_pdf` command, whose `validate_output_path` requires an absolute `.pdf` path that is not a directory and whose parent exists — any such path is accepted, not only the save-panel choice. WebKit ignores `break-inside: avoid` in print, so tables can split mid-row on macOS and Linux; Windows (Chromium) honours it. WebKit has no `@page` margin boxes, so no CSS running header/footer exists on any platform — page numbers are stamped afterwards instead. The temp HTML file the window reads is not deleted after reading; it lingers until the one-hour stale sweep on the next `write_temp_html`. Issue refs: #1087 (fit to page), #837 (save-panel filters), #1377 (menu bar on the dialog).

### Per-platform PDF renderer backends
- id: pdf-renderer-backends
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Not user-facing: one platform-neutral shell (temp document, main-thread dispatch, oneshot sink, bounded wait, staging publish, teardown) over three native backends that paginate the HTML.
- capabilities: macOS — hidden `NSWindow` + `WKWebView`, `printOperationWithPrintInfo` with `NSPrintSaveJob`, geometry from `@page` CSS (`createPDF` deliberately not used — it produces one unpaginated page); Windows — hidden Tauri `WebviewWindow` + `ICoreWebView2_7::PrintToPdf`, page box from `PageSpec`, margins from CSS; Linux — hidden Tauri `WebviewWindow` + `webkit_print_operation_print()` with printer `"Print to File"` and `output-uri`, page box and margins from `GtkPageSetup`; HTML always loaded as a `file://` URL, never `with_html` (2 MiB cap, ADR-PDF4); temp document created with `tempfile` (O_EXCL, 0600) and owned by the sink; output written to a sibling staging file and renamed into place only on a success delivered to a still-waiting caller; a timeout tears the webview down; the same three progress stages on every platform; 180 s ceiling (`PDF_OPERATION_TIMEOUT`); unique per-render window labels
- status: shipped-on
- gate: platform cfg — `target_os = "macos"` / `"windows"` / neither, in `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/mod.rs`
- surfaces: automatic (behind `export_pdf` and `print_document`)
- code: `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/shell.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/sink.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/sink_phase.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/staging.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/teardown.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/wait.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/progress.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/navigation.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/{macos,macos_ops,macos_save_job}.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/{windows,windows_nav}.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/{linux,linux_nav}.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_spec.rs`
- rust: `pdf_export::renderer::render_pdf`, `PageSpec::validate`
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Print / Export PDF" ("Available on macOS, Windows and Linux", progress stages, macOS page-size info box)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/mod.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/shell.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/sink_phase.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/staging.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/teardown.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/wait.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/progress.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/navigation.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/macos_save_job.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/windows.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_spec.test.rs`; a real webview is exercised only by the `pdf_smoke` bin
- notes: `render_pdf` re-validates the output path because an `NSPrintOperation` with a bad save URL silently spools to the default printer. `renderer/navigation.rs` is compiled on every platform but only Windows drives it (`#[cfg_attr(not(target_os = "windows"), allow(dead_code))]`).

### PDF page numbers
- id: pdf-page-numbers
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Stamps a page number into the bottom margin of the finished PDF, configured from the export dialog.

- gate: dialog option `pageNumberPosition`, default `"bottom-center"`; `"none"` disables
- surfaces: PDF export dialog → collapsible "Page numbers" section (format and skip rows hidden while position is off)
- code: `src/export/pdfOptions.ts`, `src/export/PdfSettingsSidebar.tsx`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_numbers.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_numbers_label.rs`
- rust: `pdf_export::page_numbers::stamp_page_numbers`, lopdf
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Page numbers"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_numbers.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/page_numbers_label.test.rs`, `src/export/__tests__/pageNumberSpec.test.ts`
- notes: The stamp uses base-14 Helvetica / WinAnsiEncoding, which cannot draw CJK or Cyrillic; a verbose label outside Latin-1 (or a template with no `{n}`) is replaced by the numeric `n / total` form (`render_label`). The guide states this fallback. Best-effort: failure yields the `page-numbers-failed` warning slug.

### Print (system print dialog)
- id: print-document
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: `Mod+P` renders the current document into a helper webview and opens the operating system's own print dialog, so the user can print or use the OS "save as PDF".
- capabilities: HTML taken from the focused pane's live WYSIWYG editor when it shows the active tab, otherwise a full ExportSurface render of the markdown (Source mode, or a split whose focused pane is not WYSIWYG); editor artifacts sanitized; local, relative and `asset://` images inlined as data URIs (the helper webview has no asset handler); `<details>` expanded; light theme forced; `@page { margin: 1.5cm }`; KaTeX + theme + content CSS inlined; macOS shows a sheet on the invoking window; a `completed` outcome toasts success, `cancelled`/`unknown` stay silent; failures produce a two-line toast with the real error
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu id `export-pdf` (File → Print…, accelerator `CmdOrCtrl+P`, built in both macOS and non-macOS branches of `file_menu.rs`); palette command `export.pdf` ("Print…", matching the menu label); shortcut id `print`, default `Mod-p`
- code: `src/export/useExportOperations.ts` (`exportToPdf`, `pickPrintHtmlSource`), `src/export/printDocument.ts`, `src/export/printOutcome.ts`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/file_menu.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/macos_print.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/windows_print.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/linux_print.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/outcome.rs`
- rust: `print_document` (`src-tauri/src/pdf_export/commands.rs`) → `renderer::print_document`, returns `PrintOutcome`
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Print / Export PDF"; `website/guide/shortcuts.md` ("Print | Mod + P"); `website/guide/features.md` §"PDF Export"
- tests: `src/export/__tests__/pickPrintHtmlSource.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/printDocument.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/printLiveEditor.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/printOutcome.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/printFailureToast.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportToPdfImages.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/macos_print.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/renderer/outcome.test.rs`
- notes: Outcome reporting: macOS and Linux report `completed` / `cancelled`; Windows always reports `unknown` because `ICoreWebView2_16::ShowPrintUI` returns nothing once the UI is up, so no success notice appears there. Windows keeps the helper window VISIBLE (the print UI draws inside it); macOS and Linux hide it, and Linux keeps it alive until a confirmed job's `finished`/`failed` signal (#1341, #1343). The wait is bounded only until the dialog is shown.

### Copy as HTML
- id: copy-as-html
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Copies the rendered HTML of the document (or, in Source mode, the selection) to the clipboard as plain text markup, for pasting into other apps.
- capabilities: renders markdown through ExportSurface, sanitizes editor artifacts and inlines local images as data URIs relative to the source file, then `writeText`; Source-mode variant copies the selection or the whole document; no stylesheet included; empty documents refused
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu id `copy-html` (File → Export → Copy as HTML, accelerator `CmdOrCtrl+Shift+C`); palette command `export.copyHtml`; shortcut id `copyAsHTML`, default `Mod-Shift-c` (category editing); CodeMirror Source-mode keybinding

- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Copy as HTML"; `website/guide/shortcuts.md`; `website/guide/features.md` §"Copy as HTML"
- tests: `src/export/__tests__/copyAsHtml.test.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/sourceShortcutsHelpers.test.ts`, `src/services/commands/exportCommands.test.ts`
- notes: Writes text only (`writeText`), not an HTML clipboard flavour, so rich-text targets receive the markup as literal text; the guide says so and mentions the data-URI images.

### Pandoc export (DOCX / EPUB / LaTeX / ODT / RTF / TXT)
- id: pandoc-export
- feature: Pandoc export
- summary: If Pandoc is installed, File → Export → Via Pandoc lists six formats that pipe the markdown through the Pandoc CLI to the chosen file; otherwise it shows one install-hint item.
- capabilities: formats `docx`, `epub`, `latex` (→ `.tex`), `odt`, `rtf`, `txt`; markdown piped over stdin with a concurrent stderr drain; `--standalone`; `--resource-path=<source dir>` so relative images resolve; extension allowlist (`ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS`); `..` rejected in the output path and source dir; source dir canonicalized and required to be a directory; resolution through the login-shell PATH, 5 s detection and 120 s export timeouts; hint item opens pandoc.org/installing; Settings row shows the detected version with a Detect button that rebuilds the native menu
- status: shipped-on
- gate: presence of `pandoc` on the login-shell PATH — `resolve_pandoc_path().is_some()` picks the menu branch at menu build time
- surfaces: menu ids `export-pandoc-docx|epub|latex|odt|rtf|txt` under submenu `other-formats-submenu` ("Via Pandoc"), or `export-pandoc-hint`; palette commands `export.pandoc-<fmt>` (registered lazily at menu mount) and `export.pandocHint`; Settings → Files & Images → Document Tools
- code: `src/export/pandocExport.ts`, `src/services/commands/exportCommands.ts` (`registerPandocFormatCommands`), `src/hooks/useCommandBootstrap.ts`, `src/pages/settings/DocumentToolsSettings.tsx`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/export_menu.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pandoc/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pandoc/run.rs`
- rust: `detect_pandoc`, `export_via_pandoc` (`src-tauri/src/pandoc/commands.rs`), `pandoc::run::run_pandoc`
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Export via Pandoc"
- tests: `src/export/pandocExport.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/pandoc/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pandoc/run.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/export_menu.test.rs`, `src/pages/settings/DocumentToolsSettings.test.tsx`, `src/services/commands/exportCommands.test.ts`
- notes: Pandoc is not bundled. `export_via_pandoc` still returns legacy `Result<(), String>`, and accepts any output path with an allowlisted extension and no `..` — the path is not tied to the save-panel choice. The frontend key `latex` maps to extension `tex`, and the Rust allowlist is keyed on extensions, so the two lists are kept in sync by hand. Presence is re-evaluated only when the menu is rebuilt (restart or Detect). Menu ids stay string literals at the `with_id` call sites because `scripts/extract-menu-ids.ts` scans for them.

### Resource resolution and image embedding
- id: export-resource-resolver
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Resolves every `<img>` in exported HTML — relative, absolute, `asset://`, data URI or remote — copying it into `assets/images/` (folder mode) or inlining it as a data URI (single mode), inside a containment boundary, and reports what it could not include.
- capabilities: `folder` and `single` modes; resolution base = the document's folder (`getDocumentBaseDir`), containment root = the open workspace when the document is inside it, else the document folder (`getExportContainmentRoot`, #1433); `lstat`-based symlink refusal and `isInsideBase` containment apply to relative, absolute and `asset://` paths; remote http(s) passed through; `asset://` inlined as a local file (#907, #1086); "Image not found" / "Copy failed" SVG placeholders; one count toast; KaTeX woff2 embedding for PDF (`katexFontEmbed.ts`)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic; user-visible as the `exportHtmlResourceWarning` / `exportHtmlAssetWarning` toasts and the missing-image warning on print/PDF
- code: `src/export/resourceResolver.ts`, `src/export/resourcePaths.ts`, `src/export/exportResourceWarnings.ts`, `src/export/katexFontEmbed.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` §"Which Images Get Embedded", §"Offline Viewing"
- tests: `src/export/resourceResolver.test.ts`, `src/export/resourcePaths.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/katexFontEmbed.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/uint8ArrayToBase64.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/exportWarningCount.test.ts`
- notes: KaTeX font embedding is woff2-only, bounding the math-font payload. HTML-export fonts are fetched from a CDN with a fallback and 3 retries; an offline machine gets none.

### PDF smoke harness (`pdf_smoke` bin)
- id: pdf-smoke
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Not user-facing: a feature-gated Rust binary that drives the shipped renderer on a real event loop on macOS/Windows/Linux and reads the produced PDFs back, because `cargo test` cannot host a native webview.
- capabilities: `geometry_matrix` (4 paper sizes × 2 orientations, asserts the page box); `pagination` (basic, A5, and a >2 MiB document with a sentinel past the `with_html` boundary); `page_numbers_case`; `bad_path` (missing destination refused up front); `sequential` (20 exports, no leaked window); `concurrent` (2 at once, distinct sentinels); `progress_case` (the `pdf-export-progress` stages in the shared order, through the real `export_pdf` with `ExportGate` managed); one `SMOKE …` line per case, non-zero exit on failure
- status: dev-only
- gate: cargo feature `pdf-smoke` (`[[bin]] required-features = ["pdf-smoke"]` in `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`)
- surfaces: CLI `cargo run --bin pdf_smoke --features pdf-smoke -- <out-dir>`; GitHub Actions `pdf-smoke.yml`
- code: `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/main.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/scenarios.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/verify.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/html_text.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/fixtures.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/missing_path.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/page_numbers_case.rs`, `src-tauri/src/bin/pdf_smoke/progress_case.rs`, `.github/workflows/pdf-smoke.yml`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`
- rust: `vmark_lib::pdf_export::renderer::render_pdf`, `export_pdf` under test
- docs: none (developer surface)
- tests: it is the test; its pure helpers run via `cargo test --features pdf-smoke --bin pdf_smoke` as a workflow step
- notes: The workflow runs on pull requests touching `src-tauri/src/pdf_export/**`, the bin or the workflow, plus `workflow_dispatch`; it is not a required check. A change to the frontend template (`src/export/pdfHtmlTemplate.ts`) does not trigger it. Linux runs under `xvfb-run`. `default-run = "vmark"` exists because a second `[[bin]]` makes bare `cargo run` ambiguous.

### Temp-HTML handoff
- id: temp-html

The only caller is the PDF window; print no longer uses it, although the module header still says "browser-based printing" and the sweep still matches a `print-` prefix nothing writes. Distinct from the renderer's own temp document (OS temp dir, 0600, sink-owned).

## Area 7 — Integrated terminal

Verified: `2675ad132`

The panel is lazy-imported in `src/App.tsx` behind a `FeatureErrorBoundary`; `src/components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.tsx` is the root, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalSessions.ts` owns the per-session xterm instances, `src/stores/terminalStore.ts` owns session state, and `src/services/terminal/` holds the non-React entry points (gate, focus, create/close, Open Terminal Here, Run in Terminal, cd-follow). The Rust side is `src-tauri/src/pty.rs` plus `src-tauri/src/pty/`, `src-tauri/src/shell_env.rs` and `src-tauri/src/shell_integration.rs`.

### Terminal panel
- id: terminal-panel
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: A dockable shell panel on any side of the editor, toggled with Ctrl+`, with drag-resize, double-click maximize, and an empty-state hint when no session can be created.
- capabilities: opt-in `terminal.transcriptPreview` automatically follows exact Claude/Codex SessionStart transcript bindings and renders assistant Markdown, selectable GFM tables and sandboxed Mermaid in a collapsible section beside the CLI (right of it in a top/bottom panel, below it in a side panel); collapsed by default and auto-opened once per new reply containing a table or Mermaid block; shown/hidden by a pressed-state chart toggle (`data-terminal-action="transcript"`) in the tab bar actions; hidden panels stop reads; toggle via shortcut, View menu, status-bar button or command palette; opening moves the caret into the shell and closing returns it to the editor only when nothing else owns focus; docking top/bottom/left/right/auto/auto-flipped with aspect-ratio auto-placement (ratio ≥ 1.5 → right, ≤ 0.85 → bottom, 1440 px width threshold with 50 px hysteresis in between); drag-to-resize handle on the editor-adjacent edge; double-click on the handle toggles maximize to the 80% cap and back without rewriting the stored ratio; panelRatio persisted on drag end; deferred xterm activation until first show; ResizeObserver auto-refit; `terminal-resizing` class suppresses transitions during drag; localized empty-state hint `terminal.noWorkspaceSession`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `terminal.position = "auto"`, `terminal.panelRatio = 0.4`; opening refused with a toast by `canOpenTerminal()` unless workspace mode is on or the active tab has a saved file
- surfaces: menu id `toggle-terminal` (View menu, accel `Ctrl+\``); shortcut id `toggleTerminal` (default `Ctrl-\``); status-bar terminal button; command palette `view.toggleTerminal`; resize handle
- code: `src/components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalPosition.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalResize.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalAutoFit.ts`, `src/services/terminal/terminalGate.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminal-panel.css`, `src/stores/uiStore.ts`, `src/App.tsx`
- rust: none (menu item only, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/view_menu.rs`)
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` (intro, §"Sessions"); `website/guide/settings.md` §"Terminal" (Panel Position / Panel Size); `website/guide/shortcuts.md` (Toggle Terminal rows)
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.test.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalResize.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalAutoFit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/__tests__/useTerminalPosition.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/terminalGate.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/terminalGate.focus.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/17-terminal-workspace-cwd.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/18-terminal-workspace-cd-sync.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/35-terminal-rail-scoping.mjs`
- notes: `TERMINAL_MIN_HEIGHT = 100` and `TERMINAL_MIN_WIDTH = 200` (`src/stores/uiStore.ts`) are absolute floors; `TERMINAL_MAX_RATIO = 0.8`. Hot-exit restore sets visibility through the store directly, so it does not steal focus at launch.

### Focus Terminal or Editor
- id: terminal-focus-toggle
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: A rebindable shortcut and palette command that moves the caret between the editor and an open terminal without changing the panel's visibility, opening the panel only when it is hidden.
- capabilities: toggles focus both ways (terminal → the visible editing surface, Source or WYSIWYG; editor → the active session's xterm, retried next frame if not yet mounted); hidden panel routes through `requestToggleTerminal` so the workspace refusal and toast still apply; consumed inside the terminal's key handler so the shell never sees the chord; one shared definition of "focus is in the terminal" (`TERMINAL_SURFACE_SELECTOR`) used by both the keybinding scope resolver and this toggle
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `suppressInInput: true`
- surfaces: shortcut id `focusTerminal` (default `Ctrl-Shift-\``, `Alt-Shift-\`` on Windows/Linux, no menu item); command palette `view.focusTerminal`; keyboard inside terminal focus
- code: `src/services/terminal/terminalFocus.ts`, `src/services/terminal/terminalGate.ts`, `src/utils/terminalSurface.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalKeyHandler.ts`, `src/services/commands/viewCommands.ts`, `src/stores/settingsStore/shortcutDefinitions.ts`, `src/services/keybinding/keybindingDefinitions.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` (intro paragraph, §"Keyboard Shortcuts"); `website/guide/shortcuts.md` (View table and §"Terminal")
- tests: `src/services/terminal/terminalFocus.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/terminalGate.focus.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalKeyHandler.focus.test.ts`, `src/utils/terminalSurface.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/browserTier.smoke.webkit.test.ts` (selector pinned against real xterm.js)
- notes: The stray `·` a Chinese IME used to commit on `Ctrl+\`` is stopped by `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.ts` at `beforeinput` (keybinding feature, Area 13), not by this handler.

### Terminal sessions and tab bar
- id: terminal-sessions
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Up to five concurrent shell sessions per visible scope, each with its own xterm buffer and PTY, driven by a vertical tab bar with create/close/restart/rename/swap controls.
- capabilities: create (+, disabled at cap); switch by click or `Mod+1..5`; close (trash) with visible-membership guard; restart that supersedes an in-flight spawn via `spawnGen`; every new PTY (first spawn, restart, press-any-key respawn) starts on a fully reset terminal and drops the dead program's tab title, so a killed TUI's mouse tracking, hidden cursor, alternate screen, palette and other modes never reach the next shell (#1471); a dead session stops pointer/focus reporting so only a real key restarts it; inline rename on double-click (Enter commits, Escape cancels, blur keeps, empty ignored); stable ordinal glyph, first-grapheme glyph after rename; program title from OSC 0/2 unless user-renamed; dead-session `×` glyph plus "press any key to restart" on non-zero exit; clean exit (code 0) auto-closes the tab and hides the panel when it was the last visible one; background-activity dot; swap flips the panel to the opposite end of its axis (auto ↔ auto-flipped); localized status lines written into the buffer
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `MAX_TERMINAL_SESSIONS = 5`
- surfaces: tab bar buttons carrying `data-terminal-action="new|close|restart|swap"` (E2E contract driven by `e2e/lib/terminal.mjs`); keyboard `Mod+1..5`
- code: `src/components/Terminal/TerminalTabBar.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/TerminalTabRename.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalSessions.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalSessionsInit.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalShellLifecycle.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalShellExit.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionReset.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalMessages.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/sessionActions.ts`, `src/services/terminal/createTerminalSession.ts`, `src/services/terminal/closeTerminalSession.ts`
- rust: none

### PTY backend (spawn, stream, resize, kill, flow control)
- id: pty-backend
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Rust-side pseudo-terminal management with a dedicated reader thread per session, binary output over a Tauri Channel, real pause/resume flow control, and cleanup on window close and quit.
- capabilities: two-phase startup (`pty_spawn` then `pty_start`) so no output or exit signal is lost; 64 KB read buffer; output as `InvokeResponseBody::Raw` → ArrayBuffer, point-to-point; `pty:exit:{pid}` event; Condvar-based pause/resume (zero CPU while paused); frontend watermark flow control (`CALLBACK_BYTE_LIMIT` 100 000, `HIGH_WATERMARK` 5, `LOW_WATERMARK` 2); ordered input — one `pty_write` in flight per session, later input batched behind it; write/resize/kill/close; EINTR retry; `catch_unwind` around the read loop with reaping outside it; each session records the window that spawned it and that window's `Destroyed` event kills and reaps whatever it left (unstarted sessions reaped directly, started ones by their reader); quit-path `kill_all` plus a `Drop` fallback on `PtyState`; absolute-path shell validation in Rust; commands return `CommandError`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/pty.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pty/reader.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pty/session.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pty/window_sessions.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, `src-tauri/src/quit.rs`, `src/lib/pty.ts`, `src/lib/ptyInputQueue.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/spawnPty.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/fitAndResizePty.ts`
- rust: `pty_spawn`, `pty::reader::pty_start`, `pty_write`, `pty_resize`, `pty_kill`, `pty_close`, `pty_pause`, `pty_resume` (registered in `src-tauri/src/command_registry.rs`); `pty::close_window_sessions`, `pty::kill_all`
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Not yet implemented" (pause/resume is internal flow control only), §"Sessions" (closing a session: SIGHUP to the process group, then SIGKILL)
- tests: `src/lib/pty.test.ts`, `src/lib/pty.input.test.ts`, `src/lib/ptyInputQueue.test.ts`, `src/lib/__tests__/pty.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/spawnPty.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/fitAndResizePty.test.ts`; Rust `src-tauri/src/pty/session.test.rs` (7 fns, real `/bin/sleep` children under a PTY for kill/reap), `src-tauri/src/pty/window_sessions.test.rs` (8 fns). Gap: nothing exercises the reader thread / Channel streaming path (`pty_start`) against a real PTY.
- notes: `pty_pause`/`pty_resume` are wired only to internal backpressure — no user-facing control, as terminal.md says. ConPTY vs unix pty is delegated entirely to `portable_pty::native_pty_system()`; there is no `#[cfg(target_os)]` in `src-tauri/src/pty.rs` or `src-tauri/src/pty/`.

### Shell resolution and spawn environment
- id: terminal-shell-env
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Picks which shell to run, where it starts, and what environment it inherits, so CLI tools behave as in a system terminal.
- capabilities: shell priority = configured absolute path > `get_default_shell()` (Unix: `getpwuid_r` → `$SHELL` → `/bin/sh`; Windows: `%COMSPEC%` → absolute `cmd.exe`); relative paths rejected on both sides; fallback re-spawn with the system default when the configured shell fails, with integration recomputed; cached login-shell PATH probe; available-shell list from `/etc/shells` (Unix) or `where.exe` (Windows), executability-validated; env `TERM=xterm-256color`, `TERM_PROGRAM=WezTerm`, `COLORTERM=truecolor`, `PATH`, `VMARK_WORKSPACE`, `LC_CTYPE=UTF-8` (macOS only); CWD priority = requested cwd > same-scope sibling OSC-7 cwd > owner instance root > active-scope root > active file's parent > shell default
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `terminal.shell = ""` (system default)
- surfaces: settings pane (Shell dropdown); automatic otherwise
- code: `src/components/Terminal/terminalSpawnEnv.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/spawnPty.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveTerminalSpawnContext.ts`, `src-tauri/src/shell_env.rs`, `src-tauri/src/pty/session.rs`
- rust: `get_default_shell`, `get_login_shell_path`, `list_available_shells` (module `shell_env`)
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Shell Environment" (incl. which shells may be spawned and the fallback for one that is not); `website/guide/settings.md` §"Terminal" (Shell row); `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Terminal Integration"
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/terminalSpawnEnv.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/spawnPty.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveTerminalSpawnContext.test.ts`; Rust `src-tauri/src/shell_env.test.rs` (18 fns)
- notes: `EDITOR` is deliberately unset — a blocking `vmark --wait` does not exist. `TERM_PROGRAM=WezTerm` is deliberate impersonation (ADR-006). `LC_CTYPE` is macOS-only because bare `UTF-8` is not a glibc locale.

### Shell integration (OSC 133 / OSC 7)
- id: terminal-shell-integration
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Injects command-boundary and cwd markers into zsh and bash so VMark knows where commands start and end and where the shell currently is.
- capabilities: per-shell rc materialized under `<appLocalData>/shell-integration/<shell>/` and written atomically; zsh hooked via `ZDOTDIR` with the user's real `ZDOTDIR` resolved from a login shell and `.zshenv`/`.zshrc` sourced first; bash hooked via `bash --rcfile`, composing with an existing `PROMPT_COMMAND` and `DEBUG` trap; OSC 133 A/C/D → command list, `isShellBusy()`, idle callback; OSC 7 cwd → live cwd; prompt navigation `Mod+↑/↓`; exit-status gutter decorations; relative file-link resolution against the live cwd; deferred `cd` flush when a busy shell returns to idle
- status: shipped-on, windows-linux-gap
- gate: `terminal.shellIntegration = true`; settings row hidden on Windows; Rust `shell_kind` returns `None` for anything but `zsh`/`bash` basenames
- surfaces: settings pane toggle; automatic at spawn; `Mod+↑/↓`; context-menu "Copy Command Output"
- code: `src-tauri/src/shell_integration.rs`, `src-tauri/resources/shell-integration/vmark.zsh`, `src-tauri/resources/shell-integration/vmark.bash`, `src/components/Terminal/setupOsc.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSpawnEnv.ts`
- rust: `prepare_shell_integration` (module `shell_integration`)
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Shell integration"; `website/guide/settings.md` §"Terminal" (prose paragraph); `website/guide/shortcuts.md` §"Terminal"
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/setupOsc.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/commandOutputRange.test.ts`; Rust `src-tauri/src/shell_integration.test.rs` (27 fns, including behavioural tests that run real bash); `scripts/shell-integration-smoke.test.mjs` runs both rc files in real zsh and bash under a pty (`scripts/lib/ptyRun.py`) on the gates tier
- notes: The gap is Windows only — Linux zsh/bash are supported; `bash.exe` is deliberately not matched. fish is unsupported and documented as such. Login-only files (`.zprofile`, `.bash_profile`, `.profile`) are out of scope. Changes apply only to newly spawned sessions.

### Clipboard integration (OSC 52, copy-on-select, copy/paste keys)
- id: terminal-clipboard
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Remote programs can write the host clipboard via OSC 52, selections can auto-copy, and Mod+C/V route through the Tauri clipboard plugin.
- capabilities: OSC 52 write-only — `readText` always returns `""` and logs, even with the setting on; writes go through `@tauri-apps/plugin-clipboard-manager` and are bounded to 2 s, since xterm parses nothing while one is pending (#1471); addon failure degrades to "no OSC 52" rather than failing terminal creation; copy-on-select with 150 ms debounce, re-checked at flush, trailing whitespace trimmed, suppressed during IME composition, setting read at event time; `Mod+C` copies with a selection and passes through for SIGINT without one (on Windows/Linux `Ctrl+C` copies only with a selection); `Mod+V` pastes via `term.paste()` with preventDefault to avoid double paste
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `terminal.osc52Clipboard = true` (read at creation → new sessions only); `terminal.copyOnSelect = false`
- surfaces: settings pane toggles; keyboard; context menu; automatic (OSC 52)
- code: `src/components/Terminal/setupOsc52.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/setupCopyOnSelect.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalKeyHandler.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalClipboard.ts`
- rust: none (tauri-plugin-clipboard-manager)
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Remote clipboard (OSC 52)"; `website/guide/settings.md` §"Terminal" (Remote Clipboard row)
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/setupOsc52.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/setupCopyOnSelect.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalKeyHandler.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalKeyHandler.linux.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/__tests__/terminalDocRanges.test.ts` (OSC 52 default and persist sanitiser)
- notes: Read denial matches the iTerm2/VS Code posture and is asserted directly.

### Terminal keyboard handling

### IME composition gate (Channel Ownership)
- id: terminal-ime-gate
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: A single-writer input path so CJK/IME text reaches the shell exactly once, with no duplicated or dropped characters.
- capabilities: capture-phase `input` listener that stops xterm's own handler; compositionstart/end snapshot + `resolveCommit` arbitration; synchronous textarea clear so xterm's finalizer emits nothing; write-derived insert ownership via `noteExternalWrite` with next-macrotask expiry; plain-insertText path for IMEs that skip a composition cycle; an orphan `compositionend` (no start — WebKitGTK + fcitx5) commits its data even when ASCII unless xterm's keydown already wrote it in the same task; no-op handle when the helper textarea cannot be resolved
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/components/Terminal/setupImeCompositionGate.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/imeGateMachine.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveCommit.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/imeCharClass.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionInputWiring.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveHelperTextarea.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Terminal Doesn't Accept CJK Punctuation"
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/setupImeCompositionGate.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/setupImeCompositionGate.webkit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/imeAsciiHandoff.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/imeCharClass.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveCommit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resolveHelperTextarea.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionInputWiring.test.ts`; real-IME lane `e2e/run-ime.mjs`
- notes: #1375 (open): the orphan-end ASCII commit is fixed; the flicker and misplaced candidate window reported in the same issue are not addressed (per `56acb870e`). Replay against a real xterm needs the webkit tier; jsdom cannot model the listener/microtask boundary.

### Bell, background activity, and cross-window attention
- id: terminal-bell-attention
- feature: Integrated terminal

### WebGL renderer and display reset
- id: terminal-webgl
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: GPU-accelerated rendering with dual-layer context-loss recovery and a "Reset Display" action that repaints every terminal sharing the window's glyph atlas.
- capabilities: `WebglAddon` when enabled; nothing clears the shared texture atlas unprompted; Reset Display clears the atlas and broadcasts to every other live WebGL renderer so siblings drop their model instead of drawing substituted glyphs (disposed and DOM-renderer terminals skipped, a throwing sibling tolerated); pnpm patch `patches/@xterm__addon-webgl@0.19.0.patch` backports a globally monotonic atlas page version so a merged page is re-uploaded; context loss detected via `addon.onContextLoss` and DOM `webglcontextlost` on every canvas, with a MutationObserver rebinding to replaced canvases; on loss the addon is disposed and xterm's DOM renderer takes over; `resetDisplay()` safe when WebGL is off or disposed
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `terminal.useWebGL = true` (read at creation — requires a terminal restart); never on Linux (`shouldUseWebglRenderer`), where WebKitGTK presented WebGL frames only on the next repaint (#1511), and the toggle is hidden there
- surfaces: settings pane toggle; context-menu "Reset Display"
- code: `src/components/Terminal/setupWebglRenderer.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalOptions.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/resourceStack.ts`, `patches/@xterm__addon-webgl@0.19.0.patch`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Terminal" (WebGL Renderer row); `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Context Menu" (Reset Display)
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/terminalOptions.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/TerminalSettings.test.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.webgl.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.rollback.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/webglAtlasPageUpload.webkit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/browserTier.smoke.webkit.test.ts`
- notes: The broadcast is redundant once a stable `@xterm/addon-webgl` carries upstream `0b1c0b5c`; the patch is keyed to 0.19.0 so an addon bump fails `pnpm install` until it is re-evaluated. WebGL constructor failure is `v8 ignore`d. Construction is transactional via `resourceStack.ts`.

### Per-workspace-instance terminal session sets
- id: terminal-per-instance-sessions
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: With the workspace rail on, each rail workspace owns its own set of terminal sessions; switching workspaces swaps the visible tabs while hidden shells keep running untouched.
- capabilities: owner stamped at creation via one resolver (never a placeholder instance, never mid hot-exit restore, never with the rail off); visibility = window-scoped ∪ scope-stamped; cap and ordinals over the visible creation union; per-scope `lastActiveByScope`; adoption of window-scoped sessions on switch and hydrate; ordinal renumbering on collision without renaming or killing; instance close and move-to-window remove that scope's sessions (store removal kills the PTY); loose-instance rekey merges scopes; realign after rail-mode toggle; rail-off makes stamps inert but never erases them
- status: shipped-off
- gate: `general.workspaceRailMode = false`
- surfaces: automatic (workspace rail switch); tab bar; empty-state hint
- code: `src/stores/terminalStore.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/scopeActions.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/scopeSelectors.ts`, `src/services/terminal/resolveTerminalOwnerInstanceId.ts`, `src/services/terminal/visibleTerminalSessions.ts`, `src/services/terminal/createTerminalSession.ts`, `src/services/terminal/maybeAutoCreateTerminalSession.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useVisibleTerminalSessions.ts`, `src/services/workspaces/finalizeInstanceRemoval.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Terminal sessions and the workspace rail"; `website/guide/workspace-rail.md` §"Terminal sessions"
- tests: `src/stores/terminalStore.test.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/scopeActions.test.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/scopeSelectors.test.ts`, `src/stores/terminalStore/sessionActions.scope.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.scope.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.railswitch.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.live.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalSessionsInit.scope.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalShellLifecycle.scope.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/resolveTerminalOwnerInstanceId.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/maybeAutoCreateTerminalSession.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/35-terminal-rail-scoping.mjs` with `e2e/lib/rail.mjs`
- notes: Accepted limitation: a scope can transiently exceed 5 sessions via the placeholder-adoption path — the cap is a creation-time gate, not a population invariant. No session persistence across restarts. Plan: `.claude/tdd-guardian/20260831-terminal-per-instance-sessions.md`.

### Workspace cd-follow
- id: terminal-cd-follow
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: When the workspace root changes, idle window-scoped shells `cd` into the new root automatically; busy shells defer until they return to a prompt.
- capabilities: `buildCdCommand` prefixes `Ctrl+U` and POSIX-quotes the path with newlines stripped; skips sessions whose live OSC-7 cwd already matches; defers into `pendingRoot` while `isShellBusy()`, flushed on the OSC 133 idle callback; leaving workspace mode drops pending roots; post-spawn catch-up when the root changed during an in-flight spawn (suppressed for a requested directory); one rail-aware predicate at all sites
- status: shipped-on, partial
- gate: always on; rail-scoped sessions are excluded when `general.workspaceRailMode` is on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/services/terminal/terminalCdFollow.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalShellLifecycle.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Shell Environment" (last paragraph); `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Terminal Integration"
- tests: `src/services/terminal/terminalCdFollow.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.root.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/17-terminal-workspace-cwd.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/18-terminal-workspace-cd-sync.mjs`
- notes: Without shell integration `isShellBusy()` is always false, so a `cd` can be typed into a foreground program (fish and every Windows shell). Both guide pages describe the idle-only, busy-defers and rail-scoped behaviour.

### Open Terminal Here
- id: open-terminal-here
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Right-click a folder in the file explorer to start a terminal session pinned to that directory.
- capabilities: creates a session carrying `requestedCwd` that outranks sibling-cwd inheritance and the workspace root; reveals the panel; consumed once and cleared only after a successful spawn so a failure stays retryable; typed result `{ok:true,sessionId}` / `{ok:false,reason:"no-directory"|"max-sessions"}`; menu item greyed with a "Maximum 5 sessions" label at the cap using the store's own creation-union predicate
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: file explorer context menu (`openTerminalHere`)
- code: `src/services/terminal/openTerminalHere.ts`, `src/services/terminal/revealTerminalSession.ts`, `src/components/Sidebar/FileExplorer/ContextMenu.tsx`, `src/components/Sidebar/FileExplorer/contextMenuActions.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Sessions" (Open Terminal Here paragraph)
- tests: `src/services/terminal/openTerminalHere.test.ts`, `src/services/terminal/revealTerminalSession.test.ts`, `src/components/Sidebar/FileExplorer/contextMenuActions.test.ts`, `src/components/Sidebar/FileExplorer/ContextMenu.test.tsx`
- notes: Directory names with leading/trailing spaces are preserved verbatim; `trim()` only decides whether a path was given.

### Run in Terminal (code-block button)
- id: run-in-terminal
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: A "Run in Terminal" button on shell code fences pastes the block into the terminal input line without ever executing it.
- capabilities: source fences `bash`/`sh`/`zsh`/`shell` and transcript fences `console`/`shell-session`/`shellsession`/`terminal` (prompt-only lines kept, prompts stripped); trailing newlines and whitespace-only tail lines stripped, `\r` removed; multi-line payloads refused unless `term.modes.bracketedPasteMode` is on, with a 60-frame retry budget for a still-starting shell; delivery pinned to a session id; reuses the active visible session or creates one and reveals the panel; focuses the terminal after paste; typed failure reasons
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: code-block hover toolbar button in the editor
- code: `src/services/terminal/runInTerminal.ts`, `src/services/terminal/activeTerminal.ts`, `src/services/terminal/revealTerminalSession.ts`, `src/plugins/codeBlockLineNumbers/nodeViewActions.ts`

- notes: The only path by which document content reaches a shell; "never auto-execute" is a security boundary with two independently enforced conditions (no newline appended, multi-line only under bracketed paste).

### Terminal accessibility
- id: terminal-a11y
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Screen-reader exposure, a contrast floor for faint output, and keyboard-navigable terminal chrome.
- capabilities: `screenReaderMode` exposes output to assistive technology (off by default, live-settable); `minimumContrastRatio` per-cell foreground lift with Off / AA 4.5 / AAA 7 / Max 21 presets, clamped to 1–21 and NaN-guarded, live-settable; panel `role="region"` with `terminal.ariaLabel`; tab `aria-label` reflects program title or exit state; `sr-only` background-activity announcement; context menu `role="menu"` with roving tabindex; visual bell as a non-audible alternative
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `terminal.screenReaderMode = false`, `terminal.minimumContrastRatio = 4.5`
- surfaces: settings pane (Accessibility group); automatic
- code: `src/components/Terminal/terminalOptions.ts`, `src/pages/settings/TerminalSettings.tsx`, `src/components/Terminal/TerminalContextMenu.tsx`, `src/hooks/useMenuRovingFocus.ts`
- rust: none

- notes: The contrast lift exists because light-theme ANSI palettes are tuned as foregrounds while CLI tools paint filled tags.

### Terminal state persistence (hot exit)
- id: terminal-persistence
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Panel visibility survives a hot-exit restart and panel size survives every restart (it is the persisted `terminal.panelRatio` setting); shell processes and scrollback deliberately do not.
- capabilities: hot exit captures `terminal_visible` and `terminal_height`; restore toggles visibility and clamps height to `TERMINAL_MAX_RATIO` of the viewport; the effective width or height is recomputed from `terminal.panelRatio` by `useTerminalPosition` on mount, position change and window resize, so a left/right panel keeps its size too; a restored-visible panel over a refusing scope shows the empty-state hint instead of spawning into `$HOME`
- status: partial
- gate: always on (hot exit)
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/hooks/resilience/_hotExitCapture.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restoreHelpers.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/types.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalPosition.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/useTerminalResize.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/terminal.md` §"Persistence"; `website/guide/features.md` (UI layout restore list)
- tests: `src/hooks/resilience/_hotExitCapture.test.ts`, `src/services/persistence/hotExit/restoreHelpers.test.ts`
- notes: Scrollback persistence is deliberately unbuilt (secrets at rest); `@xterm/addon-serialize` is installed but unwired, pinned as such by `src/components/Terminal/__tests__/serializeAddonDependency.test.ts`. The captured `terminal_height` is redundant with `panelRatio`, which recomputes the size; whether the restored height is ever visible before that recompute is unverified.

### Dev-only terminal surfaces
- id: terminal-dev-surfaces
- feature: Integrated terminal
- summary: Not user-facing: an IME input-trace recorder and a PTY transport benchmark used only during development.
- capabilities: `attachInputTrace` records keydown/input/composition sequences plus the helper textarea value into a ring buffer, exposed as `window.__vmarkInputTrace.snapshot()` / `.download()`; PTY transport benchmark comparing JSON-number-array encoding against the binary ArrayBuffer path
- status: dev-only
- gate: `isDev` build and `localStorage.setItem("vmark:trace-input","1")`; bench run manually via `pnpm bench`
- surfaces: DevTools console; CLI
- code: `src/components/Terminal/terminalInputTrace.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.ts`, `src/bench/terminal.bench.ts`, `src/bench/helpers.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none
- tests: `src/components/Terminal/terminalInputTrace.test.ts`
- notes: The trace is a recorder, not a replay harness — replay against a real xterm needs the webkit browser tier.

## Area 8 — Embedded browser and browser automation

Verified: `2675ad132`

The browser is macOS-only end to end: `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_stub.rs` refuses every native entry point elsewhere. The document window starts the browser's window-lifetime services (grant sync, lease wiring, tab events and lifecycle, recorder wiring, AI policy sync, conditional menu sync) from `src/services/runtimeWiring.ts`, which `src/hooks/useCommandBootstrap.ts` starts and stops. The MCP surface is two tools — `browser` (mutating) and `browser_read` (read-only) — whose sidecar code lives in `server/mcp/src/tools/browser*.ts` and whose webview handlers live in `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browser*.ts`; the spine assigns both of those to "MCP server & bridge", but the blocks describing them are here.

### Embedded browser tab (native WKWebView surface)
- id: embedded-browser-surface
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: A web page becomes a first-class tab inside a document window, rendered by a raw native `WKWebView` that VMark constructs itself (not a Tauri-created webview), so no IPC bridge is injected into the page.
- capabilities: create/destroy one native view per browser tab; rect reservation and `browser_set_bounds` alignment through a serialized latest-wins pusher, with NaN/negative rects refused; background tabs keep their native view alive so page state and typed input survive switching to a document; the tab-removal bus is what destroys a view; freeze/thaw; window-scoped teardown including secondary windows; one decoder and fan-out hub for native events; no-bridge assertion as a permanent regression check
- status: shipped-on, macos-only, windows-linux-gap
- gate: `browser.enabled = true` AND `target_os = "macos"`
- surfaces: editor-area browser tab; automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_stub.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_create_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_lifecycle_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/surface_view_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/teardown.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/no_bridge.rs`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserSurface.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/useBrowserNativeView.ts`, `src/components/Browser/browserBounds.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNativeViews.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserTabEvents.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserTabLifecycle.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNativeEvents.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNativeEventDecoder.ts`, `src/services/browser/serializedPusher.ts`
- rust: `browser_create`, `browser_destroy`, `browser_set_bounds`, `browser_freeze`, `browser_thaw`, `browser_assert_no_bridge`; modules `browser::surface`, `surface_create_macos`, `surface_lifecycle_macos`, `surface_view_macos`, `teardown`, `no_bridge`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"How it is built (and why it's private by design)", §"Using it"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/browser/surface.test.rs` (pins stub signatures against macOS), `src-tauri/src/browser/teardown.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/geometry.test.rs`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserSurface.test.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/useBrowserNativeView.test.ts`, `src/components/Browser/browserBounds.test.ts`, `src/components/Browser/browserLifecycleBound.test.tsx`, `src/services/browser/browserTabLifecycle.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNativeEvents.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/22-browser-tab-lifecycle.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/31-browser-no-bridge.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/37-browser-secondary-window-teardown.mjs`. Gap: 16 of the 25 `src-tauri/src/browser/*_macos.rs` modules carry no unit test, and every browser e2e journey declares `platforms: ["darwin"]` while the only CI journey runner (`.github/workflows/tier0-e2e.yml`) is `ubuntu-latest`, so no browser journey runs in CI
- notes: Windows (WebView2) and Linux (webkit2gtk) backends do not exist; the stub returns `NativeSurfaceError::UnsupportedPlatform`.

### Browser chrome: omnibox and navigation controls
- id: browser-omnibox-chrome
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: Back / forward / reload-or-stop plus an address bar that accepts either a URL or a search query.
- capabilities: omnibox classification (explicit `http(s)://` as-is with fragment kept; bare host, `localhost:port` or IPv4 literal → navigate, loopback defaulting to http and everything else to https; spaces or a dotless word → DuckDuckGo search); back/forward greyed when there is nowhere to go; reload/stop; address bar re-syncs on redirect and link navigation; bookmark star toggle (see `browser-bookmarks`); late navigation events older than the entry's generation refused whole; two placements — in the macOS overlay title bar vs an in-pane stack where the system draws the title bar (#1296), the second reachable only off macOS, where no browser tab can be created; no autocomplete dropdown
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: mounted only while the active tab is a browser tab
- surfaces: title-bar strip (macOS); browser chrome
- code: `src/components/Browser/BrowserOmnibox.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserChrome.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/AppTitleBar.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserWorkspaceSurface.tsx`, `src/lib/browser/omnibox.ts`, `src/lib/browser/url.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNavigation.ts`, `src/services/browser/navIntent.ts`, `src/services/browser/navigationOrder.ts`, `src/stores/browserUiStore.ts`
- rust: `browser_navigate`, `browser_back`, `browser_forward`, `browser_stop`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Using it"
- tests: `src/components/Browser/BrowserOmnibox.test.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserChrome.test.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/AppTitleBar.test.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserWorkspaceSurface.test.tsx`, `src/lib/browser/omnibox.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/url.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserNavigation.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/navigationOrder.test.ts`, `src/stores/browserUiStore.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/23-browser-chrome-controls.mjs`

The start page and search provider are hardcoded DuckDuckGo (`NEW_BROWSER_TAB_URL`, `SEARCH_URL_BASE`), as `browser.md` §"Using it" states; no `browser.homepage` setting exists.

### Browser sidebar: history and site permissions
- id: browser-sidebar-views

### Browser bookmarks

### Occlusion (native-view freeze while an overlay is up)
- id: browser-occluder
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: Any React overlay that can land in the browser rect freezes every mounted native browser view while it is up, because the WKWebView paints above all DOM and z-index cannot reach it.
- capabilities: reference-counted occluders with a serialized per-tab op loop and desired-vs-confirmed reconciliation (`OcclusionController`); freezes every mounted tab, not just the focused one (split-view correctness); 16 call sites (command palette, Quick Open, content search, genie picker, approval dialogs, context menus, knowledge base, file drop…); a declared per-overlay policy table that a test checks against `App.tsx`
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/hooks/useBrowserOccluder.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserOcclusion.ts`, `src/services/browser/occlusion.ts`
- rust: `browser_freeze`, `browser_thaw`
- docs: none (internal; the vacated-rect placeholder is described indirectly in `website/guide/browser.md`)
- tests: `src/hooks/useBrowserOccluder.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserOcclusion.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/occlusion.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/__tests__/overlayPolicies.test.ts` (with its table `src/services/browser/__tests__/overlayPolicies.ts`); e2e `e2e/journeys/24-browser-occlusion.mjs`
- notes: The policy table is test infrastructure only — the hook freezes unconditionally and nothing at runtime reads the table.
