- tests: `src/services/commands/browserCommands.test.ts`, `src/services/menu/conditionalMenuItemSync.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/menu/conditional_items.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized.test.rs`
- notes: The menu mechanism (`conditional_items.rs`, `conditionalMenuItemSync.ts`) is owned by the native-menu feature; this block cites it because it is how the browser entry point hides.

### Neutral chrome around a web page
- id: browser-neutral-chrome
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: While a browser tab is focused the surrounding window, and an adjacent terminal, drop the theme tint for a plain neutral — white in a light theme, dark in a dark one — and restore it on return to a document.
- capabilities: `--browser-*` token family shadowed onto the global names under `.browser-workspace-active`, so consumers need no change; neutral-for-its-theme rather than always light; terminal collapses to the `white` or `night` theme so the two halves meet without a seam
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on for a focused browser tab
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/shell/app-shell.css`, `src/shell/appShellClassName.ts`, `src/theme/terminalThemeForBrowser.ts`, `src/components/Browser/browser-chrome.css`, `src/components/Browser/browser-surface.css`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"The window goes neutral around a page"
- tests: `src/shell/appShellClassName.test.ts`, `src/theme/terminalThemeForBrowser.test.ts` (pins the exact background equality with the neutral tokens)
- notes: The shell and theme files are owned by other spine features; they are cited because they implement this browser behaviour.

### Settings: embedded browser and AI posture
- id: browser-settings
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The embedded-browser on/off switch and two AI-posture controls that appear only while it is on, in Settings → Advanced's macOS group.
- capabilities: `browser.enabled`; `browser.aiSession` = `sandbox` | `shared`; `browser.aiAllowLoopback`; turning the browser off closes every browser tab, revokes all standing grants, and withdraws the AI surface; the setting is pushed to Rust as `browser_ai_policy`, and Rust starts fail-closed until pushed; malformed persisted posture values normalize back to sandbox/false; the whole group is hidden off macOS
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `browser.enabled = true`, `browser.aiSession = "sandbox"`, `browser.aiAllowLoopback = false`; group rendered only when `isMacPlatform()`; `showDevSection = true` keeps Advanced visible
- surfaces: settings pane Advanced → macOS group
- code: `src/pages/settings/AdvancedSettings.tsx`, `src/stores/settingsStore/defaults.ts`, `src/stores/settingsStore/persistGuards.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserAiPolicySync.ts`
- rust: `browser_ai_policy`
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Embedded Browser", `website/guide/browser.md` §"Turning it off"
- tests: `src/stores/settingsStore.browser.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/AdvancedSettings.browser.test.tsx`, `src/pages/settings/AdvancedSettings.nonMac.test.tsx`, `src/services/browser/browserAiPolicySync.test.ts`
- notes: `.claude/rules/60-ai-governance.md` §12 records the 2026-08-15 decision to ship on, macOS-only, without a Windows/Linux surface; default-on opens the AI/MCP browser path at bootstrap, not merely the UI.

### SSRF / private-network destination policy
- id: browser-ssrf-policy
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: AI-initiated navigation refuses loopback, private-LAN, link-local, cloud-metadata, malformed and unsupported-scheme destinations, by URL text and by pre-flight DNS resolution, with a WebKit content rule list covering subresources on AI tabs.
- capabilities: URL-text validator distinct from the human omnibox (humans may reach localhost); IPv6 disguises of private IPv4 blocked; pre-flight DNS before an AI `open`/`navigate`, refusing `SSRF_BLOCKED` with `reason: resolves-private` or `reason: unresolved`, resolved outside the surface locks; `WKContentRuleList` on AI-owned tabs blocking private-range subresources in every frame, fail-closed on compile failure; subframe navigations on AI tabs run the destination policy; WebKit rule-regex dialect restrictions (no alternation, no `{n}`) enforced by test
- status: shipped-on, macos-only, partial
- gate: always on for AI tabs; `browser.aiAllowLoopback = false` (relaxes loopback only)
- surfaces: automatic (MCP `browser` open/navigate)
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy_dns.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy_addr.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_transactions_preflight.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_content_rules.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_content_rules_cidr.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_content_rules_cidr6.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/content_rules_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/navigation_policy.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/nav_delegate_macos.rs`
- rust: `browser_ai_navigate`, `browser_ai_create`; modules `browser::ai_policy`, `ai_policy_dns`, `ai_transactions_preflight`, `ai_content_rules`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Reviewing and revoking permissions" (the destination-policy paragraph, which states both remaining limits), `website/guide/settings.md` §"Embedded Browser"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy_dns.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_content_rules.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_content_rules_cidr6.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/content_rules_native.test.rs` (compiles through the real `WKContentRuleListStore`); e2e `e2e/journeys/29-browser-ssrf-policy.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/32-browser-redirect-ssrf.mjs` (darwin-only)
- notes: partial — the pre-flight covers navigations the AI issues, not redirect targets, in-page link clicks or embedded subresources (those remain URL-text checks plus content rules for literal private addresses), and resolved addresses are not pinned, so a DNS answer that changes after the pre-flight (rebinding) is not re-checked. `browser.md` states both limits.

### Data-store isolation and named profiles
- id: browser-data-store-isolation
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: One pure policy decides which `WKWebsiteDataStore` a tab gets per posture: human browsing gets its own identified persistent store on macOS 14+, AI sandbox a shared non-persistent store, and a named AI profile its own persistent context.
- capabilities: `store_policy(mode, profile, macos_major)` decision table, platform-independent and unit-tested; human browsing isolated from the app's own webview and from other apps; below macOS 14 human browsing falls back to the default store; a named AI profile below 14 still gets a distinct non-persistent store; human identity namespaced apart from AI profile names; per-use profile-open authorization bound to (profile, destination origin), single-use, consumed inside `browser_ai_create`; pending profile opens capped at 64 and named stores at 32
- status: shipped-on, macos-only, partial
- gate: `browser.aiSession = "sandbox"` (or `shared`); macOS 14+ for identified stores
- surfaces: automatic; MCP `browser` action `open` with `profile`
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/browser_store_policy.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/browser_store_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/profile_open.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/commands_auth.rs`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserOpenFlow.ts`
- rust: `browser_add_profile_open`, `browser_forget_profile`, `browser_ai_create`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"How it is built (and why it's private by design)", §"Driving the browser with AI" (*Open* bullet and the Sandbox/Shared posture list)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/browser/profile_open.test.rs`, in-file `store_policy` table in `src-tauri/src/browser/browser_store_policy.rs` (unverified: not opened), `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserOpenFlow.test.ts`
- notes: partial — isolation of human browsing is unmet on macOS ≤ 13, stated in `browser_store_policy.rs`. Moving human tabs to the identified store costs a one-time loss of existing logins, and a downgrade re-shows the old default store.

### Storage-state sessions in the OS keychain
- id: browser-session-keychain
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The AI can save and restore a login by an opaque handle — cookies plus `localStorage`, both scoped to the committed origin — stored in the OS keychain, with values that never cross to the AI.
- capabilities: origin-scoped capture and replay; cookies domain-scoped in both directions; Secure / Expires / SameSite preserved on replay; value-free `redacted_summary` counts are all the AI sees; per-call approval that cannot be spent on a different handle; keychain service `app.vmark.browser.storagestate`, distinct from API keys; forget clears the blob
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: operation `session` — never grantable, per-call approval only
- surfaces: MCP `browser` actions `session_save` / `session_load`; sidebar permissions view (forget)
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/session_state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/session_cookies_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/session_restore_script.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/session_restore.src.js`, `src-tauri/src/browser/session_commands.rs`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserSession.ts`
- rust: `browser_save_storage_state`, `browser_load_storage_state`, `browser_forget_storage_state`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Session save / load bullet and the scope tip), `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser` (`session_save` / `session_load`)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/browser/session_state.test.rs` (keyring in-memory `mock` store), `src-tauri/src/browser/session_commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/session_restore_script.test.rs`, `src/services/browser/sessionRestoreScript.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserSession.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/34-browser-session-roundtrip.mjs` (darwin-only). Gap: the real keychain path is exercised only by the darwin e2e journey
- notes: `browser.md` §"How it is built" names this as the one exception to "no password or cookie store of its own".

### Approval prompts, standing grants, and one-shots
- id: browser-approval-grants
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: Every AI action on a page is gated by a human approval that binds an exact descriptor — origin, operation, element role + accessible name, and for content-carrying operations the exact payload — with Allow once / Allow on this site / Deny.
- capabilities: closed operation vocabulary (`read, attach, click, type, scroll, key, style, navigate, upload, eval, session, record`); `upload` never automatable (sourced from `uxPolicy.aiMayChooseUploadFile()`); `eval`, `session`, `record` never grantable; origin-scoped standing grants with explicit strict-subdomain wildcards and punycode display; one-shots bound to tab + generation + origin + operation + target + payload hash; activation delay (`ACTIVATION_DELAY_MS`) plus pointerdown/click pairing on the same request, Deny never delayed; Escape denies exclusively and Deny holds focus so Enter denies; authorization lapses on full and same-document navigation; grants memory-only; bidi controls in a displayed name rendered as visible escapes; clipped display names; the dialog scrolls rather than outgrowing the viewport; Rust caps of 512 grants and 256 one-shots
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on for AI operations
- surfaces: modal approval dialog; sidebar permissions view (revoke)
- code: `src/components/Browser/BrowserApprovalDialog.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/approvalDialogFormat.ts`, `src/components/Browser/useApprovalDialogKeyboard.ts`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.ts`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.resolve.ts`, `src/lib/browser/approval/grants.ts`, `src/lib/browser/origin/originGuard.ts`, `src/services/browser/grantSync.ts`, `src/services/browser/oneShotMints.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserApprovalFlow.ts`
- rust: `browser_set_grants`, `browser_add_one_shot`, `browser_revoke_one_shot`, `browser_eval`; modules `authorize`, `authorize_decision`, `authorize_spend`, `one_shot`, `mint`, `operation`, `origin_guard`, `redact`, `refusals`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Approving an action", §"Reviewing and revoking permissions"; `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser`
- tests: `src/components/Browser/BrowserApprovalDialog.test.tsx`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.test.ts`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.resolve.test.ts`, `src/stores/__tests__/browserApprovalStore.runid.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/approval/grants.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/approval/operationVocabulary.test.ts` (parity with Rust `operation.rs`), `src/lib/browser/origin/originGuard.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/grantSync.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/browser/authorize.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/authorize_decision.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/authorize_spend.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/one_shot.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/mint.test.rs`; e2e `e2e/journeys/30-browser-approval-scoping.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/33-browser-approval-invalidation.mjs`. Gap: `src/services/browser/oneShotMints.ts` has no sibling test
- notes: `browser_eval` takes a caller-supplied `operation` label (`src-tauri/src/browser/commands_auth.rs`) and authorizes against it; accepted on the reasoning that every caller is in-process TypeScript inside the trust boundary.

### Human-tab attachment
- id: browser-human-tab-attach
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: When an AI targets a tab the human opened, VMark first asks whether to attach AI access to that tab, bound to the current navigation generation.
- capabilities: "Allow once" spent by the first read or action the driver authorizes; "Allow until navigation" expires on the next full or in-page navigation, close, disable, or restart; until attached the AI sees only the tab's origin; a failed attach keeps the prompt raised with an alert; buttons disabled while the IPC is in flight; the frontend mirror is spent exactly when the driver spends it and reconciled to the driver's report after a rejection; `wait_for` refuses a once-attachment up front (`ATTACHMENT_ONCE_INSUFFICIENT`)
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: operation `attach`
- surfaces: approval dialog
- code: `src/services/browser/humanTabAttach.ts`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.attach.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserAccess.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserAttachmentMirror.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserWaitFor.ts`
- rust: `browser_ai_attach`, `browser_ai_attachment_state`; modules `attachment_state`, `tab_attachments`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Reviewing and revoking permissions" (the human-tab paragraph)
- tests: `src/services/browser/humanTabAttach.test.ts`, `src/stores/browserApprovalStore.attach.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserAttachmentMirror.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserAccess.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/browser/attachment_state.test.rs`
- notes: none

### Automation lease and human takeover
- id: browser-automation-lease
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: A per-tab lease decides who drives the page — an AI workflow run or the human — and any human interaction with the page or its chrome reclaims it immediately and stops the run.
- capabilities: native `NSEvent` monitor observing (never consuming) mouse-down / key-down / scroll and emitting `browser://user-input {tabId}`; chrome interaction reclaims; tab close clears lease state; takeover `epoch` bumped only on authority transitions (reclaim, release), never on navigation; run envelopes validate `{holder, epoch}` → `lease-lost` / `stale`; every ending transition fires the registered canceller at most once; "AI is controlling — click to take over" indicator on the chrome and a dot on the page tab
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on
- surfaces: browser chrome indicator; page-tab dot; automatic (native input monitor)
- code: `src/services/browser/lease.ts`, `src/stores/browserLeaseStore.ts`, `src/services/browser/leaseTransitions.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserLeaseWiring.ts`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserChrome.tsx`, `src/components/Browser/BrowserPageTabs.tsx`, `src-tauri/src/browser/user_input_monitor_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/user_input_resolve_macos.rs`
- rust: module `browser::user_input_monitor_macos` (event emitter; no command)
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Co-driving: watch an AI drive the browser from the terminal"
- tests: `src/services/browser/lease.test.ts`, `src/stores/browserLeaseStore.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/leaseTransitions.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/browserLeaseWiring.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/36-browser-takeover.mjs` (darwin-only). Gap: the native monitor modules have no unit tests
- notes: The monitor's installed-once guard is a declared `static` exception (rule 50). While an overlay is up the native views are hidden, so an input event resolves to no tab and is a no-op.

### Policy epochs and navigation-generation freshness
- id: browser-policy-epoch
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: Two independent clocks make a late command stale: a monotonic policy epoch that invalidates every in-flight AI request when the posture changes, and a per-tab navigation generation that invalidates an authorization when the page, or the view within it, changes.
- capabilities: policy epoch bumped on every posture push and checked by `require_current_epoch` at create, navigate and driver-authorize time; navigation generation bumped on commit and on same-document (KVO URL) navigation, which also clears one-shots and attachments; every act and read stamped with the generation and refused as `STALE_COMMAND` on mismatch; every dispatched act response carries `data.url` and `data.generation`
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_guards.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_transactions.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/authorize_decision.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/registry_state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/registry_same_document.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/nav_kvo_macos.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/nav_ring.rs`, `src/stores/browserUiStore.ts`
- rust: `browser_ai_policy`, `browser_ai_state`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Approving an action" ("Permission also lapses when the page navigates" and the in-page navigation paragraph)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/browser/registry_same_document.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/registry.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/nav_ring.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_policy.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/ai_guards.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/browser/authorize_decision.test.rs`
- notes: none

### UX-surface policy and conformance ledger
- id: browser-ux-policy-ledger
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: Not user-facing: a decided disposition for each of 25 browser UX surfaces, paired with a shrink-only test-side ledger stating whether the shipped native behaviour conforms.
- capabilities: `UX_POLICY` forbids a "tbd" member; `UxSurface` / `UX_SURFACES` derived from the policy; `aiMayChooseUploadFile()` is the production source of the upload prohibition in `grants.ts`; `UX_LEDGER` marks each surface `conforms` or `pending` with a reason; the test fails on a surface present in one list and not the other, and the pending list is identity-ratcheted shrink-only
- status: shipped-on, partial
- gate: always on (compile/test-time contract)
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/lib/browser/uxPolicy.ts`, `src/lib/browser/__tests__/uxPolicyLedger.ts`
- rust: none (the native delegates in `nav_delegate_macos.rs` and `dialogs_macos.rs` implement the dispositions independently)
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Current limitations" is the user-facing mirror of the pending list
- tests: `src/lib/browser/uxPolicy.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/__tests__/uxPolicyLedger.test.ts`
- notes: 10 of 25 surfaces are `pending`: `prompt`, `window-open`, `download`, `basic-auth`, `permission-camera`, `permission-mic`, `permission-geolocation`, `permission-notifications`, `find`, `zoom`. Conformance is a handwritten assertion — nothing executes the native delegates against the ledger. `browser.md` §"Current limitations" now mirrors every pending surface.

### MCP `browser` tool (mutating half)
- id: mcp-browser-tool
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The mutating half of the AI browser surface — twelve actions that drive the page, the tab, a stored login, or a workflow run — annotated `readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: true`.
- capabilities: `act` (operations `click` / `type` / `scroll` / `key`, targeted by `{ref}` or ARIA `{role,name}`, with typed refusal reasons `ambiguous` / `obscured` / `hidden` / `offscreen` / `disabled` / `upload` / `rejected-value` and `data.url`, `data.generation`, `data.popup.url` on every dispatched response); `open` (AI-owned tab, at most 8 live — `TAB_LIMIT` — optional named `profile`); `navigate`; `close` (never gated); `style`; `execute_js` (isolated content world, per-call approval, 64 KiB script cap); `session_save`; `session_load`; `console_clear`; `workflow_run`; `workflow_cancel`; `workflow_record`; arguments bounded in UTF-8 bytes, not just UTF-16 units

### MCP `browser_read` tool (read-only half)
- id: mcp-browser-read-tool
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The non-mutating half — eight observation actions, annotated `readOnlyHint: true, destructiveHint: false`, so an MCP client may auto-approve them.
- capabilities: `read` (flat ARIA snapshot of `{role, name, ref}` plus level/checked/disabled/upload, walking open shadow roots, with `unreachable` counts for closed roots and frames and a `truncated` flag); `extract` (reader-mode Markdown); `query` (CSS-selector structured DOM detection); `console` (never drains); `screenshot` (JPEG); `wait` (existing navigation ticket); `wait_for` (`{ref}` | `{role,name}` | `{text}` | `{urlContains}`, `matched:true|false`, capped at 9 s below the bridge's 10 s deadline; `urlContains` refuses `?` or `#`); `workflow_status`; none changes the focused tab

### Reader-mode extract and the site-plugin registry
- id: browser-reader-sites
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: `browser_read extract` returns the page as reader-mode Markdown, dispatched through an origin-keyed site-plugin registry with a generic reader as the always-present fallback.
- capabilities: generic reader (main-content selection + Markdown serialization, escaping untrusted text nodes, code spans exempt); origin-dispatching registry with validation, frozen manifests, exact-vs-wildcard collision detection and a single-read manifest snapshot; atomic manifest+reader registration; built-in Wikipedia plugin for the article namespace only, stripping infoboxes, navboxes, hatnotes, TOC, edit links and reference markers; `read`-only capability vocabulary; lazy import off the eager bundle; the page only exports HTML, extraction runs in the webview
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on when extract is called
- surfaces: MCP `browser_read` action `extract`; workflow `extract` steps
- code: `src/lib/sites/registry.ts`, `src/lib/sites/builtins.ts`, `src/lib/sites/types.ts`, `src/lib/sites/wikipedia/wikipedia.ts`, `src/lib/browser/reader/reader.ts`, `src/lib/browser/reader/siteReader.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/extractScript.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserExtract.ts`
- rust: `browser_eval` (captures the HTML)
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Extract bullet), `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser_read`
- tests: `src/lib/sites/registry.test.ts`, `src/lib/sites/builtins.test.ts`, `src/lib/sites/types.test.ts`, `src/lib/sites/wikipedia/wikipedia.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/reader/reader.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/reader/siteReader.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/extractScript.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserExtract.test.ts`
- notes: Wikipedia is the only built-in plugin; there is no user-facing plugin installer or list. A workflow `extract` step keeps only `{title, textLength, truncated}` as step data, not the Markdown.

### Gate detection (login walls, consent, CAPTCHA, rate limits)
- id: browser-gate-detection
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: After an AI navigation the result can carry `gate: {kind, hint}` telling the model it is looking at a login wall, consent interstitial, human-verification challenge, or rate-limit notice rather than the content it asked for.
- capabilities: precision-first classifier requiring a size-checked rendered challenge widget or at least two independent signals; text-only verdicts additionally require a terse page; leaf-pure and jsdom-free testable; advisory only — it changes what the model is told, never what is authorized; hints point at involving the user
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: always on for AI open/navigate results
- surfaces: MCP `browser` `open` / `navigate` result field
- code: `src/lib/browser/gates.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/gateScript.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserGateProbe.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Navigate bullet)
- tests: `src/lib/browser/gates.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/gateScript.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/gateScript.webkit.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserGateProbe.test.ts`
- notes: none

### Console capture (page `console.*`, uncaught errors, rejections)
- id: browser-console-capture
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The AI can read a page's captured `console.*` output plus uncaught errors and unhandled rejections, without any messaging channel back into the app.
- capabilities: page-world `WKUserScript` overriding `console.log/info/warn/error/debug` into a capped ring buffer on a hidden DOM element; uncaught errors and unhandled rejections recorded as `error` entries; the isolated-world driver reads the element, so no `WKScriptMessageHandler` is registered; drain via `console_clear`; every AI-owned posture (sandbox and shared), never a human's page
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: AI-owned tabs only
- surfaces: MCP `browser_read` action `console`; MCP `browser` action `console_clear`
- code: `src-tauri/src/browser/console_shim_macos.rs`, `src/lib/browser/agent/consoleShim.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/consoleShim.src.js`, `src/lib/browser/agent/shimDrain.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserConsole.ts`
- rust: `browser_eval`; module `console_shim_macos`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Console bullet), `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser_read`
- tests: `src/lib/browser/agent/consoleShim.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/shimDrain.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserConsole.test.ts`
- notes: The shim is injected into every frame but read from the main frame only (unverified whether subframe output is reachable at all). The read-class drain is noted under `mcp-browser-tool`.

### Web workflow run engine (async runs)
- id: browser-workflow-run
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: The AI can run a short line-oriented workflow (`click` / `type` / `navigate to` / `extract`, plus `goal` / `confirm` / `api` / free-prose steps) on an AI-owned tab as an asynchronous run that returns a run id and is polled for status.
- capabilities: `workflow_run` returning `{runId, steps, firstStep}`; `workflow_status` with `status`, `completedSteps`, `stepCount`, `pausedAt`, `reasonCode`, `reason`, `pendingApproval` and one `stepResults` entry per step; `workflow_cancel`, never approval-gated; every deterministic step individually approval-gated like a hand-issued act; non-deterministic steps pause for the model; `resumeRunId` inherits completed steps and treats the paused step as done; a session ledger keyed on canonical IR + inputs skips write steps that already succeeded (`allowRepeat` overrides); a 120 s running-time budget paused while a prompt is open; one run per tab, aborted by human takeover; write-safety core where an `unknown` outcome never auto-retries; self-heal; role resolution from a fresh snapshot; CRLF/CR/LF and a leading BOM accepted; the 64 most recently finished runs are retained, running and paused runs never evicted
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `browser.enabled = true` + macOS
- surfaces: MCP `browser` `workflow_run` / `workflow_cancel`; MCP `browser_read` `workflow_status`
- code: `src/lib/browser/workflow/engine.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/runner.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/parser.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/stepGrammar.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/classify.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/safety.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/selfHeal.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/roleResolution.ts`, `src/services/workflow/workflowRunService.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runExecutor.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runApproval.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runClock.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runNavigate.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runRegistry.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runStepGuard.ts`, `src/services/workflow/workflowRunValidate.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserWorkflow.ts`
- rust: none dedicated (steps execute through `browser_eval` and the AI navigation commands)
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Run a workflow bullet), `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser`
- tests: 13 suites under `src/lib/browser/workflow/` (e.g. `src/lib/browser/workflow/engine.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/parser.test.ts`), `src/services/workflow/workflowRunService.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runExecutor.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runApproval.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runClock.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runNavigate.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runRegistry.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/runStepGuard.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/workflowRunValidate.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/browserWorkflow.test.ts`
- notes: Runs are in-memory only and do not survive a restart. The spine assigns `src/services/workflow/` to "Workflow engine (bespoke YAML)", but everything cited here from that directory is this browser run engine.

### Workflow recorder (capture the user's own actions)
- id: browser-workflow-recorder
- feature: Embedded browser
- summary: With a fresh per-call approval, VMark records the clicks and field edits the user performs on an AI-owned tab and hands back value-free, replayable workflow source.
- capabilities: `workflow_record {recordOp: "start"|"stop", site?}`; `record` is never a standing grant; value-free — nothing typed is captured, every text field becomes `{input}`, a password field becomes a `confirm:` step, URLs stripped to origin+path and credential-bearing paths (`/reset/<token>`, token-shaped segments, JWTs) to origin only; a field once seen as a password stays sensitive across a show-password type flip; the entry URL is recorded host-side as the first navigate; the shim never reads `.value` (pinned by a Rust parity test); host session drains every 500 ms and at stop, capped at 1000 events; navigation recorded host-side and the shim re-armed in each new document; one recording per tab, duplicate start and stop-without-start refused, tab close discards
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: operation `record` — never grantable
- surfaces: MCP `browser` action `workflow_record`
- code: `src/services/workflow/recorderSession.ts`, `src/services/browser/recorderWiring.ts`, `src/lib/browser/workflow/recorder.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.src.js`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShimSensitivity.src.js`, `src-tauri/src/browser/recorder_shim_macos.rs`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserRecord.ts`
- rust: `browser_eval`; module `recorder_shim_macos`
- docs: `website/guide/browser.md` §"Driving the browser with AI" (Record a workflow bullet), `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`browser` (`workflow_record`)
- tests: `src/lib/browser/workflow/recorder.test.ts`, `src/services/workflow/recorderSession.test.ts`, `src/services/browser/recorderWiring.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.residuals.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.walk.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShim.labelTarget.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorderShimRustParity.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/browserRecord.test.ts`, `src/lib/browser/agent/recorder.webkit.test.ts`
- notes: Stated residual in `recorderSession.ts`: events between the last drain and a navigation commit can be lost, bounded by the drain interval; the navigation record itself is never lost.

## Area 9 — AI genies and providers

Verified: `bf8b69ceb`

Genies are prompt files in `<appDataDir>/genies/`, listed by `src-tauri/src/genies/`, picked in `src/components/GeniePicker/`, and run by `src/hooks/useGenieInvocation.ts` through `src/services/genieInvocation/` into the Rust `run_ai_prompt` command (`src-tauri/src/ai_provider/`). The genie lifecycle (load, native menu sync, menu events) is mounted in the main window only, by `src/hooks/lifecycle/MainWindowRunners.tsx`.

### AI Genie Picker (Cmd+Y spotlight)
- id: ai-genie-picker
- feature: AI Genies
- summary: Spotlight-style overlay for browsing, searching and running AI genies, or typing a freeform AI prompt against the current selection, block or document.
- capabilities: one textarea that filters genies by name/description/category and becomes a freeform prompt when nothing matches; category-grouped list with a scope-filtered "Recently Used" section; quick-action chips (Polish, Condense, Grammar, Rephrase) at `selection` scope with empty input; `Tab` cycles scope selection → block → document → all; two-step Enter for freeform submission (Shift+Enter is a newline); Arrow/Home/End navigation with wrap-around and `aria-activedescendant`; IME composition guard; inline processing/preview/error response view with Accept/Reject/Retry/Cancel; footer provider switcher; click-outside and Escape dismissal; freezes mounted browser views while open
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: shortcut `aiPrompts` (`Mod-y`) → command `genies.togglePicker`; menu Edit → Genies → Search Genies… (`search-genies`) → `genies.openPicker`; universal toolbar genie button (opens at `selection` scope); command palette

### Genie definitions, storage and authoring
- id: genie-definitions
- feature: AI Genies
- summary: Genies are user-editable files in `<appDataDir>/genies/`; VMark installs 13 bundled markdown genies on first run, and any `.md`, `.yml` or `.yaml` file the user adds becomes a genie.
- capabilities: 13 bundled genies (editing, creative, structure, tools) published through a temp file and an atomic no-clobber rename, never overwriting user edits; a symlink or other squatter on a genie name is reported and does not block the rest; bounded worklist scan (depth ≤ 8, ≤ 10,000 entries) where the subdirectory is the category and the file stem is the display name; symlinks never followed; name ties broken by path for a stable order; frontmatter `description`, `scope`, `category`, `model`, `action`, `context`, `approval` plus Genie-Spec-v1 `version`/`input`/`output`/`tags`; `read_genie` confines reads to the canonical genies directory (`permission-denied` otherwise), reads through one handle with a 1 MB cap, rejects non-UTF-8 and non-`.md`/`.yml`/`.yaml` files, and runs off the IPC thread; per-window persisted recents and favorites; Reload Genies and Open Genies Folder
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu Edit → Genies submenu (`genies-submenu`) with per-genie items `genie-item-{n}`, `reload-genies`, `open-genies-folder`, disabled `no-genies`; command `genies.openFolder`; picker list
- code: `src-tauri/src/genies/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/install.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/scanning.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/parsing.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/types.rs`, `src-tauri/resources/genies/**`, `src/stores/aiStore/genies.ts`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.ts`, `src-tauri/src/menu/dynamic.rs`, `src/services/commands/miscCommands.ts`

- docs: `website/guide/ai-genies.md` §"Writing Custom Genies" (§"Where Genies Live", §"Directory Structure", §"File Format", §"Frontmatter Fields"), §"Troubleshooting"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/genies/tests.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/install.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/scanning.test.rs`, `src/stores/__tests__/geniesStore.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.lifecycle.test.tsx`

The genie menu and its events are registered by the main window only (`src/hooks/lifecycle/MainWindowRunners.tsx`).

### Genie invocation pipeline (extract → fill → stream → apply)
- id: genie-invocation
- feature: AI Genies
- summary: Running a genie extracts scoped editor text, fills the prompt template, streams the provider response into the picker, and either shows an inline suggestion or applies it directly; Cancel stops the provider.
- capabilities: scope extraction for `selection` (empty selection expands to the block), `block`, `document` (flagged `wholeDoc`); ±1/±2 surrounding-block `{{context}}`; `{{content}}`/`{{context}}` template fill; provider preflight (`providerValidation.ts`); single-invocation lock with elapsed timer, 3 s success flash and error state; `ai:response` streaming filtered by request id; real cancellation — `cancel_ai_prompt` fires the request's token (kills a CLI child, drops a REST request), and a cancel that arrives before registration is remembered; auto-apply vs suggestion preview; a result is kept as a suggestion instead of written when the tab switched, the editor no longer shows the originating tab, the document is read-only, or the captured range changed mid-stream; coherence capture on auto-apply; recents record a dispatch, not an intent; Source Mode re-checked after the provider await
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; direct apply gated by `advanced.mcpServer.autoApproveEdits = false`; WYSIWYG only
- surfaces: picker submit; menu `genie-item-{n}` → `menu:invoke-genie`; picker response view; status bar AI indicator
- code: `src/hooks/useGenieInvocation.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/streamRunner.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/streamRunnerContext.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/applyGenieResult.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/extraction.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/providerValidation.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/cancelRequest.ts`, `src/stores/aiStore/invocation.ts`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.ts`, `src/components/StatusBar/StatusBar.tsx`
- rust: `run_ai_prompt`, `cancel_ai_prompt` (`src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cancel.rs`)
- docs: `website/guide/ai-genies.md` §"Processing Feedback", §"Scope", §"Status Bar Indicator", §"Limitations"; `website/guide/settings.md` §"Integrations" (Auto-approve row)
- tests: `src/hooks/__tests__/useGenieInvocation.test.ts`, `src/hooks/__tests__/useGenieInvocation.cancel.test.ts`, `src/hooks/__tests__/useGenieInvocation.dispatchGating.test.ts`, `src/hooks/__tests__/useGenieInvocation.workflowRace.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.invokeGenie.test.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/streamRunner.test.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/extraction.test.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/providerValidation.test.ts`, `src/services/genieInvocation/cancelRequest.test.ts`, `src/stores/aiStore/invocation.test.ts`, `src/stores/__tests__/aiInvocationStore.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cancel.test.rs`; `applyGenieResult.ts` is covered only through `streamRunner.test.ts`
- notes: the direct picker path does not fence document text — `fillTemplate` substitutes `{{content}}` literally; `<<<DOCUMENT-DATA-…>>>` fencing is applied only to workflow genie steps (`src-tauri/src/workflow/untrusted.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workflow/genie_step.rs`), which `ai-genies.md` now states. The `menu:invoke-genie` listener runs in the main window and ignores the window label in the payload, so a menu genie click while another document window is focused is handled by the main window's editor — inferred from code (`src-tauri/src/menu/events/dispatch.rs`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.ts`), unverified at runtime. The status bar's **Retry** after an error only dismisses it (`handleRetryAi` in `src/components/StatusBar/StatusBar.tsx`); `ai-genies.md` says so.

Ghost text renders in `--primary-color` italic at `--opacity-subtle`, the original with an `--error-color` wavy strikethrough, as `ai-genies.md` describes. There is no MCP surface for suggestions.

### Workflow genies (YAML multi-step)
- id: workflow-genies
- feature: AI Genies
- summary: A `.yml`/`.yaml` file in the genies folder appears in the picker and the Edit → Genies menu; choosing it runs the multi-step workflow engine instead of a single prompt.
- capabilities: `GenieKind` (`markdown` vs `workflow`) from the extension, set by the scanner and re-derived from the path for menu clicks; `read_genie` parses top-level `description` (else `name`) for display and returns the raw YAML as the template, choosing the format by the canonical target's extension; scope fixed to `document`; dispatch to `run_workflow` through the transaction the run panel shares (`dispatchWorkflowRun`) with the shared provider payload, the workspace root, a pre-registered execution id with no owner tab, and the current capture policy (`currentCapturePolicy()`, so the Stamp-identity-block setting governs its writes), once the window's workflow-event subscription is live; refused when no event owner is mounted, while this window holds a run, and by Rust (`conflict`) while any run is active; `action/save-file` targets snapshotted before the run by the engine; typed `CommandError` rendering
- status: shipped-off
- gate: `advanced.workflowEngine = false`, whose toggle is visible only while `advanced.developerMode` (default `false`) is on; Rust `run_workflow` refuses with `feature-disabled` while off; also requires an open workspace
- surfaces: GeniePicker list item; Edit → Genies menu item; Settings → Advanced → Experimental Features → Workflow Engine
- code: `src/hooks/useGenieInvocation.ts`, `src/services/workflow/dispatchWorkflowRun.ts`, `src/services/workflow/providerPayload.ts`, `src/services/coherence/capturePolicy.ts`, `src-tauri/src/genies/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/genies/scanning.rs`, `src/hooks/useGenieShortcuts.ts`, `src-tauri/src/workflow/guards.rs`
- rust: `read_genie` (YAML branch); engine `run_workflow`, `workflow::genie_step`, `workflow::untrusted` — owned by Area 12
- docs: `website/guide/workflow-genies.md` (Requirements, Writing one); `website/guide/workflows.md`; `website/guide/ai-genies.md` §"Genies in Workflows"
- tests: `src/hooks/__tests__/useGenieInvocation.workflowRace.test.ts` (execution-id race, the `capturePolicy` argument, refusal with no event owner), `src/services/workflow/dispatchWorkflowRun.test.ts` (a run with no owner is registered unowned), `src-tauri/src/genies/commands.test.rs` (YAML branch), `src-tauri/src/genies/scanning.test.rs`, `src/pages/settings/AdvancedSettings.workflow.test.tsx`
- notes: the picker lists a workflow genie while the engine is off; running it fails with a toast (documented). The run is registered unowned (`runWorkflowGenie` passes no `owner`, so `setExecution` in `src/stores/workflowPreviewSlice.ts` leaves `preview.runTabId` null), and only the owning tab's panel paints statuses, offers Cancel (`src/components/Editor/WorkflowPanel/useWorkflowRunControls.ts`) or looks up the run's snapshot (`src/components/Editor/WorkflowPanel/useRunSnapshot.ts`), so no panel shows its step graph, cancels it, or offers Restore Files for its snapshot, and open workflow panels show "busy" meanwhile; `workflow-genies.md` says the run behaves "exactly as described" in `workflows.md`. No workflow genie ships in the genies folder — the sample `src-tauri/resources/workflows/examples/triage-and-translate.yml` is bundled as an app resource and must be copied in by hand (documented in `workflows.md`).

### CLI AI providers (claude / codex / gemini)
- id: ai-cli-providers
- feature: AI providers (CLI + REST)
- summary: VMark detects locally installed `claude`, `codex` and `gemini` CLIs and runs them as subprocesses on the user's existing CLI subscription, streaming stdout into the genie.
- capabilities: `which`/`where` detection with resolved absolute path, off the IPC thread and cached for the process lifetime; login-shell `$PATH` resolution for GUI launches (fish-aware, ZDOTDIR-aware, sentinel-delimited); Windows PowerShell `$PROFILE` PATH probe; `.cmd`/`.bat` shim wrapping and `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` on Windows; line-streamed stdout, concurrent stderr drain, `kill_on_drop`, cancellation that kills the child, 300 s hard timeout; `cli_path` validated at the command boundary (basename must be the provider's own binary, no `..`, no shell metacharacters)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; availability detected at runtime
- surfaces: Settings → Integrations → AI Providers (Detect button, CLI radio list); picker ProviderSwitcher
- code: `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/detection.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cli.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cli/stream.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/spawn.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cli_path_guard.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/dispatch.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai_provider/cancel.rs`

- notes: detection is cached for the session (`DETECTION_CACHE`), so Detect after installing a CLI mid-session still reports "Not found" until restart. `max_tokens` is not enforced for CLI providers (logged warning in `dispatch.rs`). Residual (ADR-016): a planted file literally named `claude` passes `validate_cli_path`. Login-shell PATH probing is macOS/Linux only.

### REST AI providers (Anthropic / OpenAI / OpenAI-compatible / Google AI / Ollama)

### API key storage (OS keychain)
- id: api-key-storage
- feature: AI providers (CLI + REST)
- summary: AI provider API keys live in the OS credential store (macOS Keychain, Windows Credential Manager, Linux Secret Service), never in localStorage or the persisted settings file.
- capabilities: `apikey.<providerType>` accounts under service `app.vmark.secrets`; tri-state read (present/absent/error) so a transient failure never blanks a live key; write-then-verify one-time migration of legacy plaintext keys with a no-clobber pre-check; empty value deletes; idempotent delete; `partialize` strips `apiKey` from the persisted blob; keychain write failure raises an error toast; macOS OSStatus classification (`errSecAuthFailed`/`errSecInteractionNotAllowed` → `permission-denied`, `errSecUserCanceled` → `cancelled`, others `internal` with `detail.osStatus`); non-secret provider config persists via `tauri-plugin-store`; keys validated at the Rust boundary (empty, over 256 characters, or any control character is `invalid-input`); commands are `async` and run the keychain call on `spawn_blocking`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: Settings → Integrations API key field (reveal/hide, copy, Test); automatic on rehydrate
- code: `src/services/secrets/apiKeySecrets.ts`, `src/services/secrets/secureStorage.ts`, `src-tauri/src/secure_store.rs`, `src/stores/aiStore/provider.ts`
- rust: `set_secret`, `get_secret`, `delete_secret` (all `Result<_, CommandError>`)
- docs: `website/guide/ai-providers.md` §"Where API keys live", §"Security Notes"; `website/guide/privacy.md` §"Where API keys are stored"
- tests: `src/services/secrets/apiKeySecrets.test.ts`, `src/services/secrets/secureStorage.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/secure_store.test.rs` (keyring `mock` store; the real OS keychain is not exercised)
- notes: ad-hoc-signed dev builds may re-prompt for keychain access after every re-sign (documented). The `secure_store.rs` header names a frontend `secureSecrets.ts` that does not exist; the consumer is `src/services/secrets/apiKeySecrets.ts`. The embedded browser keeps its own keychain entries (`src-tauri/src/browser/session_state.rs`), outside this service.

## Area 10 — MCP server, bridge, sidecar, e2e harness

Verified: `bf8b69ceb`

Three processes meet here. The Rust bridge (`src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/`, driven by `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/control.rs`) is a loopback WebSocket server inside VMark; the sidecar (`server/mcp/src/`, bin `vmark-mcp-server`) is spawned by each AI client and speaks MCP over stdio; the webview dispatcher (`src/services/mcpBridge/`) executes the requests Rust forwards. Coherence operations are answered in Rust and never reach the webview. The e2e harness under `e2e/` drives a debug build through a separate, debug-only automation bridge on `127.0.0.1:9323`.

### MCP bridge WebSocket server (Rust)
- id: mcp-bridge-server
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: A loopback WebSocket server inside VMark that AI-client MCP sidecars connect to; VMark never spawns a sidecar itself, it only runs the bridge.
- capabilities: start/stop on an OS-assigned `127.0.0.1` port (the command takes no port); status projected from one `BridgePhase` (`Stopped` / `Starting` / `Running(port)`), so `running` always comes with a port; start and stop serialized end to end by `BridgeLifecycle::serialize`; the port file is published only after bind and rolled back if the start unwinds; the accept loop refuses sockets once the bridge is marked stopped; connected-client count and identity list; events `mcp-server:started`, `mcp-server:stopped`, `mcp-bridge:clients-changed`; read-class operations run concurrently, everything else serializes through the write lock (`is_read_only_operation`, unknown types fail closed as writes); App Nap wake-and-retry (10 s request timeout, one wake + re-emit, 20 s total); 1000 pending requests max with a 60 s TTL; synchronous teardown on app exit
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `advanced.mcpServer.autoStart = true` (auto-start on launch); Settings toggle starts/stops on demand
- surfaces: Settings → Integrations toggles (Enable MCP Server, Start on launch); status bar satellite indicator; automatic on launch
- code: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/control.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/start.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/lifecycle.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/accept_loop.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/server.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/managed.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/wake_retry.rs`, `src/hooks/useMcpServer.ts`, `src/hooks/useMcpAutoStart.ts`
- rust: `mcp_bridge_start`, `mcp_bridge_stop`, `mcp_server_status`, `mcp_bridge_client_count`, `mcp_bridge_connected_clients`, `mcp_bridge_respond`, `mcp_bridge_heartbeat`, `mcp_bridge_set_window_workspace`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-setup.md` §"Quick Setup", §"How It Works", §"Checking MCP Status"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/control.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/start.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/lifecycle.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/accept_loop.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/server.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/state.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/state_lifecycle.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/managed.test.rs`, `src/hooks/useMcpServer.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useMcpAutoStart.test.ts`
- notes: There is no local sidecar and no port setting any more; `mcp_bridge/control.rs` states both. A destroyed window's workspace registration is now forgotten by the Rust `Destroyed` handler (`src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`), not only by the frontend.

### Bridge port file and shared auth token
- id: mcp-port-file-auth
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: The sidecar discovers the bridge with zero configuration by reading `<appDataDir>/mcp-port`, which holds `{port}:{token}`; that token is the only barrier between another process and full editor authority.
- capabilities: token minted by the single CSPRNG minter (`secret_token.rs`, 32 bytes hex); temp file set to 0600 before any byte is written and re-stat'ed before the rename; publication through `atomic_replace::persist_with_retry`; the bridge refuses to start (and deletes the token) if the mode cannot be verified; parent-directory guard in `token_dir.rs`; file removed on bridge stop; constant-time token compare; strict port parse (full-string digits, 1–65535) shared by the port file and `--port`; the sidecar re-reads the file on every connection attempt so a VMark restart is picked up; `VMARK_APP_IDENTIFIER` selects the dev profile (`app.vmark.dev`) instead of `app.vmark`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on while the bridge runs
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_file.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_dir.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_compare.rs`, `src-tauri/src/secret_token.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_paths.rs`, `server/mcp/src/utils/portFile.ts`
- rust: `mcp_bridge::token_file::{write_port_file, remove_port_file}`, `app_paths::get_port_file_path`, `secret_token::generate_secret_token`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-setup.md` §"How It Works" (step 2); `website/guide/privacy.md` (MCP server bullet)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_file.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_dir.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/token_compare.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/secret_token.test.rs`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/portFile.test.ts`, `e2e/portFileAgreement.test.mjs`
- notes: Windows has no mode to set — protection is the per-user app data directory's inherited ACLs. Accepted limitation: a same-UID process can read the token.

### Pre-auth connection policy and bridge principal
- id: mcp-client-principal
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: Who a bridge connection is comes from the per-client credential VMark wrote into that AI client's own MCP config, never from a name the client asserts.
- capabilities: `Origin` allowlist deliberately empty (absent Origin = non-browser = allowed; `null`/unreadable rejected); 10 s auth deadline; 32 concurrent connections; 16 MiB message / 4 MiB frame / 64 KiB pre-auth message caps; `VMARK_MCP_TOKEN` env credential read by the sidecar; principal is one of `Anonymous`, `Unrecognized`, `Ambiguous`, `Provider(name)`; the `identify` frame only sets the display name (bounded, control characters stripped, empty names refused, `clients-changed` emitted only on a real change); delegated actions (`coherence_resolve`) key off the authenticated principal and fail closed
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic; connected-client names in Settings → Integrations and the status-bar tooltip
- code: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/handshake.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/principal.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/connection.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/identify.rs`, `src-tauri/src/peer_text.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_config/client_tokens.rs`, `server/mcp/src/utils/clientIdentity.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/authHandshake.ts`
- rust: `mcp_bridge::handshake`, `mcp_bridge::principal`, `mcp_bridge::identify`, `mcp_config::client_tokens::{snapshot, refresh, mint_client_token}`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-setup.md` §"Install Configuration" (per-client credential), §"Security Notes"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/handshake.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/principal.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/connection.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/identify.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/peer_text.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_config/client_tokens.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_config/client_token_field.test.rs`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/clientIdentity.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/parentProcess.test.ts`
- notes: The client configs are the token store — no keychain entry, no separate secret file. The registry is refreshed before the accept loop starts; a panic in the refresh publishes an empty registry. Installs predating the mechanism connect as `Anonymous` and lose only delegated actions. No server-side rate limit; the token bucket is sidecar-side.

### Webview bridge dispatcher and route table
- id: mcp-bridge-dispatch
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: Every `vmark.*` request Rust emits to a webview is routed through two typed tables — eager editor routes and lazily imported browser routes — with an unknown-type diagnostic naming the supported prefixes.
- capabilities: `EAGER_ROUTES` — `vmark.session.get_state`, `vmark.workspace.{new,open,open_workspace,save,save_as,close,switch_tab,focus_window}`, `vmark.document.{read,write,transform}`, `vmark.workflow.{apply_patch,validate}`, `vmark.selection.{get,set}`; `BROWSER_ROUTES` (lazy `import("./browser")`) — 19 `vmark.browser.*` operations; own-property lookup so `constructor` cannot route; `SUPPORTED_TOOL_PREFIXES` as the single diagnostic source; `wrapHandler` renders thrown errors through `bridgeErrorEnvelope`; `respond()` returns via `invoke("mcp_bridge_respond")`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: MCP tool calls (automatic)
- code: `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/dispatch.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/handleRequest.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/wrapHandler.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/bridgeError.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/utils.ts`, `src/hooks/useMcpBridge.ts`
- rust: `mcp_bridge::routing::emit_to_window_or_reply`, `mcp_bridge::routed_request`, `mcp_bridge_respond`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-tools.md`

- notes: `vmark.coherence.*` never reaches this table (answered in Rust). The `handleRequest.ts` header still describes a "5-tool dispatcher" of five namespaces; `dispatchV2` also routes `vmark.browser.*`. Issue refs #900 (version-skew diagnostic), #1208 (focused-window resolution).

### Bridge operation contract (generated, four-surface parity)
- id: mcp-operation-contract
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: One Zod source of truth for every wire payload; the sidecar's `BridgeRequest` union and the webview's field descriptors are generated from it, and every outbound request is checked before it crosses the wire.
- capabilities: `checkOutboundRequest` in the sidecar's `server.ts` rejects or strips undeclared fields per class posture (`OPERATION_CLASS_POSTURE`: session strips-and-logs for version skew, the rest reject); handlers parse through `readOperationArgs`; a test-side manifest (`BRIDGE_OPERATIONS`, 40 operations) records owner (webview/rust) and effect (read/write) and is proven against the sidecar schemas, the webview dispatcher, Rust `is_read_only_operation` and Rust routing
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; regenerate with `pnpm gen:mcp-contracts`, enforced by `pnpm lint:mcp-contracts` and `pnpm lint:mcp-docs` in `check:static`
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `server/mcp/src/bridge/operationSchemas.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/operationSchemas.browser.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/operationSchemas.primitives.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/generated/bridgeRequests.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/generated/bridgeContracts.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/readOperationArgs.ts`, `server/mcp/scripts/gen-mcp-contracts.ts`, `scripts/check-mcp-docs.mjs`
- rust: `mcp_bridge::state::is_read_only_operation`, `mcp_bridge::routing`
- docs: none (contributor contract in `.claude/rules/50-codebase-conventions.md` §4)
- tests: `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/operationManifest.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/operationManifestParity.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/bridgeFieldParity.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/readOperationArgs.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/scriptLimitParity.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/waitBoundParity.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/contractsGenerator.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/operationPosture.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/operationSends.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/operationSchemaPrimitives.test.ts`, `scripts/check-mcp-docs.test.mjs`

### Bridge filesystem path guard
- id: mcp-bridge-path-guard
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: Every bridge file read or write is confined to the open workspace root plus the parent directory of each open document, with symlinks resolved in Rust.
- capabilities: pure JS policy first (absolute, no `..`, no NUL, component-wise containment); then `mcp_bridge_check_path` canonicalizes the target, or its deepest existing ancestor for a new file; rejects prefix-sibling roots (`/ws-evil` vs `/ws`) and symlink escapes; empty roots skipped, no usable root ⇒ reject; a bridge module importing `@tauri-apps/plugin-fs` without referencing `checkBridgePath` fails CI
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic (`INVALID_PATH` envelope on refusal)
- code: `src/services/mcpBridge/bridgePathGuard.ts`, `src/utils/mcpBridgePathPolicy.ts`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/path_guard.rs`
- rust: `mcp_bridge_check_path`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §"Errors" (`INVALID_PATH`); `website/guide/mcp-setup.md` §"Security Notes"; `website/guide/privacy.md`
- tests: `src/services/mcpBridge/bridgePathGuard.test.ts`, `src/utils/mcpBridgePathPolicy.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/__tests__/fsGuardInvariant.test.ts`, inline `#[test]`s in `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/path_guard.rs`
- notes: Known limitation (TOCTOU), documented in the Rust header — the check and the later `readTextFile`/`writeTextFile` are two calls; accepted for a local single-user editor. The `file_path`/`allowed_roots` ↔ `filePath`/`allowedRoots` binding is pinned only by the JS test. `open_workspace` deliberately bypasses this guard (it expands the boundary): it validates with `validate_workspace_dir` and asks Rust for access with `allow_workspace_access` instead.

### Delivery reliability: dedup, heartbeat, window routing
- id: mcp-delivery-reliability
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: A request executes at most once and is answered at least once even when macOS App Nap suspends the webview, and workspace-scoped requests land in the window that owns the workspace.
- capabilities: execute-once / respond-at-least-once dedup keyed by request id (256-entry insertion-ordered map; a duplicate of a completed request re-sends the cached response, a duplicate of an in-flight one is dropped); bridge-internal request ids so two sidecars' colliding message ids cannot cross-route; 5 s webview heartbeat; wake-webview eval plus a single retry; window→workspace registration with dedup, rollback and teardown safety, and a Rust-side forget when a window is destroyed; three-valued focused-window resolution
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/services/mcpBridge/requestDedup.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/windowWorkspaceSync.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/focusedWindow.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/revisionTracker.ts`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/delivery.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/wake_retry.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/window_routing.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`
- rust: `mcp_bridge_heartbeat`, `mcp_bridge_set_window_workspace`, `mcp_bridge::wake_retry`, `mcp_bridge::window_routing`
- docs: none
- tests: `src/services/mcpBridge/requestDedup.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/windowWorkspaceSync.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/focusedWindow.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/revisionTracker.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/delivery.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/window_routing.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/managed.test.rs`; `wake_retry.rs` has no dedicated test file (exercised through `server.test.rs`/`delivery.test.rs`)
- notes: #1208 — the focused flag used to be derived from the responding window's own label.

### MCP sidecar CLI (`vmark-mcp-server`)
- id: mcp-sidecar-cli
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: The stdio MCP server AI clients spawn; it auto-discovers VMark's bridge, registers nine tools and exits cleanly when its parent client dies.
- capabilities: `--version`/`-v`; `--health-check` — builds the server against a built-in mock bridge (never contacts VMark), verifies registered tool NAMES against `TOOL_REGISTRY` (duplicates, missing, unexpected), prints `{status, version, toolCount, resourceCount: 0, tools}` and sets a non-zero exit code on mismatch; `--port <n>` legacy override that fails fast (exit 64) on a missing, malformed or conflicting value; `process.exitCode` instead of `process.exit()` so piped output is not truncated; auto-reconnect (30 attempts, 2 s → 60 s backoff); shutdown on SIGINT/SIGTERM and stdin EOF, registered before any await; stderr-only quiet logger; `instructions` primer at initialize; `tools` capability only — no MCP resources
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on once an AI client spawns it
- surfaces: CLI flags; MCP stdio; Settings → Integrations "Test Connection" / "Check sidecar"
- code: `server/mcp/src/cli.ts`, `server/mcp/src/index.ts`, `server/mcp/src/server.ts`, `server/mcp/src/utils/healthCheck.ts`, `server/mcp/src/utils/shutdown.ts`, `server/mcp/scripts/build-sidecar.js`, `server/mcp/package.json`
- rust: `mcp_sidecar_health` (`src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/sidecar_health.rs`, runs `--health-check` on the bundled sidecar); bundled as `externalBin` `binaries/vmark-mcp-server`
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-setup.md` §"CLI flags (advanced)"
- tests: `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/cli.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/healthCheck.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/server.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/shutdown.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/integration/sdkBoundary.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/integration/sdkRoundTrip.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/buildSidecarCore.test.ts`
- notes: Package `@vmark/mcp-server` 0.9.84; `VERSION` in `cli.ts` is a hand-maintained literal moved by the five-file bump. Built for `aarch64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`. Tool counts in `index.ts` are derived from `TOOL_REGISTRY`, not hand-written.

### Tool: `document`

A saved write calls `captureMcpWrite` (`src/services/coherence/mcpCapture.ts`), which funnels through `captureWrite` with the capture policy from `general.coherenceCaptureOnSave`: off (`tracked-only`) never creates `.vmark/` or stamps a `vmark:` block; on (`adopt`) may do both.

### Tools: `coherence` / `coherence_resolve`
- id: mcp-tool-coherence-pair
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: A read-only view of a workspace's coherence ledger, and a separately annotated tool for the one AI write — resolving a stale edge under a delegation the user granted.

- status: shipped-on

- surfaces: MCP tools `coherence`, `coherence_resolve`
- code: `server/mcp/src/tools/coherence.ts`, `server/mcp/src/tools/coherenceResolve.ts`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/coherence_answers.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/routing.rs`

- notes: `edges` is write-class because it reconciles first and may append provenance records. `status` returns `initialized: false` for a workspace with no `.vmark/` ledger. Claim and context mutation is never exposed over MCP. Kernel behaviour is Area 11.

### Bounded tool output and structured errors
- id: mcp-tool-output-budget
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: Every tool result passes one choke point that caps its size in UTF-8 bytes and appends a per-tool recovery instruction, so an agent cannot mistake a truncated read for a short document.
- capabilities: byte-based bound (`MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES`, derived from a token budget); the steering notice is paid for inside the budget; `TRUNCATION_OUTPUT_SHAPE` on tools that can truncate; per-tool `RECOVERY` strings; `structuredErrorResult` caps the message (8 KiB) and the structured record (32 KiB) separately; STALE refusals ride as `isError` results that still carry structured content
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic on every MCP tool result
- code: `server/mcp/src/utils/toolOutput.ts`, `server/mcp/src/utils/errorOutput.ts`, `server/mcp/src/utils/mcpAdapters.ts`, `server/mcp/src/tools/staleError.ts`, `server/mcp/src/tools/toolArgs.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none
- tests: `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/toolOutput.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/utils/mcpAdapters.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/tools/toolContract.test.ts`
- notes: none

### Sidecar transport resilience
- id: mcp-sidecar-resilience
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: The sidecar survives VMark restarts, holds requests while disconnected, and refuses to flood the bridge.
- capabilities: port and auth resolvers re-read on every connection attempt; token-bucket rate limiter (default 100 req/s); bounded drop-oldest queue while disconnected (default 100) with per-entry deadlines; reconnect loop charging only real connection failures against the budget; auth handshake parked until `auth_result` with single-settle guarantees; default `requestTimeout` 25 000 ms (above the Rust 20 s total)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `server/mcp/src/bridge/websocket.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/websocketConfig.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/rateLimiter.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/requestQueue.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/reconnect.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/pendingRequests.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/wsProtocol.ts`, `server/mcp/src/bridge/connection.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none
- tests: `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/websocket.test.ts`, `server/mcp/__tests__/unit/bridge/requestQueue.test.ts`; `rateLimiter.ts` and `reconnect.ts` have no dedicated files and are exercised through `websocket.test.ts`
- notes: Issue refs #279, #524, #959.

- id: mcp-client-config-installer

### Approval: `open_workspace` one-shot grant
- id: mcp-open-workspace-approval
- feature: MCP server & bridge
- summary: Opening a folder as a workspace grants the AI a whole new file tree, so it always requires a fresh human click — no standing grants.
- capabilities: fail-now → approve → AI-retry; first call returns `{needsApproval: true, folderPath}`; approval mints a one-shot bound to the canonical path (Rust `validate_workspace_dir`), target window and a client id; before the one-shot is spent, Rust is asked for access (`allow_workspace_access`) — a folder outside the static fs scope that the user never chose opens Rust's folder picker at that folder (`request_workspace_confirmation`, which answers once the dialog is up) and the retry fails with `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` while the one-shot survives, so the retry after the pick opens; another folder dialog already open returns `BUSY` with no dialog shown and a dialog that cannot be shown returns `INTERNAL`, both keeping the one-shot; a vanished folder returns `INVALID_PATH`, an access check that cannot run fails closed with `INTERNAL`; TTL 5 minutes; 32 pending approvals max (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`), 8 one-shots max; semantic dedup so rapid retries cannot stack dialogs; non-consuming peek before the window-transition guard; concurrent menu Open Folder → `BUSY`; internal failure fails closed
- status: shipped-on, partial
- gate: always on (no setting disables it)
- surfaces: `WorkspaceApprovalDialog` (mounted in `src/App.tsx`); MCP `needsApproval` envelope
- code: `src/stores/workspaceApprovalStore.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/workspaceOpenFolder.ts`, `src/services/workspaces/workspaceAccess.ts`, `src/components/Workspace/WorkspaceApprovalDialog.tsx`, `server/mcp/src/tools/workspace.ts`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/validation.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/picker.rs`
- rust: `validate_workspace_dir`, `allow_workspace_access`, `request_workspace_confirmation` (`workspace::grants`)
- docs: `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` §`open_workspace` and §"Errors" (`APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `BUSY`); `website/guide/privacy.md` ("Runtime grants" bullet)
- tests: `src/stores/workspaceApprovalStore.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/workspaceOpenFolder.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaces/workspaceAccess.test.ts`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/picker.test.rs`, `src/components/Workspace/WorkspaceApprovalDialog.test.tsx`, `e2e/lib/workspace.test.mjs`
