## Area 14 — Lint, link check, CJK and IME, fonts, security boundaries

Verified: `bf8b69ceb`

Lint runs only on demand through the `lint.check` command (`src/services/lint/runActiveLint.ts`), which dispatches to the linter the file's format contributes and then, for saved files, the local link check; results live in `src/stores/documentStore/lint.ts`. CJK formatting has one entry point, `formatMarkdownChecked` in `src/lib/cjkFormatter/formatter.ts`. The security blocks describe refusals from the boundary's side; the feature-side blocks live in their owning areas. Which folders get a recursive runtime grant is decided in Rust (`src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/mod.rs`, block `workspace-grants-boundary`); `src-tauri/src/fs_scope.rs` only performs grants. Per-window capability scopes are the area 15 block `capability-sandbox`, and the macOS quarantine strip is the area 15 block `mac-quarantine-strip`. MCP bridge path confinement is the area 10 block `mcp-bridge-path-guard`; the dev-only automation handle is the area 13 block `dev-debug-handle`.

### Markdown lint engine
- id: markdown-lint-engine
- feature: Markdown lint engine
- summary: On-demand correctness linter for markdown that parses the document with the app's own remark pipeline and returns positioned diagnostics; correctness only, never style.
- capabilities: E01 undefined full/collapsed reference link or image (shortcut form ignored); E02 table body row cell count differs from the header; E03 reversed link `(text)[url]`, escape- and nesting-aware; E04 ATX heading missing space after `#`, including behind a `>` or list-item container; E05 space inside emphasis/strong delimiters, symmetric only, arithmetic-shaped `3 * 4 * 5` not flagged (warning severity); E06 empty link text (inline code and images count as content); E07 duplicate link-reference definition using the parser's case-folded identifier, first wins; E08 unclosed fenced code block (backtick and tilde, CommonMark close rules, CRLF-tolerant); W01 heading level skip; W02 image missing alt text; W03 unused link-reference definition; W04 `#fragment` matching no heading slug (duplicate-heading suffixes, inline HTML stripped, percent-decoded); W05 empty link `href`; one shared masking model (`sourceMask`) so fenced/indented code, front matter, raw HTML blocks and multi-line code spans are never scanned as prose; one rule-metadata table (`RULE_META`) that is the source of every emitted id and severity; per-pass shared line index; diagnostics sorted by line then column; each diagnostic carries a `uiHint` of `exact`, `block` or `sourceOnly`; linting is contributed per format (`FormatDefinition.lint`), so only markdown and YAML files produce diagnostics
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `markdown.lintEnabled = true`
- surfaces: menu `check-markdown` (View menu, `Alt+CmdOrCtrl+V`); shortcut `validateMarkdown` (`Alt-Mod-v`); command palette `lint.check`; settings pane Settings → Markdown → Lint (`markdown.lintEnabled` toggle)
- code: `src/lib/lintEngine/linter.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/ruleMeta.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/rules/allRules.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/rules/sourceMask.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/rules/referenceScanner.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/types.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/markdown.tsx`, `src/services/lint/runActiveLint.ts`, `src/services/commands/lintCommands.ts`, `src/stores/documentStore/lint.ts`, `src/pages/settings/MarkdownSettings.tsx`
- rust: none — menu items and accelerators only, in `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/view_menu.rs`
- docs: `website/guide/lint.md` §"Rule Reference", §"Triggering lint"; `website/guide/features.md` §"Markdown Lint"; `website/guide/settings.md` §"Lint"
- tests: one file per rule under `src/lib/lintEngine/rules/__tests__/` (13 rules plus `sourceMask`, `referenceScanner`, `positionOffset`), `src/lib/lintEngine/__tests__/linter.test.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/__tests__/parserIntegration.test.ts`, `src/lib/lintEngine/ruleMeta.test.ts`, `src/services/lint/runActiveLint.test.ts`, `src/services/commands/lintCommands.test.ts`, `src/stores/__tests__/lintStore.test.ts`; `scripts/lib/docJoins/lintTable.mjs` joins the lint.md rule table to `RULE_META` in both directions; no e2e journey exercises lint
- notes: No autofix or quick-fix exists — diagnostics are read-only. `lint.check` has no `when` predicate, so it runs on any active tab; a non-markdown, non-YAML format yields zero sync diagnostics, but the link check (id `markdown-link-check`) still runs over its text whenever the tab has a path. No rule inspects footnote references (lint.md says so).

### YAML lint bridge
- id: yaml-lint
- feature: Markdown lint engine
- summary: YAML parse errors and warnings surface in the same badge and F2 navigation as markdown lint, and a separate live linter marks them in the Source-mode gutter as you type.
- capabilities: Y001 YAML parse error; Y002 YAML parse warning; offset → line/column through a line-start index built once per call that breaks on LF, CRLF and bare CR (matching CodeMirror's line model); `uiHint: "sourceOnly"` so nothing is painted in WYSIWYG; replaces all prior diagnostics for the tab (YAML files never also run markdown rules); live CodeMirror parse-error linter (`sourceYamlLint.ts`) registered, regardless of the lint setting, for every `.yaml`/`.yml` file the markdown Source editor opens — an ordinary YAML file never reaches that editor; its split-pane source pane marks parse errors through the adapter's validator instead (`yaml/syntax`, shown as the bare id on the validation gutter's rule pill, which localizes `lint.rule.<id>` titles only for lint-engine ids)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `markdown.lintEnabled = true` for the badge/F2 path; the live gutter linter is always on for YAML files
- surfaces: menu `check-markdown` / `Alt-Mod-v` populates the badge and F2 / Shift+F2; Source-mode gutter (automatic)
- code: `src/lib/lintEngine/yaml.ts`, `src/lib/yamlValidation/parseErrors.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/sourceYamlLint.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/yaml.tsx`, `src/services/assembly/sourceEditorExtensions.ts`, `src/components/Editor/SplitPaneEditor/ValidationGutter.tsx`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/lint.md` (rule rows Y001/Y002 and the "For YAML files…" paragraph)
- tests: `src/lib/lintEngine/yaml.test.ts`, `src/lib/yamlValidation/parseErrors.test.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/sourceYamlLint.test.ts`
- notes: `useLintStore.runYamlLint` and `runLint` remain in `src/stores/documentStore/lint.ts` with no production caller — `runActiveLint.ts` uses only `runLintForFormat`; they are exercised solely by `src/stores/__tests__/lintStore.test.ts`, and the `yaml.ts` header still names `runYamlLint` as its coordinator.

### Lint result UI (gutter, decorations, badge, navigation)
- id: lint-ui
- feature: Markdown lint engine
- summary: Lint results appear as CodeMirror diagnostics in Source mode, block decorations in WYSIWYG, a status-bar count badge, and F2 / Shift+F2 navigation between findings, with a toast reporting the run's outcome.
- capabilities: CodeMirror `@codemirror/lint` diagnostics with translated messages; `forceLinting` refresh right after a run; Source mode clears the tab's stored diagnostics on every edit; WYSIWYG per-block `Decoration.node` with `.lint-block-error` (2px inset error bar, 3% tint) or `.lint-block-warning` (warning bar, 4% tint); `sourceOnly` diagnostics skipped in WYSIWYG; WYSIWYG decoration toggle is live (reads `hostSettings.lintEnabled` at build time, no remount); doc-epoch guard drops async link-check results that land after an edit, and a closed tab's epoch counters are forgotten on tab cleanup; status-bar badge `⊗ n` (any error) or `⚠ n` (warnings only), hidden at zero, click advances the selection; F2 / Shift+F2 wrap around, scroll CodeMirror to the diagnostic offset, and for a `sourceOnly` diagnostic in WYSIWYG switch to Source mode and hand the pending scroll offset to the Source editor on mount; success toast when clean, info toast with the combined sync + link-check count otherwise
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `markdown.lintEnabled = true`; the Source-mode diagnostic extension is included according to the setting at editor mount
- surfaces: Source-mode gutter and underline; WYSIWYG block bars; status bar badge; menu `lint-next` (`F2`) / `lint-prev` (`Shift+F2`); shortcuts `lintNext` / `lintPrev`; palette `lint.next` / `lint.prev`; toast
- code: `src/plugins/lint/tiptap.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/lineMap.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/docEpoch.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/lint.css`, `src/plugins/codemirror/sourceLint.ts`, `src/components/StatusBar/LintBadge.tsx`, `src/services/lint/lintNavigation.ts`, `src/services/assembly/hostAdapters.ts`, `src/services/windowClose/tabCleanup.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/lint.md` §"Triggering lint"; `website/guide/link-check.md` (Source underline vs WYSIWYG block bar); `website/guide/features.md` §"Markdown Lint"
- tests: `src/plugins/lint/tiptap.test.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/tiptapDecorations.test.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/lineMap.test.ts`, `src/plugins/lint/docEpoch.test.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/sourceLint.test.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/__tests__/sourceLint.test.ts`, `src/components/StatusBar/LintBadge.test.tsx`, `src/services/lint/lintNavigation.test.ts`, `src/services/windowClose/tabCleanup.test.ts`; harness `src/plugins/lint/__tests__/testHarness.ts`
- notes: WYSIWYG granularity is block only — no inline squiggle (lint.md says so). Editing in WYSIWYG removes the decorations but leaves the stored diagnostics, so the badge count and F2 targets stay stale until the next run or tab close; features.md §"Markdown Lint" documents this. F2 in WYSIWYG for a non-`sourceOnly` diagnostic moves the selection index but does not scroll. `lineMap` re-serializes the doc per rebuild and renders nothing for a block whose standalone serialization diverges from its in-context form.

### Local link & image existence check
- id: markdown-link-check
- feature: Link check
- summary: On each lint run of a saved document, every local link and image target is checked against the filesystem, and missing targets appear as lint errors.
- capabilities: M001 image file not found; M002 linked file not found; async `exists` per unique resolved path (deduped per run); relative, `./` and `../` targets resolve against the source file's directory and never climb above its root; an absolute target (`/…`, `C:\…`, `C:/…`) names that file directly, and a `/`-rooted target in a Windows document lands on that document's drive; `#fragment` stripped before lookup; percent-decoding of the path part with raw fallback on malformed `%`; skips any RFC-3986 scheme and protocol-relative `//host/…`; refuses to probe UNC `\\host\…` and drive-relative `C:foo` targets (an SMB probe can leak credentials); fragment-only links left to W04; nothing for untitled documents; a thrown `exists()` is classified `error` and produces no diagnostic; range authorization via `createRangeAuthorizer` so a link inside a re-parsed `<details>` body is skipped rather than anchored to the wrong span; per-tab monotonic tokens drop a stale completion, and M001/M002 are replaced (not appended) on each run
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `markdown.lintEnabled = true` and the tab has a file path
- surfaces: automatic, as part of the lint run (menu `check-markdown` / `Alt-Mod-v`); results in the lint badge and F2 navigation
- code: `src/lib/markdownLinkCheck/check.ts`, `src/stores/documentStore/lint.ts`, `src/services/lint/runActiveLint.ts`, `src/utils/markdownPipeline/positionTrust.ts`, `src/utils/markdownUrl.ts`
- rust: none — uses the `fs:allow-exists` capability in `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` (`$HOME/**`, `/Volumes/**`, `/mnt/**`, `/media/**`); a target outside those roots and any runtime grant makes `exists()` throw and is silently skipped
- docs: `website/guide/link-check.md`; cross-referenced from `website/guide/lint.md`
- tests: `src/lib/markdownLinkCheck/check.test.ts`, `src/lib/markdownLinkCheck/detailsOffsets.test.ts`, `src/stores/__tests__/lintStore.test.ts` (token ordering)
- notes: link-check.md documents the absolute-path resolution and the UNC / drive-relative refusal. The module header's `@coordinates-with` says it "runs on save"; it runs only on the explicit lint trigger. Because `lint.check` is not format-gated, it also runs over non-markdown files that have a path (see `markdown-lint-engine`).

### CJK document formatter
- id: cjk-formatter
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: One-shot typographic normalization of Chinese/Japanese text in markdown — spacing, fullwidth conversion, dashes, quotes, ellipsis, cleanup — run only through a protected-region parser and a post-run integrity check.
- capabilities: 24 settings under `cjkFormatting.*` (one literal, `DEFAULT_CJK_FORMATTING`, spread into the settings defaults) — `ellipsisNormalization` = true, `newlineCollapsing` = false, `fullwidthAlphanumeric` = true, `fullwidthPunctuation` = true, `fullwidthParentheses` = true, `fullwidthBrackets` = false, `cjkEnglishSpacing` = true, `cjkParenthesisSpacing` = true, `currencySpacing` = true, `slashSpacing` = true, `spaceCollapsing` = true, `dashConversion` = true, `emdashSpacing` = true, `smartQuoteConversion` = true, `quoteStyle` = "curly" (curly | corner | guillemets), `contextualQuotes` = true, `quoteSpacing` = true, `singleQuoteSpacing` = true, `cjkCornerQuotes` = false, `cjkNestedQuotes` = false, `quoteToggleMode` = "simple" (simple | full-cycle), `consecutivePunctuationLimit` = 0 (0 off, 1 single, 2 double), `trailingSpaceRemoval` = true, `skipReferenceSections` = false; 15 protected region types — fenced_code (an unclosed fence claims the rest of the document), inline_code, indented_code, link_url, image, frontmatter (YAML `---` and TOML `+++`), html_tag, wiki_link, footnote_ref, footnote_def, math_block, math_inline, thematic_break, reference_section, character_reference (HTML `&#x…;` / `&#…;` / `&name;`, shape-matched, so a CJK neighbour cannot turn its `;` fullwidth); GFM tables formatted cell by cell; Latin span scanner protects URLs, emails, domains, versions, decimals, times, thousands separators; Hangul excluded from the spacing rules; script-aware ellipsis (`……` Han/kana, `…` Hangul, `...` Latin); backslash-escaped punctuation preserved; two-space hard breaks kept when the resolved hard-break style is `twoSpaces`; capped fixed-point rule iteration (`MAX_RULE_PASSES` = 8, logs via `cjkFmtWarn` on non-convergence); selection formatting widens to the top-level blocks the selection spans (no selection = the block at the cursor) in both Source and WYSIWYG; WYSIWYG round-trips the blocks through markdown so marks survive; command-palette commands `editor.formatCJK` / `editor.formatCJKFile`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on (each rule individually gated by its `cjkFormatting.*` key)
- surfaces: menu Format → CJK → `format-cjk` (`CmdOrCtrl+Shift+F`, shortcut id `formatCJKSelection`, `Mod-Shift-f`) and `format-cjk-file` (`Alt+CmdOrCtrl+Shift+F`, shortcut id `formatCJKFile`, `Alt-Mod-Shift-f`); palette; settings pane Settings → Language → CJK Formatting (all 24 keys have a row); MCP `document.transform` kind `cjk-format`
- code: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/formatter.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/types.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/markdownParser.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/markdownParserBlocks.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/markdownParserInline.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/characterReferences.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/formatterTables.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules/applyRules.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/latinSpanScanner.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceCjkActions.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterCjk.ts`, `src/plugins/actions/actionDefinitionsText.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/documentTransform.ts`, `src/pages/settings/LanguageSettings.tsx`
- rust: none — menu ids and accelerators in `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/format_menu.rs`, SF Symbol icons in `src-tauri/src/macos_menu_icons.rs`
- docs: `website/guide/cjk-formatting.md`; `website/guide/features.md` §"CJK Formatting"; `website/guide/settings.md` (CJK formatting table); `website/guide/mcp-tools.md` (`document.transform`)
- tests: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/formatter.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/battleTest.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/segments.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/markdownParser.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/latinSpanScanner.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/characterReferences.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/__tests__/*.test.ts` (idempotence property, hard breaks, math-region parity, punctuation adjacency, quote/slash spacing, region gaps/reuse, script awareness, segment edges, trailing newline), `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules/applyRulesCap.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules/fullwidthScaling.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceCjkActions.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceCjkActions.protection.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterCjk.test.ts`
- notes: `formatMarkdown` / `formatMarkdownChecked` are the only entry points; every editor call site and MCP `cjk-format` go through `formatMarkdownChecked`. `formatCJK` / `formatCJKFile` are `disallow` under multi-cursor (`src/plugins/toolbarActions/multiSelectionPolicy.ts`). MCP `cjk-spacing` / `cjk-punctuation` are fixed regex passes over unprotected segments that ignore `cjkFormatting.*` and include Hangul, unlike the formatter; `cjk-punctuation` converts half-width to full-width only — `cjk-formatting.md` §"AI-Assisted Formatting" says all of this.

### CJK format refusal notice
- id: cjk-format-refusal
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: When the post-format integrity check fails, the document is left byte-identical and a red toast says the run was refused — so "refused" is never mistaken for "nothing to change".
- capabilities: three independent signals that must all match before and after — a content skeleton (strip `<br>` spellings, NFKC fold, strip whitespace and punctuation; a sequence, so reordering fails too), counts of seven structural literals (`[^`, `<!--`, ` ``` `, `~~~`, `$$`, `[[`, `` ` ``), and the ordered inventory of HTML character references (shared definition with the parser's protection, so the checker cannot be stricter than the detector); failure details log lengths only, never document text; `formatMarkdownChecked` returns `{ text, refused }`; only a refusal is surfaced (a no-op run is silent by design); all four editor call sites (Source selection/block, Source whole file, WYSIWYG block span, WYSIWYG whole file) call `notifyCjkFormatRefused`; MCP `cjk-format` turns a refusal into a failed response
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: toast (`dialog:toast.cjkFormatRefused`); MCP error for `document.transform` `cjk-format`
- code: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/integrity.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/formatter.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/cjkFormatFeedback.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/documentTransform.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/cjk-formatting.md` §"Integrity Verification"
- tests: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/integrity.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/characterReferences.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/cjkFormatFeedback.test.ts`
- notes: the guide describes only the skeleton check; the structural counts and the character-reference inventory are undocumented (harmless — both only make refusal stricter).

### Quote style toggle at cursor
- id: quote-style-toggle
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Toggle the quote pair enclosing the cursor between straight, curly, corner-bracket and guillemet forms without reformatting the document.
- capabilities: `simple` mode toggles straight ↔ the configured `quoteStyle`; `full-cycle` mode cycles straight → curly → corner → guillemets → straight; innermost enclosing pair wins when nested; apostrophes (`don't`), primes (`5'10"`) and decade abbreviations (`'90s`) never count as pairs (inherited from `analyzeQuotes`); separate bracket-matching pass for corner and guillemet characters; operates within the cursor's textblock only; palette command `editor.toggleQuoteStyle`
- status: shipped-on, partial
- gate: `cjkFormatting.quoteToggleMode = "simple"`; `cjkFormatting.quoteStyle = "curly"`
- surfaces: menu Format → Transform → `toggle-quote-style` (`Shift+CmdOrCtrl+'`), shortcut id `toggleQuoteStyle` (`Shift-Mod-'`); palette (WYSIWYG only); settings pane Settings → Language → CJK Formatting → Quote Toggle Mode
- code: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/quoteToggle.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/quotePairing.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/quoteClassification.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterFormatting.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapter.ts`
- rust: none — menu item in `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/format_menu.rs`
- docs: `website/guide/cjk-formatting.md` §"Toggle Quote Style at Cursor"
- tests: `src/lib/cjkFormatter/quoteToggle.test.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/quotePairing.test.ts`
- notes: partial — WYSIWYG only. The action is declared `supports: { source: false }` and `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceAdapter.ts` has no `toggleQuoteStyle` case (recorded in `src/plugins/toolbarActions/__tests__/parity/parityFixtures.ts`); the guide says so. `quoteToggle.ts` still imports `QuoteStyle` from `@/stores/settingsStore` although the type now lives in `src/lib/cjkFormatter/types.ts`. Non-ASCII quote characters are written as Unicode escapes in source to prevent LLM character substitution.

### CJK letter spacing
- id: cjk-letter-spacing
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Optional display-only letter-spacing on CJK character runs in the WYSIWYG editor and in PDF export, without touching the document.
- capabilities: 7 levels — `0` (off, default), `0.02` Subtle, `0.03` Light, `0.05` Normal, `0.08` Wide, `0.10` Wider, `0.12` Extra (em); inline ProseMirror decorations with class `cjk-spacing`, width from the `--cjk-letter-spacing` variable written by `useTheme`; zero-cost when off (no scan, no decorations); incremental re-scan of changed ranges on doc change; setting changes pushed by a `cjkLetterSpacingChanged` meta transaction; enablement injected through an `isEnabled` option (the plugin reads no store); covers CJK Unified Ideographs, Ext A, Compatibility Ideographs, Hiragana, Katakana, Hangul Syllables, Bopomofo; forced to 0 inside `pre` / `code`; PDF export has its own per-export value, default `0.05em`
- status: shipped-off
- gate: `appearance.cjkLetterSpacing = "0"`
- surfaces: settings pane Settings → Editor → Typography → CJK Letter Spacing; PDF export sidebar
- code: `src/plugins/cjkLetterSpacing/plugin.ts`, `src/plugins/cjkLetterSpacing/cjk-letter-spacing.css`, `src/hooks/useTheme.ts`, `src/hooks/useTiptapSettingsSync.ts`, `src/services/assembly/tiptapExtensions.ts`, `src/pages/settings/EditorSettings.tsx`, `src/export/PdfExportDialog.tsx`, `src/export/PdfSettingsSidebar.tsx`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/cjk-formatting.md` §"CJK Letter Spacing" (all 7 levels); `website/guide/settings.md` (Typography table)
- tests: `src/plugins/cjkLetterSpacing/__tests__/cjkLetterSpacing.test.ts`, `src/plugins/cjkLetterSpacing/__tests__/plugin.test.ts`
- notes: the PDF dialog starts at `0.05em` regardless of the editor setting (it seeds fonts from settings but not letter spacing), so a user with spacing off gets spaced CJK in a PDF unless they change it. `plugin.ts` header still says `@coordinates-with stores/settingsStore.ts — reads appearance.cjkLetterSpacing` two lines above stating the plugin reaches no store.

### CJK text cleanup actions
- id: cjk-cleanup-actions
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Whole-document trailing-whitespace removal and blank-line collapsing, sharing the CJK formatter's rule implementations and hard-break awareness, plus metadata-only line-ending selection.
- capabilities: `removeTrailingSpaces` keeps a 2+-space run after content when the resolved hard-break style is `twoSpaces`; `collapseNewlines` collapses 3+ newlines to 2 and drops standalone `<br />` lines; both apply as a single CodeMirror transaction / PM transform so one undo reverts them, with the cursor clamped into the new text; line-ending actions `lineEndingsLF` / `lineEndingsCRLF` only record the convention (the buffer stays LF; save applies it); palette commands `editor.removeTrailingSpaces`, `editor.collapseBlankLines`, `editor.lineEndingsLF`, `editor.lineEndingsCRLF`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: menu Format → Text Cleanup → `remove-trailing-spaces`, `collapse-blank-lines` (no default accelerator); menu Edit → Line Endings → `line-endings-lf` / `line-endings-crlf`; palette
- code: `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceCjkActions.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterCjk.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterUtils.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules/cleanup.ts`, `src/lib/cjkFormatter/rules/universal.ts`, `src/services/formats/lineEndingMetadata.ts`
- rust: none — menu items in `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/format_menu.rs` and `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/edit_menu.rs`
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Text Cleanup (Format → Text Cleanup)"; `website/guide/cjk-formatting.md` §"Other Cleanup" (the formatter's own trailing-space rule)
- tests: `src/plugins/toolbarActions/sourceCjkActions.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterCjk.test.ts`, `src/plugins/toolbarActions/wysiwygAdapterUtils.test.ts`
- notes: unlike Format CJK, the two cleanup actions run over the raw document with no protected regions — trailing spaces and runs of blank lines inside fenced code blocks are rewritten too; no test pins the fenced-code case either way. All four actions are `disallow` under multi-cursor (`src/plugins/toolbarActions/multiSelectionPolicy.ts`).

### CJK auto-pair brackets and quotes
- id: cjk-auto-pair
- feature: Auto-pair & tab escape
- summary: Typing an opening CJK bracket or curly quote auto-inserts its closing partner, with switches for CJK pairs, curly quotes, and treating a typed right double quote as an opener.
- capabilities: CJK pairs `（）` `【】` `「」` `『』` `《》` `〈〉` plus curly `“”` `‘’`; `markdown.autoPairCJKStyle` = "auto" ("off" | "auto"; off drops CJK and curly pairs); `markdown.autoPairCurlyQuotes` = true; `markdown.autoPairRightDoubleQuote` = false, effective only when CJK pairing AND curly quotes are on; all three ride behind `markdown.autoPairEnabled` = true; config read per keystroke through an injected `getConfig`, so a change applies without rebuilding the editor; persisted blobs missing a key fall back to the shipped default
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `markdown.autoPairCJKStyle = "auto"` (behind `markdown.autoPairEnabled = true`)
- surfaces: automatic while typing in WYSIWYG; settings pane Settings → Editor → Behavior (CJK brackets row disabled when auto-pair is off; curly-quote row shown only when CJK brackets is Auto; right-quote row only when curly quotes are also on)
- code: `src/services/assembly/autoPairConfig.ts`, `src/plugins/autoPair/pairs.ts`, `src/plugins/autoPair/handlers.ts`, `src/plugins/autoPair/tiptap.ts`, `src/pages/settings/EditorSettings.tsx`, `src/stores/settingsTypes/cjk.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` (rows "CJK brackets", "Include curly quotes", "Also pair `"`"); `website/guide/features.md` (CJK pair list)
- tests: `src/services/assembly/autoPairConfig.test.ts`, `src/plugins/autoPair/autoPair.test.ts`, `src/plugins/autoPair/pairs.test.ts`
- notes: settings.md warns curly-quote pairing "may conflict with some IME smart quote features". The Auto-pair & tab escape feature itself (ASCII pairs, tab escape) is described by its owning area; this block covers only the CJK/curly-quote settings.

### IME composition guard (editor)
- id: ime-composition-guard
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Protects CJK/Hangul IME composition in the WYSIWYG and Source editors from other plugins and key handlers, and repairs engine-specific composition bugs.
- capabilities: priority-1200 Tiptap extension intercepting composition first; tracks composition start position, data and pinyin; `filterTransaction` admits doc-changing transactions during composition because ProseMirror omits the `composition` meta when storedMarks are present (#66); key handling claims only DISPATCHED key events (`target !== null`), so a Backspace/Enter that ProseMirror synthesizes from a DOM change is not answered — which keeps committed text in an empty table cell on macOS instead of redrawing the raw pinyin (#1392, prosemirror-view #188); Safari table-header caret fix after `fixUpBadSafariComposition`; macOS WebKit split-block repair for a heading split during composition acceptance; Korean Hangul deferred `splitBlock` after the grace period (Tiptap #4108); multi-block selection fix (Tiptap #5416); 50 ms `IME_GRACE_PERIOD_MS` after `compositionend`; per-view action queues for ProseMirror and CodeMirror (`runOrQueue…Action`, `flush…CompositionQueue`), the CodeMirror plugin flushing on `compositionend` and `blur`; leftover-pinyin prefix cleanup; `isImeKeyEvent` treats `isComposing` or `keyCode === 229` as IME; `useImeComposition` is the ref-based counterpart for React inputs with the same grace period
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/plugins/compositionGuard/tiptap.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/compositionKeys.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/splitBlockFix.ts`, `src/utils/imeGuard.ts`, `src/plugins/codemirror/imeGuard.ts`, `src/hooks/useImeComposition.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Editing Details" (Input methods); `website/guide/multi-cursor.md` (composition affects only the primary cursor)
- tests: `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/tiptap.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/tiptap.imecleanup.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/tiptap.splitblock.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/tiptap.tablefix.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/tiptap.domreconciliation.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/compositionKeys.test.ts`, `src/plugins/compositionGuard/__tests__/splitBlockFix.test.ts`, `src/utils/imeGuard.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useImeComposition.test.ts`; real-engine tiers in block `ime-test-tiers`
- notes: stated known limitation in `src/plugins/compositionGuard/tiptap.ts`: the Safari table-header fix uses heuristic position detection and may not cover all edge cases. The `tiptap.ts` header summary does not yet mention the dispatched-event rule in `compositionKeys.ts`.

### IME-safe toasts
- id: ime-toast
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Notifications are deferred while an IME composition is in progress, so a toast's DOM insertion cannot commit a half-typed CJK character; long toasts can be pinned open.
- capabilities: composition detected on the active Tiptap view and the active CodeMirror view; only `info` / `success` / `message` are deferred (error / warning / loading / dismiss pass through immediately); flush on `compositionend` plus a 60 ms delay; re-checks composition before flushing and re-defers if a new session started; 5 s fallback force-flush if `compositionend` never fires; opt-in `{ pin: true }` adds a Pin button that re-fires the same toast id with `duration: Infinity`; a caller-supplied `action` wins over the pin; `errorDetail(message, detail)` renders a two-line, always-pinnable error with the detail rendered through `commandErrorMessage` (never `"[object Object]"`)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; pin is opt-in per call site
- surfaces: toast (the app-wide toast entry point — about 70 production modules import it)
- code: `src/services/ime/imeToast.ts`, `src/services/ime/imeToastPinAction.tsx`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Editing Details" (Input methods)
- tests: `src/services/ime/imeToast.test.ts`
- notes: `imeToastPinAction.tsx` imports `i18next` directly rather than `@/i18n` to break a dependency cycle. `src/plugins/toolbarActions/cjkFormatFeedback.ts` calls sonner directly, which is safe only because it emits `error` (never deferred anyway).

### Real-engine IME test lanes
- id: ime-test-tiers
- feature: CJK formatting & IME
- summary: Test tiers beyond jsdom that pin composition behaviour in the engine VMark ships; the top tier drives the user's own macOS input method.
- capabilities: `src/test/editorComposition.webkit.test.ts` (Playwright WebKit) — multi-step hiragana composition commits once, committed 你好 lands and serializes, delete-during-composition, Escape-cancel leaves the document unchanged, ASCII after commit does not interleave, composition inside existing text, plus a manual real-OS release checklist; `src/components/Terminal/setupImeCompositionGate.webkit.test.ts` — terminal single-writer guarantee; `src/components/Terminal/browserTier.smoke.webkit.test.ts` — real xterm constructs and real keyboard input drains a microtask between two capture listeners (the WKWebView mechanism synthetic `dispatchEvent` cannot reproduce); `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.webkit.test.ts` — a cancelable `beforeinput` for `insertText` and the chord veto in a real editor; `e2e/journeys/25-editor-ime-composition.mjs` — synthetic composition in the shipping WKWebView (cancelled composition leaves the tab clean, committed one dirties it and survives save into exact on-disk bytes); `e2e/run-ime.mjs` — real macOS IME via OS-level `key code` injection: compose 你好 and 明天 (schema-keyed, 微软双拼 or quanpin), Escape-cancel, Backspace mid-composition, and the scratch tab dirtied
- status: dev-only
- gate: `pnpm test:browser` (`vitest.browser.config.ts`, also the CI `webkit` job); `pnpm e2e:journeys`; `pnpm e2e:ime` requires `VMARK_REAL_IME=1` on macOS
- surfaces: CI and developer command line
- code: `e2e/run-ime.mjs`, `e2e/lib/sessionLock.mjs`, `e2e/journeys/25-editor-ime-composition.mjs`, `vitest.browser.config.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none (`e2e/README.md` is repo-internal)
- tests: `src/test/editorComposition.webkit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/setupImeCompositionGate.webkit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/browserTier.smoke.webkit.test.ts`, `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.webkit.test.ts`, `e2e/sessionLock.test.mjs`; the real-IME lane itself is not run in CI
- notes: only `run-ime.mjs` is macOS-only; the WebKit tier runs on CI Linux. `run-ime.mjs` fails closed, refusing by name on: non-macOS, `VMARK_REAL_IME` unset, missing `.vmark/ime-machine-profile.json` or schema table, unreadable or locked console session (read from `ioreg` IOConsoleUsers, fixtures in `e2e/sessionLock.test.mjs`), missing Accessibility, failed delivery-live probe, IME schema mismatch via `defaults` (adapts, never mutates), unverified input-source switch (previous source restored on every exit path). There is deliberately no automated chord case: System Events injection produces no modifier keydown, so the IME chord guard is covered only by the WebKit test and a manual checklist step.

### IME chord guard
- id: ime-chord-guard
- feature: Keyboard shortcuts
- summary: A command chord typed under a CJK input method (e.g. `` Ctrl+` `` with Chinese punctuation on) no longer leaves the IME's rewritten character (`·`, `【`) in the document or the terminal.
- capabilities: vetoes, at window `beforeinput` in the capture phase, a cancelable single-glyph `insertText` that is not composing while a command modifier is held — so it works whether the IME commits before or after the keydown, and covers ProseMirror, CodeMirror and the xterm helper textarea at once; command modifier is Ctrl or Cmd on macOS, Ctrl only elsewhere (Super never arms it); Ctrl+Alt (AltGr) excluded off macOS; pastes (`insertFromPaste`), composition commits and dead keys untouched; a modifier arms only from its own observed keydown, and any keyboard event whose flag reads up disarms; `blur`, `focus`, `visibilitychange` and `pointerdown` clear the state (the last also covers the macOS emoji picker)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on (installed by the keybinding router)
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.ts`, `src/hooks/useKeybindingRouter.ts`, `src/utils/shortcutMatch.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"A Shortcut Types a Character While a Chinese Input Method Is On"
- tests: `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.test.ts`, `src/services/keybinding/imeChordGuard.webkit.test.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/imeAsciiHandoff.test.ts`; no automated real-IME case (see block `ime-test-tiers`)
- notes: accepted residuals, each pinned by a test — a missed modifier keydown (window entered mid-chord, a remapper's synthetic key) lets one IME character through; the first chord after a Ctrl+click leaks its character until the modifier is pressed again; on macOS a stuck Cmd (swallowed keyup) can still silently veto single-glyph typing until the next click or focus change.

### Font selection and stacks
- id: font-stacks
- feature: Themes, fonts & layout
- summary: Separate Latin, CJK and monospace font choices — a curated key or any installed family — compose into the editor's `--font-sans` / `--font-mono` tokens, and a user-supplied family name is validated before it can reach a stylesheet.
- capabilities: 6 curated Latin keys (`system`, `athelas`, `palatino`, `georgia`, `charter`, `literata`); 6 CJK keys (`system`, `pingfang`, `songti`, `kaiti`, `notoserif`, `sourcehans`); 16 mono keys (`system`, `sfmono`, `monaco`, `menlo`, `consolas`, `dejavu`, `liberation`, `ubuntumono`, `notosansmono`, `notosansmonocjk`, `jetbrains`, `firacode`, `saucecodepro`, `ibmplexmono`, `hack`, `inconsolata`); a setting may instead be `custom:<family>` naming any family (#1429) — a custom Latin family heads the sans stack, a custom CJK family keeps the system CJK stack behind it, a custom mono family keeps the platform tail; sans stack is `<latin>, <cjk>` with the Latin stack's trailing generic stripped so CJK glyphs reach the CJK families (#1056); per-platform mono tail — `ui-monospace, monospace` on macOS and Windows, bare `monospace` on Linux; unknown mono key resolves to the platform tail alone; `customFont.ts` is a deny-list validator (quotes, backslash, `;{}()`, comment markers, commas, `\p{Cc}`/`\p{Cf}` and more; 64-character cap) and an unusable value falls back to the curated system stack; one curated option table shared by Settings and the PDF export sidebar
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `appearance.latinFont = "system"`, `appearance.cjkFont = "system"`, `appearance.monoFont = "system"`
- surfaces: settings pane Settings → Editor → Typography (one picker per role, each with a curated list, an "Installed Fonts" group and "Custom…" typed entry); PDF export sidebar font selects
- code: `src/utils/fontStacks.ts`, `src/utils/customFont.ts`, `src/utils/fontOptions.ts`, `src/hooks/useTheme.ts`, `src/pages/settings/FontSettings.tsx`, `src/pages/settings/FontSettingRow.tsx`, `src/export/PdfSettingsSidebar.tsx`
- rust: none (installed-family enumeration is block *installed-font-enumeration*)
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Typography" and §"Using a font you installed yourself"; `website/guide/features.md` §"Fonts"
- tests: `src/utils/fontStacks.test.ts`, `src/utils/customFont.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/FontSettings.test.tsx`, `src/export/__tests__/pdfPresets.test.ts`; `src/pages/settings/FontSettingRow.tsx` has no sibling test (covered only through `FontSettings.test.tsx`)
- notes: HTML export cannot bundle a local font, so a custom family degrades to the reader's fonts (stated in `settings.md`). Sibling block *typography-fonts* (area 13) covers the typography pane; this block is the stack-resolution and input-validation view.

### Installed font enumeration
- id: installed-font-enumeration
- feature: Themes, fonts & layout
- summary: The font pickers list every font family installed on the Mac, so a custom family can be picked rather than typed.
- capabilities: Rust `list_system_font_families` enumerates via `NSFontManager` on the main thread (async command that hops to main, checking for an existing main-thread marker first); dot-prefixed hidden families dropped; names normalized (sorted, deduped); frontend caches a successful result for the session and never caches a failure; every returned name is re-validated through `sanitizeCustomFontFamily` before use; any failure or non-array payload degrades to an empty list, never an error
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` — other platforms return an empty list and the typed "Custom…" field still works
- surfaces: settings pane Settings → Editor → Typography ("Installed Fonts" group in each picker)
- code: `src-tauri/src/system_fonts.rs`, `src/services/fonts/systemFonts.ts`, `src/hooks/useSystemFontFamilies.ts`, `src/pages/settings/FontSettingRow.tsx`
- rust: `system_fonts::list_system_font_families`
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Using a font you installed yourself" (including the "Installed Fonts is macOS-only" tip)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/system_fonts.test.rs`, `src/services/fonts/systemFonts.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useSystemFontFamilies.test.tsx`
- notes: Windows (DirectWrite) and Linux (fontconfig) enumeration are not implemented. The `@coordinates-with` line in `src/services/fonts/systemFonts.ts` attributes `sanitizeCustomFontFamily` to `utils/fontStacks.ts`; it lives in `src/utils/customFont.ts`.

### Verified monospace stack
- id: verified-mono-stack
- feature: Themes, fonts & layout
- summary: Before a monospace stack is handed to anything that assumes a character grid, it is measured in the running engine and narrowed until it actually renders monospace.
- capabilities: measures the mean advance of `'W' × 32` vs `'i' × 32` at 32 px (the quantity xterm.js derives its cell from); drops leading families one at a time and re-measures, performing the cascade step the engine skipped; memoized per preferred-stack string; returns the preferred stack unmeasured when there is no DOM; a zero-width measurement is treated as "cannot measure", not "not monospace"; falls back to the bare trailing generic when nothing measures monospace; applies to curated and `custom:` mono families alike; `resetMonoStackCache()` for tests
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on (behaviour only differs where the engine misresolves — in practice WebKitGTK under a CJK locale)
- surfaces: automatic — `--font-mono` (code blocks, Source mode), terminal creation and live terminal font sync
- code: `src/services/fonts/verifiedMonoStack.ts`, `src/hooks/useTheme.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/createTerminalInstance.ts`, `src/components/Terminal/terminalSessionStoreSync.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/features.md` §"Fonts"
- tests: `src/services/fonts/verifiedMonoStack.test.ts` (injectable `rendersMonospace`); `src/services/fonts/verifiedMonoStack.webkit.test.ts` leads a stack with `sans-serif` so the guard fails on every engine
- notes: #1334. The memo assumes the installed font set is session-stable; a font installed while VMark runs is measured against a stale cache until restart. Sibling block *terminal-theme-font* (area 7) covers the terminal side.

### Runtime filesystem scope grants
- id: fs-scope-grants
- feature: Save pipeline & file watching
- summary: Files and workspaces opened from outside the static capability roots — Finder, the CLI, a new window, another drive — get an in-memory fs and asset-protocol grant so they can actually be opened.
- capabilities: `allow_fs_read(path)` grants exactly one file on both the fs scope and the asset-protocol scope; `grant_fs_read` is the strict variant that fails when the runtime scope did not take the pattern (`open_*_in_new_window`); the recursive `path/**` grant on both scopes lives only in `workspace::grants::scope::grant_workspace_scope` (strict, and confirmed against a swapped link); per-file callers are Finder/CLI opens (`files/open.rs`), CLI arguments at startup on Windows/Linux (`app_setup.rs`) and the three `open_*_in_new_window` commands after `validate_openable_path` (`window_manager/commands.rs`); best-effort except the new-window path; `confirm_grant_target` re-resolves a name after a grant and fails the grant when it moved (used by the workspace and media grants); the media grant (`asset_access.rs`) does not come through here and extends the asset-protocol scope only; a symlink swapped after the grant is refused because requests are canonicalized; static fs scope `$HOME/**`, `/Volumes/**`, `/mnt/**`, `/media/**` plus `C:\**`–`F:\**` in `windows.json`, and the asset-protocol scope is the same roots (`tauri.conf.json`, `tauri.windows.conf.json`); non-UTF-8 canonical paths rejected; Windows `\\?\` prefix stripping
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic (Finder/CLI open, new-window opens, workspace open, Open Recent, session restore, media tabs)
- code: `src-tauri/src/fs_scope.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/open.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, `src-tauri/src/window_manager/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/window_manager/path_validation.rs`, `src-tauri/src/canonical_path.rs`, `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json`
- rust: `fs_scope::{allow_fs_read, grant_fs_read, confirm_grant_target}`, `workspace::grants::scope::grant_workspace_scope` (no command of its own)
- docs: `website/guide/privacy.md` §"What VMark can read on disk"; `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Folder Access and `forbidden path` Errors"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/fs_scope.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/scope.test.rs`, inline tests in `src-tauri/src/canonical_path.rs` (non-UTF-8 refusal, verbatim-prefix strip), `src-tauri/src/capabilities.test.rs` (static fs roots per window, Windows drive letters stop at F:, asset scope equals the document-window read roots with no catch-all), `src-tauri/src/window_manager/commands.test.rs`, inline tests in `src-tauri/src/workspace/validation.rs`
- notes: Grants are in-memory; recorded folders are re-granted at launch by block `workspace-grants-boundary`, single files are not. A name swapped for a link during a confirmed grant fails the grant, but the allow Tauri already made for the link's target is not revoked: that target stays readable (fs scope: writable) until restart, as `confirm_grant_target` states. A runtime fs grant is not read-only: the fs plugin accepts it for every verb the capability permits, write, rename and remove included. The new-window commands accept any absolute path from webview JS that canonicalizes to a regular file with a registered extension, and grant it, so a script in a document window can gain read/write on one such file anywhere on disk. An image beside a document opened alone from outside the static roots does not render until its folder is opened (privacy.md says so). The per-window static scopes are the area 15 block `capability-sandbox`.

### Rust-owned workspace grants (security boundary)
- id: workspace-grants-boundary
- feature: Workspace management
- summary: Only a folder the user demonstrably chose — in the folder picker Rust shows, from Finder, or recorded from one of those in an earlier session — gets the recursive fs + asset grant; a path a script names is refused.
- capabilities: `pick_workspace_folder` shows the native folder dialog (optionally opened AT a requested folder, title `workspaceAccess.pickTitle` / `confirmTitle`), grants the pick recursively and records it; `request_workspace_confirmation` shows the same dialog at a folder and returns once it is up, settling the pick in the background (a dialog whose callback is dropped, or that answers within `LAUNCH_GRACE`, 300 ms, is `internal`); one dialog at a time (atomic slot held until the answer is recorded, a second request is `conflict`); `allow_workspace_access` canonicalizes the path and re-grants only a recorded root or a folder inside one, otherwise `permission-denied` and nothing extended, with at most 4 checks at once and one per path (`conflict`, `workspaceAccess.checkBusy`); a grant the scope did not take, or whose root moved during the grant, is an error and nothing is recorded; a macOS Finder folder open is granted and recorded before its window opens; the record is `<app data>/workspace-grants.json`, written atomically, read only up to 1 MiB, loaded and re-granted during `setup_app` on up to 8 concurrent workers with a 500 ms bounded wait, so a dead mount neither hangs launch nor holds back other roots, and a root chosen before the load is merged into the file; a recorded root that now resolves elsewhere (e.g. became a link) is not re-granted; the list file is fenced off the fs plugin (`forbid_file`, created at launch so every spelling is caught), refused by `atomic_write_file` / `create_file_exclusive` (the path resolved once; on Unix the folder is held open and judged by identity; everywhere the folder is compared after canonicalize and the name case-insensitively; a path that exists but cannot be resolved counts as the list) and by `run_workflow` when a workflow root contains it (checked on the caller's string and again on the admitted root), and uses an array format the store plugin cannot produce; frontend `resolveWorkspaceAccess` classifies the answer as granted / readable (static scope, probed with one `exists()`) / missing / needs-confirmation / unverified; Open Recent sends an unchosen, unreadable folder to `pick_workspace_folder` opened at it, and the MCP `open_workspace` tool sends it to `request_workspace_confirmation` (the request fails `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` once the dialog is up, `BUSY` when another dialog is open, `INTERNAL` when none could be shown, and keeps its one-shot approval for the retry)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: File → Open Workspace (folder picker); Open Recent Workspace; session restore; Finder folder open (macOS); MCP `vmark.workspace.open_workspace`; automatic re-grant at launch
- code: `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/picker.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/launch.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/scope.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/registry.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/protect.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/write.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/create.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/write/anchored.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/open.rs`, `src/services/workspaces/workspaceAccess.ts`, `src/services/workspaces/openWorkspaceByPath.ts`, `src/services/commands/workspaceCommands.ts`, `src/services/commands/recentWorkspacesCommands.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/workspaceOpenFolder.ts`
- rust: `workspace::grants::commands::allow_workspace_access`, `workspace::grants::picker::{pick_workspace_folder, request_workspace_confirmation}`; `workspace::grants::{grant_chosen_root, restore_at_launch, refuse_list_write, refuse_held_write, refuse_root_containing_list}`, `workspace::grants::scope::grant_workspace_scope`; `files::write::atomic_write_file`, `files::create::create_file_exclusive`
- docs: `website/guide/privacy.md` §"What VMark can read on disk" (Runtime grants); `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Folder Access and `forbidden path` Errors"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/mod.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/registry.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/picker.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/launch.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/scope.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/protect.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/commands_policy.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/write.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/files/open.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/workflow/commands.test.rs`, `src/services/workspaces/workspaceAccess.test.ts`, `src/services/workspaces/openWorkspaceByPath.test.ts`, `src/services/commands/recentWorkspacesCommands.test.ts`, `src/services/commands/workspaceCommands.test.ts`, `src/services/mcpBridge/v2/workspaceOpenFolder.test.ts`; the one native-panel call (`show` in `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/picker.rs`) has no unit test (MockRuntime cannot show a panel)
- notes: What it refuses: any unrecorded path (including `/`), a relative path, a non-folder, a second concurrent picker, a webview-supplied write to the list. Not protected, and the module header says so: any process running as the user (the terminal's shell, an AI provider CLI), which can edit the list; an edit takes effect at the next launch. On Windows `atomic_write_file` / `create_file_exclusive` stay path-based, so a folder swapped between the check and the rename is not caught (`src-tauri/src/files/write/anchored.rs` header). The list holds at most 128 roots (`MAX_ROOTS`), oldest dropped first (`src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/registry.rs`). Feature-side workspace behaviour lives in the Workspace management area.

### Media-only asset access grant
- id: asset-access-grant
- feature: Media (image/video/audio)
- summary: The media viewer can ask for asset-protocol read access to one media file before mounting, and only a media file.
- capabilities: extension-only allowlist of 38 previewable types — images `png jpg jpeg jfif gif webp svg bmp ico avif apng heic heif tiff tif`, video `mp4 webm mov avi mkv m4v ogv mpeg mpg wmv flv 3gp`, audio `mp3 m4a ogg oga wav flac aac opus weba aiff wma`; case-insensitive; judged on the path as named and again on its canonical target, so a link named like media to a non-media file is refused and a path that does not resolve is refused; a non-media path is a hard `Err` that never widens a scope; on success the canonical target is granted with `asset_protocol_scope().allow_file` only (no fs-scope grant) and confirmed with `fs_scope::confirm_grant_target`, and a failed or moved grant is an `Err`; `async`, resolved off the IPC thread; because the asset-protocol scope is the static roots, this grant is what lets a media file outside them render
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic (before a media tab mounts)
- code: `src-tauri/src/asset_access.rs`, `src/utils/mediaExtensions.json`, `src/utils/mediaExtensions.ts`, `src/components/Editor/MediaView/useMediaAsset.ts`
- rust: `asset_access::grant_asset_access`
- docs: `website/guide/privacy.md` §"What VMark can read on disk" (Images and media)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/asset_access.test.rs`, including `media_extensions_come_from_the_shared_file`: the Rust list is `include_str!`'d from `src/utils/mediaExtensions.json`, the same file the TypeScript module imports, so the two cannot drift
- notes: What it refuses: anything without a media extension, including traversal strings like `../../etc/passwd`. What it does not bound is WHICH media file: any file whose name and canonical target carry a media extension, anywhere on disk, is readable over asset:// once webview JS asks (the module header states this); the fs scope is not extended. Still returns `Result<(), String>` (on the command-error ratchet).

### Cryptographic token minting
- id: secret-token
- feature: MCP client config installer
- summary: Not user-facing: one function mints every secret token VMark generates, so the entropy property is defined once.
- capabilities: 64-character hex from two `Uuid::new_v4()` values drawn from `getrandom`; consumers are the ephemeral MCP bridge token written to the port file (`mcp_bridge::state`), the per-client credential written into each AI client's MCP config (`mcp_config::client_tokens`), and trusted-HTML grant tokens (`trusted_html::state`)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/src/secret_token.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_bridge/state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/mcp_config/client_tokens.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/state.rs`
- rust: module `secret_token` (no command of its own)
- docs: none
- tests: `src-tauri/src/secret_token.test.rs`
- notes: The module header says "Two secrets are minted from here" and omits the trusted-HTML consumer. It also claims "32 bytes of CSPRNG entropy"; two v4 UUIDs carry 244 random bits (6 fixed version/variant bits each) — ample, but the stated figure is 12 bits high.

### Trusted HTML preview (security boundary)
- id: trusted-html
- feature: Trusted HTML preview (standalone .html)
- summary: An opt-in, per-file, per-session authorization that lets one standalone `.html` document run scripts in an opaque origin of its own; every other HTML preview stays sandboxed, DOMPurified and script-free.
- capabilities: `trusted_html_grant` mints a token from an explicit user confirmation and is the only way a grant comes into existence; `trusted_html_publish` refuses an unknown token (the Reload action after editing); `trusted_html_revoke` is idempotent; grants are attributed to the runtime-supplied window and reaped by `revoke_window` on `WindowEvent::Destroyed`; limits `MAX_DOC_BYTES` 16 MiB per document, `MAX_GRANTS` 64, `MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` 64 MiB resident; the frontend `htmlTrustStore` owns an exact-path → token map, never persisted, and refuses an untitled document; served from `vmark-trusted://doc/<token>` on macOS/Linux and `http://vmark-trusted.localhost/<token>` on Windows, with its own CSP response header; frame mounted `sandbox="allow-scripts"` with no `allow-same-origin` and an empty `allow` (no delegated permissions); the token is encoded as one path segment; the bridge validates the returned token's 64-hex shape and revokes a malformed grant; local trust is dropped even when the backend revoke fails; a stale indicator appears when the source diverges from what the frame runs
- status: shipped-off
- gate: `formats.htmlPreview = false` (the HTML preview itself is off by default); trust is then opt-in per file per session, nothing persisted
- surfaces: "Enable trusted preview…" in the bar above the HTML preview, with a warning; the bar then reads "Trusted — scripts enabled" with "Revoke trust"; Settings → Formats (HTML preview toggle)
- code: `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/protocol.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/commands.rs`, `src/services/trustedHtml/trustedHtmlBridge.ts`, `src/stores/htmlTrustStore.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/htmlTrust.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/HtmlTrustBar.tsx`
- rust: `trusted_html::commands::trusted_html_grant`, `trusted_html_publish`, `trusted_html_revoke`; scheme registered in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`
- docs: `website/guide/formats.md` §"Trusted HTML preview (opt-in)"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/state.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/commands.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/protocol.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/trusted_html/tauri_pin.test.rs`; `src/services/trustedHtml/trustedHtmlBridge.test.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/htmlTrust.test.ts`, `src/lib/formats/adapters/HtmlTrustBar.test.tsx`; real-WebKit origin isolation in `src/lib/formats/adapters/__tests__/trustedHtmlIsolation.webkit.test.ts`; cross-layer scheme/token contract gate `scripts/check-trusted-html-contract.mjs` (`pnpm lint:trusted-html`)
- notes: What it refuses: a grant inferred from any path or extension; `publish` on an unknown token; a sibling file of a trusted one; an untitled buffer. The Windows origin is derived from wry's custom-protocol source and has not been exercised by a Windows run (unverified). Residual risks recorded in code: `window.webkit.messageHandlers` is reachable from the frame and only the missing `__TAURI_INVOKE_KEY__` (bootstrap injected `for_main_frame_only`) stops an invoke — `tauri_pin.test.rs` fails on a Tauri bump so this is re-verified; a webview destroyed while the process lives leaves that window's grants resident until quit. Sibling block *trusted-html-preview* (area 1) covers the editor-facing view.

### Export HTML sanitizer
- id: export-html-sanitizer
- feature: Export & print (HTML/PDF)
- summary: Editor artifacts are stripped from rendered HTML before any export path writes or copies it, so exported output does not carry ProseMirror internals.
- capabilities: removes `.ProseMirror-separator` and `.ProseMirror-trailingBreak` elements; removes `.html-preview-block` / `.html-preview-inline` placeholders entirely; strips `contenteditable`, `draggable`, `sourceline`, `data-render-mode` from every element; strips `display: none` from inline styles and drops an emptied `style`; empties paragraphs holding only `<br>` or whitespace while keeping them for spacing; leaves `asset://` URLs for the later `resolveResources` pass; applied on all four paths — HTML export (`htmlExport.ts`), print and native PDF (`prepareExportBody` / `renderPrintableHtml` in `printDocument.ts`) and Copy as HTML
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic (Export HTML, Print, Export PDF, Copy as HTML)
- code: `src/export/htmlSanitizer.ts`, `src/export/htmlExport.ts`, `src/export/printDocument.ts`, `src/export/useExportOperations.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/export.md` (HTML export, Print / Export PDF, Copy as HTML); the artifact stripping itself is not documented
- tests: `src/export/htmlSanitizer.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/copyAsHtml.test.ts`, `src/export/__tests__/printDocument.test.ts`
- notes: Cosmetic cleanup, not XSS defence — no tag/attribute allowlist; it runs on already-rendered editor output. The rendered-markdown XSS boundary is `src/utils/sanitize.ts` + `src/utils/htmlAllowlists.ts` (DOMPurify; owned by *Markdown round-trip artifacts & frontmatter panel*). The module header's `@coordinates-with` names only `htmlExport.ts`; `printDocument.ts` also calls it.

### Link protocol allowlist
- id: link-protocol-policy
- feature: Tabs & windows
- summary: Activating a link routes it by kind — heading, file tab, or OS opener — and only an allowlisted URL scheme ever reaches the OS opener; a floor of schemes can never be enabled by a setting.
- capabilities: `openLinkTarget` is the single router shared by WYSIWYG and Source link controllers: `#fragment` → heading navigator, file path → `open-file` tab event (relative resolved against the active document, absolute as written, UNC refused, fragment carried), URL → `openExternalLink`; always-safe schemes `http:`, `https:`, `mailto:`; user-extensible via `advanced.customLinkProtocols`; hard `NEVER_OPENABLE_SCHEMES` floor `javascript:`, `vbscript:`, `data:`, `file:`, `blob:`, `filesystem:`, `about:`, `view-source:`, `jar:`, `chrome:`, `chrome-extension:`; malformed URLs blocked; non-string entries dropped on hydration and on cross-window settings merge (`reconcile.ts`); persisted list unioned with the defaults on every hydration; the same custom list feeds clipboard-URL detection
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `advanced.customLinkProtocols = ["obsidian","vscode","dict","x-dictionary"]`
- surfaces: automatic on link activation (Cmd/Ctrl+click, link popup open icon, both modes); settings pane Settings → Advanced → Link Protocols
- code: `src/services/navigation/linkOpen.ts`, `src/pages/settings/AdvancedSettings.tsx`, `src/stores/settingsStore.ts`, `src/stores/settingsStore/reconcile.ts`, `src/services/editor/clipboardUrl.ts`
- rust: none — the `opener` plugin's capability scope (`opener:default` plus `ccswitch://*`) is a second gate
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Link Protocols"
- tests: `src/services/navigation/linkOpen.test.ts`, `src/services/navigation/linkSecurity.test.ts`, `src/services/editor/clipboardUrl.test.ts`, `src/stores/settingsStore.test.ts`, `src/stores/__tests__/settingsStore.fault.test.ts`
- notes: Because the persisted list is unioned with the defaults on every load, a removed default (`obsidian`, `vscode`, `dict`, `x-dictionary`) returns at next launch; `settings.md` §"Link Protocols" says so, and says the list also decides which links may open.

### Media path validation
- id: media-path-validation
- feature: Media (image/video/audio)
- summary: A media `src` is classified before it is resolved: URI schemes, home-relative paths and directory-naming paths are refused; relative paths, `..` included, resolve against the document's directory.
- capabilities: `hasUriScheme` (RFC 3986 scheme grammar, case-insensitive) is the one definition all media resolvers share; `isRelativePath` rejects any scheme, absolute paths, `~/` and `~`, empty/whitespace, and a path whose last segment is empty, `.` or `..`; a `..` segment followed by a filename is ordinary and resolved (#1433); `validateImagePath` classifies with the same rules; exports bound embedded files separately via `getExportContainmentRoot` (the open workspace when the document is inside it, else the document folder)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic (image/video/audio node rendering, Source-mode image preview, paste, export)
- code: `src/plugins/shared/mediaSecurity.ts`, `src/services/media/resolveMediaSrc.ts`, `src/plugins/imageView/resolveSrc.ts`, `src/plugins/imagePreview/resolveSrc.ts`, `src/export/resourcePaths.ts`
- rust: none (paired with `asset_access::grant_asset_access`)
- docs: `website/guide/media-support.md` §"Path Resolution" and §"Security"
- tests: `src/plugins/shared/mediaSecurity.test.ts`, `src/export/resourcePaths.test.ts`
- notes: This classifies, it does not confine: absolute paths are converted on an earlier branch without validation. What an embed can actually load is bounded by the asset-protocol scope — the static roots plus runtime grants (block `fs-scope-grants`) — and only export applies a containment root. `media-support.md` §"Security" omits the home-relative refusal.

### Locale detection and UI language switch
- id: locale-detect
- feature: Localization (10 languages)
- summary: On first run VMark picks its UI language from the OS locale; afterwards the persisted choice wins, and switching it re-labels the webview and the native menu in one transaction.
- capabilities: 10 locales — `en`, `zh-CN`, `zh-TW`, `ja`, `ko`, `de`, `es`, `fr`, `it`, `pt-BR`; reads `navigator.languages` (else `navigator.language`) in preference order; `zh` disambiguated by `Hans`/`Hant` script subtag, then region (`tw`/`hk`/`mo` → `zh-TW`, otherwise `zh-CN`); any `pt` → `pt-BR`; no match → `en`, never throws; the picker lists only locales whose `common.json` is bundled (`import.meta.glob`); a switch changes the JS locale, invokes the Rust `set_locale`, rebuilds the native menu (re-populating genies, recent files and recent workspaces), and persists only after all three succeed; on failure the JS locale is reverted and the Rust locale and menu revert is fired without being awaited; reentrancy-guarded
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `general.language = resolveInitialLanguage()` (OS-derived on first run)
- surfaces: settings pane Settings → Language
- code: `src/utils/localeDetect.ts`, `src/pages/settings/LanguageSettings.tsx`, `src/stores/settingsStore/defaults.ts`, `src/i18n.ts`, `src/services/menu/rebuildNativeMenu.ts`
- rust: `menu::set_locale` (`src-tauri/src/menu/localized.rs`)
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Language"
- tests: `src/utils/localeDetect.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/LanguageSettings.test.tsx`
- notes: settings.md §"Language" documents the OS-derived default. The wider i18n machinery (namespaces, lazy loading, startup menu sync) is described by the area 13 block `localization`.

### Diagnostic logging tiers
- id: debug-logging
- feature: infrastructure
- summary: Not user-facing: two frontend logging tiers — dev-only `console.log` helpers that vanish from production bundles, and warn/error helpers that in production also write to the on-disk log users attach to bug reports.
- capabilities: per-subsystem named loggers (`historyLog`, `autoSaveLog`, … in `log.ts`; tagged warn loggers built by `createWarnLogger`; error loggers); each dev logger is a compile-time ternary on `import.meta.env.DEV` so the no-op arm is tree-shaken; warn/error always print to the console, and in production builds are also forwarded (lazily imported) to `@tauri-apps/plugin-log`; object arguments are serialized, and a non-serialisable one is logged by kind rather than as `"[object Object]"`; the Rust log plugin writes Stdout + LogDir + Webview targets at Debug in debug builds and Info in release, 5 MB per file, one rotated file kept
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `import.meta.env.DEV` for the dev tier; warn/error always on (file persistence in production builds only)
- surfaces: automatic; log directory documented in the troubleshooting guide
- code: `src/utils/debug.ts`, `src/utils/debug/log.ts`, `src/utils/debug/warn.ts`, `src/utils/debug/error.ts`, `src/utils/debug/internals.ts`, `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`
- rust: `tauri_plugin_log` registered in `app_plugins::register`; command `app_setup::debug_log` is `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`; `app_setup::window_close_log` and `app_setup::update_log` ship in release
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Log Files"
- tests: `src/utils/__tests__/debug.test.ts`, `src/utils/debug/internals.test.ts`, `src/utils/debug/warn.test.ts`
- notes: frontend warn/error in a dev build reach the console only, not the log file. The `src/utils/debug.ts` header says the log plugin is "registered first in lib.rs"; it is registered in `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`, after the single-instance plugin. The dev-only `window.__VMARK_DEBUG__` handle is the area 13 block `dev-debug-handle`.

### Menu-ID contract
- id: menu-id-contract
- feature: Native menu bar & menu events
- summary: Not user-facing: every native menu item the Rust side declares is either routed through the action registry or explicitly excluded, checked by a test against a generated manifest.
- capabilities: regex extraction of `MenuItem::with_id(app|&app, "id", …)` from the Rust menu sources; dynamic ids containing `{` skipped; `isMenuSourceFile` excludes sibling `*.test.rs` so test fixtures never enter the contract, shared by the generator and the contract test; `src/shared/menu-ids.json` holds `menuIds` (87 action-routed) and `allMenuIds` (169); `EXCLUDED_MENU_IDS` lists ids handled by dedicated listeners or natively, grouped under reason comments; `partitionMenuIds` splits the extracted set; `actionRegistry.test.ts` pins `MENU_TO_ACTION` bidirectionally against `menuIds`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on (build/test-time contract)
- surfaces: automatic; regenerate with `pnpm extract-menu-ids`
- code: `src/shared/menuIdExtraction.ts`, `src/shared/menu-ids.json`, `scripts/extract-menu-ids.ts`, `src/plugins/actions/menuMapping.ts`
- rust: source of truth is `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/*.rs` and `src-tauri/src/menu/dynamic.rs`
- docs: none (`website/guide/shortcuts.md` documents the resulting shortcuts)
- tests: `src/shared/menuIdExtraction.test.ts` (fails when the checked-in JSON drifts from Rust, and pins the test-file exclusion), `src/plugins/actions/actionRegistry.test.ts`
- notes: extraction fails open — an id the regex never matches is never checked, so a new spelling of `MenuItem::with_id` would silently escape the contract.

### Window bootstrap and ready barrier
- id: window-context
- feature: Tabs & windows
- summary: Not user-facing: per-window bootstrap that decides what a newly created window shows and signals readiness to Rust before content renders.
- capabilities: window-label detection; per-window localStorage preparation (a `doc-*` window does not inherit main's persisted workspace state; the Settings window reads the source document window's workspace key); workspace-transfer then tab-transfer payload claiming from the Rust registries before normal init; URL-param startup files and workspace root; workspace resolution for Finder/drag opens via `openPolicy`; either startup workspace root is re-granted (`regrantWorkspaceAccess`, awaited; a refusal still opens) before it is opened and read; startup-content policy in `openStartupContent` — a forced blank untitled tab only for an explicitly created `doc-*` window with no file, the launch window lands on the WelcomeScreen; per-window browser session restore; StrictMode double-invoke guard set synchronously so Rust gets exactly one `ready`; Settings and other non-document windows skip document init entirely; runtime transfer listeners attached after ready
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/contexts/WindowContext.tsx`, `src/contexts/useWindowReady.ts`, `src/contexts/startupFileOpen.ts`, `src/contexts/tabTransferHandlers.ts`, `src/contexts/prepareWindowStorage.ts`

- docs: none directly; resulting behaviour in `website/guide/tab-navigation.md` and `website/guide/workspace-management.md`
- tests: `src/contexts/WindowContext.test.tsx`, `src/contexts/WindowContext.restoredGrant.test.tsx`, `src/contexts/useWindowReady.test.tsx`, `src/contexts/readyBarrierScope.test.ts`, `src/contexts/startupFileOpen.test.ts`, `src/contexts/tabTransferHandlers.test.ts`; e2e `e2e/journeys/01-boot-editor-ready.mjs`
