### Extension composition contract
- id: extension-composition
- feature: Editor modes (WYSIWYG/Source/Split)
- summary: Not user-facing: the single path by which editor features become part of the running composition, with deterministic ownership of ambiguous markdown nodes.
- capabilities: `resolve` flattens extension groups, dedupes by `id` (the same descriptor twice is fine, two descriptors with one id is an error), validates `requires`/`before`/`after` references, and performs a stable topological sort where constraints are hard and bucket/registration order is only the tie-break; a dangling ordering reference is an error; on any error the ordering is empty rather than partial; `claim` ranks ownership bids `exact` > `semantic` > `fallback`, treats two winning-strength claims as an error, records every bid, makes `fallback` a contributed claim, and lets a throwing recognizer decline; `pmConverterRegistry` keys the markdown↔ProseMirror converters
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/lib/extensions/resolve.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/claim.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/pmConverterRegistry.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/types.ts`, `src/services/assembly/extensionOrdering.ts`, `src/services/assembly/createTiptapExtensions.ts`, `src/utils/markdownPipeline/mdastParagraphClaims.ts`, `src/plugins/codePreview/fenceRegistry.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none (`.claude/adr/ADR-015-extension-model.md`)
- tests: `src/lib/extensions/resolve.test.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/claim.test.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/adoption.test.ts`, `src/lib/extensions/pmConverterRegistry.test.ts`
- notes: decides user-visible node ownership — whether a paragraph holding one media element becomes `block_image`, `block_video`, `block_audio` or stays a paragraph, and which renderer owns a fence language.

### Leaf utilities with user-visible behaviour
- id: leaf-utilities
- feature: infrastructure
- summary: Not user-facing: small pure modules in `src/utils/` that each own one user-observable rule so two surfaces cannot disagree about it.
- capabilities: `motion.ts` — one owner for "should this animate?", with an eslint `no-restricted-syntax` rule forbidding a literal `behavior: "smooth"` elsewhere; `platform.ts` — `navigator.platform` sniffing read at call time; `displayFileName.ts` — only a registered extension is hidden, never an empty label; `appName.ts` — one untranslated spelling of the brand for window titles; `shortcutMatch.ts` — `Mod-Shift-n` matching with a physical-key-code fallback for CJK IME remapping and an `event.code` fallback for macOS Ctrl+letter; `wordSegmentation.ts` — `Intl.Segmenter` word boundaries (CJK-aware) with a regex fallback; `stringWidth.ts` — CJK counts as width 2 for table alignment; `focusGuard.ts` — blocks menu actions while focus is outside the editor; `reloadGuard.ts` — dirty-document reload prevention, letting Ctrl+R through to a focused terminal; `reentryGuard.ts` — per-window `windowLabel:operation` async locks that return `undefined` rather than throw; `rovingTabFocus.ts` — shared tablist roving focus; `safeUnlisten.ts` — swallows unlisten rejections when a component unmounts before `listen()` resolves
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src/utils/motion.ts`, `src/utils/platform.ts`, `src/utils/displayFileName.ts`, `src/utils/appName.ts`, `src/utils/shortcutMatch.ts`, `src/utils/wordSegmentation.ts`, `src/utils/stringWidth.ts`, `src/utils/focusGuard.ts`, `src/utils/reloadGuard.ts`, `src/utils/reentryGuard.ts`, `src/utils/rovingTabFocus.ts`, `src/utils/safeUnlisten.ts`
- rust: none
- docs: none — the resulting behaviours are described on the pages of the features that use them
- tests: `src/utils/motion.test.ts`, `src/utils/platform.test.ts`, `src/utils/displayFileName.test.ts`, `src/utils/shortcutMatch.test.ts`, `src/utils/wordSegmentation.test.ts`, `src/utils/stringWidth.test.ts`, `src/utils/focusGuard.test.ts`, `src/utils/reloadGuard.test.ts`, `src/utils/reentryGuard.test.ts`, `src/utils/rovingTabFocus.test.ts`, `src/utils/safeUnlisten.test.ts`; `src/utils/appName.ts` has no sibling test
- notes: the spine assigns most of these files to features (for example `motion.ts` to Themes, fonts & layout, `shortcutMatch.ts` to Keyboard shortcuts); this block groups them only because each encodes one cross-surface rule.

## Area 15 — Platform integrations and app backbone (Rust)

Verified: `bf8b69ceb`

The Rust composition root is `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` (`run`, `manage_state`), with plugin registration in `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`, one-time setup and `RunEvent` dispatch in `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, and the command list in `src-tauri/src/command_registry.rs`. The frontend's per-window service list is `src/services/runtimeWiring.ts`. Menus (area 13), the terminal's shell discovery (area 7), keychain storage (area 9), fs-scope and save-target resolution (areas 4/5), runtime fs grants and Rust-owned workspace grants (area 14 blocks `fs-scope-grants`, `workspace-grants-boundary`), live document refs (area 5), temp-HTML and the PDF smoke harness (area 6) and `mcp-bridge-path-guard` (area 10) are described in their owning areas.

### App bootstrap & Tauri plugin surface
- id: app-bootstrap-plugins
- feature: App bootstrap & IPC contract
- summary: Not user-facing: one composition root builds the Tauri app, registers every plugin the webview can call, manages the app-wide backend state, and dispatches app-level lifecycle events; the document window starts its window-lifetime services from one list.
- capabilities: plugin registration in order (single-instance first and off macOS only, then log, opener, fs, dialog, clipboard-manager, shell, store, updater with an `X-Machine-Id` header, process, notification, window-state); window-state with `settings`/`pdf-export` denylisted and `VISIBLE` excluded from restore; managed state via `manage_state` (workflow runner, AI cancel registry, MCP bridge, hot-exit, content server, browser surface, window status, PDF export gate, trusted-HTML grants, close-to-tray, workspace grants) plus PTY and coherence state added in `setup_app`; `vmark-trusted` URI scheme served with `try_state` and a fail-closed 404; `RunEvent` dispatch (ExitRequested, WindowEvent Destroyed/Focused, macOS Reopen/Opened); on `Destroyed` the backend reaps the window's file-open slot, ready flag, transfers, window status, file watcher, PTY sessions and MCP workspace registration; setup first re-grants the recorded workspace roots (`workspace::grants::restore_at_launch` in `src-tauri/src/workspace/grants/launch.rs`: bounded wait, roots resolved concurrently, a root still resolving at the deadline granted late; `main` is already built, but no window can load or invoke until setup returns), installs default genies, cleans the legacy `~/.vmark/`, logs the content-server runtime state, and applies conditional menu-item visibility before any window paints; `runtimeWiring` starts grant sync, browser lease/tab/recorder wiring, coherence scan-on-change, window workspace sync, browser AI and workflow policy pushes, and conditional menu sync, transactionally
- status: shipped-on, dev-only
- gate: always on; the Tauri MCP automation bridge is `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` only
- surfaces: automatic (process start, window lifetime)
- code: `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, `src-tauri/src/main.rs`, `src/services/runtimeWiring.ts`
- rust: `vmark_lib::run`, `manage_state`, `app_plugins::{register, attach_automation_bridge}`, `app_setup::{setup_app, handle_run_event, machine_id_hash}`
- docs: `website/guide/privacy.md` §"The update check in detail" (links `app_setup.rs` for `machine_id_hash`); otherwise none (developer-facing)
- tests: `src-tauri/src/lib.test.rs` (4 tests, incl. `manage_state_registers_every_backend_state` on a mock builder), `src-tauri/src/app_setup.test.rs` (5 tests: machine-id digest, and a source pin that `setup_app` restores the workspace grants before menus, genies, the file-open queue and the `ready` listener), `src/services/runtimeWiring.test.ts`; no test drives `handle_run_event`'s `Destroyed` reaping as a whole
- notes: `dev-only` applies to the automation bridge alone: it binds `127.0.0.1:9323`, the port is probed first, and a busy port means no bridge plus an stderr line rather than a slid port. `default.json` grants `mcp-bridge:default` in every build, although the plugin is registered only in debug builds. Release profile keeps `panic = "unwind"` because `catch_unwind` containment (task.rs, pty, hot_exit, cli_install, workflow approval) depends on it.

### Linux WebKitGTK blank-window workaround
- id: linux-webkit-dmabuf-workaround
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: On Linux, VMark sets `WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1` before GTK/WebKit start, so AMD/Mesa/KDE combinations do not boot to a blank content area.
- capabilities: variable set only when unset, so `WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=0` opts back in; no-op on macOS and Windows
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`
- surfaces: automatic (before `run()`)
- code: `src-tauri/src/main.rs`
- rust: `apply_linux_webkit_workarounds`
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Blank Window on Linux"
- tests: none
- notes: Linux only. Issue #1058 (closed), tauri-apps/tauri#11994.

- gate: always on

- code: `src-tauri/src/command_registry.rs`, `src-tauri/src/command_error.rs`, `src-tauri/src/command_error_from.rs`, `src/services/commands/commandError.ts`, `scripts/check-ipc-contract.mjs`, `scripts/check-command-error-ratchet.mjs`

- docs: none
- tests: `src-tauri/src/command_error.test.rs` (13 tests), `src-tauri/src/command_error_from.test.rs` (10 tests), `src/services/commands/commandError.test.ts`, `scripts/check-ipc-contract.test.mjs`
- notes: A command no literal `invoke()` names is reported by `--report` only, not failed (const-resolved names, MCP and e2e paths). 22 files still carry legacy `Result<T, String>` commands (`scripts/command-error-baseline.json`), including `webview_edit.rs`.

### Per-window capability sandbox
- id: capability-sandbox
- feature: App bootstrap & IPC contract
- summary: Tauri permissions are granted per window label, so the Settings and PDF-export windows get far less than document windows, and Windows drive-letter filesystem globs live in their own platform-scoped file.
- capabilities: `default.json` (`main`, `doc-*`) — fs read/write/stat/rename/remove/mkdir/copy/read-dir under `$HOME/**`, `/Volumes/**`, `/mnt/**`, `/media/**`; `opener:allow-open-url` for `ccswitch://*`; `shell:allow-execute` for the `vmark-mcp-server` sidecar; spawn/kill, updater, process restart/exit, notification, clipboard (text and image), store, window-state, log, print, `mcp-bridge:default`. `settings.json` — UI, store, dialog, opener, `updater:default`, `ccswitch://*`; no fs, shell or PTY. The asset-protocol scope in `tauri.conf.json` is the same four static roots, with `C:\**`–`F:\**` added by `tauri.windows.conf.json`. `pdf-export.json` — dialog, print, `opener:allow-open-path`, read `$APPDATA/temp/**`, no fs write (the PDF is written by the Rust `export_pdf` command). `windows.json` — the same fs verbs over `C:\**` through `F:\**`, `"platforms": ["windows"]`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `windows.json` is Windows-only
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/capabilities/default.json`, `src-tauri/capabilities/settings.json`, `src-tauri/capabilities/pdf-export.json`, `src-tauri/capabilities/windows.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json`
- rust: none (data); runtime widening lives in `fs_scope.rs`, `workspace/grants/` and `asset_access.rs` (area 14 security blocks)
- docs: `website/guide/privacy.md` §"What VMark can read on disk"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/capabilities.test.rs` (10 tests: the `ccswitch://` scopes, default/settings window disjointness, document windows reach exactly the static roots, drive letters Windows-only and stopping at F:, the PDF window reads app temp and writes nothing, the Settings window has no fs or shell permission, the asset scope equals the document-window read roots on every platform with no catch-all)
- notes: Each file carries a `[capability-version: x.y.z]` marker. Backslash globs are split out because they are invalid globs on Unix. `store:default` (document and Settings windows) lets webview JS load and save a store at any absolute path — `tauri-plugin-store` resolves the path against app data with `PathBuf` join semantics, so an absolute path replaces the base — writing a pretty-printed JSON object there. `default.json` grants `mcp-bridge:default` in every build, although the plugin is registered only in debug builds.

### Window chrome & app security config
- id: window-chrome-config
- feature: App bootstrap & IPC contract
- summary: The configured main window is an 800×600 overlay-titlebar window with a hidden title and repositioned macOS traffic lights, under a CSP that allows only same-origin scripts and the trusted-HTML frame origin.
- capabilities: `titleBarStyle: "Overlay"`, `hiddenTitle: true`, `trafficLightPosition {x: 19, y: 28}`, `minWidth 480` / `minHeight 400`; `macOSPrivateApi: true`; `withGlobalTauri: true`; CSP `script-src 'self'`, `frame-src 'self' vmark-trusted: http://vmark-trusted.localhost`, `img-src` adds `asset:`, `https:`, `http:`, `blob:`, `data:`; `assetProtocol` enabled with scope `$HOME/**`, `/Volumes/**`, `/mnt/**`, `/media/**` (plus `C:\**`–`F:\**` from `src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json` on Windows); `plugins.fs.requireLiteralLeadingDot: false`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `trafficLightPosition` needs the `macos-private-api` Cargo feature
- surfaces: automatic
- code: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`
- rust: runtime windows (`doc-*`, `settings`, `pdf-export`) are built by `window_manager` (area 5), which restates the traffic-light position
- docs: none for the chrome specifics
- tests: `src/shell/trafficLights.test.ts` (config, `window_manager` and `trafficLights.ts` agree), `src-tauri/src/window_manager/document_windows.test.rs`, `src-tauri/src/capabilities.test.rs` (asset scope)
- notes: The traffic-light position is macOS-only. `Cargo.toml` records that `macos-private-api` must stay literal in the `features` array (tauri-build reads manifest text) and that it bars Mac App Store submission. The `http://vmark-trusted.localhost` frame source is the Windows form of the custom scheme.

### File associations & Windows installer hooks
- id: file-associations
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: VMark registers as an editor for markdown, text, JSON, YAML, TOML, Mermaid, SVG and HTML files, and on Windows the installer yields the default to any handler already present and cleans up after itself on uninstall.
- capabilities: 15 extensions (`md`, `markdown`, `mdown`, `mkd`, `mdx`, `txt`, `json`, `jsonl`, `yaml`, `yml`, `toml`, `mmd`, `svg`, `html`, `htm`), each with a MIME type and `role: "Editor"`; NSIS `POSTINSTALL` writes `OpenWithProgids` for every extension and gives the default back where HKLM already names a handler (#1378); NSIS `POSTUNINSTALL` deletes the empty per-user ProgID the association cleanup leaves behind (which shadowed the machine-wide handler) and re-creates `HKCR\.txt\ShellNew` (#1142)
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; the NSIS hooks apply to the Windows `.exe` installer only
- surfaces: Finder / Explorer / desktop launcher double-click, Open With
- code: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/windows/installer-hooks.nsh`
- rust: consumed by `files::open` and `supported_files::filter_supported_args`
- docs: `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Opening a file from outside the current workspace" (single-instance handoff only); `website/guide/formats.md` §"Opening files from your system" (the associated types and the Windows installer behaviour)
- tests: `scripts/release-smoke-windows-installer.test.mjs` (hook macros, the smoke job's extension list and `fileAssociations` must agree), `.github/workflows/release-smoke.yml` job `windows-installer` (two install/uninstall cycles against the published installer)
- notes: Verified on a real Windows runner by release-smoke from v0.9.67 on. The `.nsh` header still says "Both repairs below run from NSIS_HOOK_POSTUNINSTALL" although the file now holds a third, POSTINSTALL repair; the `windows-installer` job header still says the hook "had never run on Windows".

### App bundle, packaging & release artifacts
- id: app-bundle-packaging
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: Every platform's installer is built in CI with updater artifacts, a bundled MCP sidecar, workflow resources and macOS entitlements; each install format is served its own updater bundle.
- capabilities: `bundle.targets: "all"`, `createUpdaterArtifacts: true`; `externalBin: binaries/vmark-mcp-server`; `resources: resources/workflows/**/*`; macOS `minimumSystemVersion 13.4`, `Resources/Assets.car`, sidecar entitlements; release matrix: macOS aarch64 and x86_64, Windows, Ubuntu 22.04; `latest.json` derived from a platform-key table with per-installer keys (`{os}-{arch}-{installer}`), and the release fails if a signed bundle is unreachable from it (#1444); dev builds override the identifier to `app.vmark.dev` with `icons-dev/`; post-release smoke on the published DMG and Windows installer
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; dev identifier via `src-tauri/tauri.dev.conf.json`
- surfaces: CI release build, installers, Homebrew cask
- code: `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/tauri.dev.conf.json`, `src-tauri/sidecar-entitlements.plist`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/release-smoke.yml`
- rust: none
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Updates" (the update matches the install format); `README.md` Install section
- tests: `scripts/check-release-updater-targets.test.mjs`, `scripts/release-smoke-windows-installer.test.mjs`, `scripts/release-smoke-kb-runtime.test.mjs`, `.github/workflows/pdf-smoke.yml`
- notes: The dev-identifier split is load-bearing: a shared identifier makes two processes read-modify-write one session (see `single-instance`). The macOS release-smoke asserts `cli=missing` for the Knowledge Base runtime (rule 60 §12, D1).

### Diagnostic logging
- id: app-logging
- feature: App bootstrap & IPC contract
- summary: Backend and frontend log lines go to stdout, a rotating file in the OS log directory, and the webview console; two INFO-tier commands make window-close and update stalls visible in a release log.
- capabilities: `tauri_plugin_log` with Stdout + LogDir + Webview targets, 5 MB per file, `KeepOne` rotation, Debug level in debug builds and Info in release; `debug_log` (debug builds only), `window_close_log` (INFO, #1253), `update_log` (INFO, #1270); `CloseRequested` logged at INFO in `window_events.rs`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` for `debug_log`; the rest always on
- surfaces: automatic; log file
- code: `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`
- rust: `app_setup::{debug_log, window_close_log, update_log}`
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Log Files"
- tests: none
- notes: none

### Finder / CLI / Explorer file-open routing
- id: file-open-routing
- feature: Save pipeline & file watching
- summary: Files handed to VMark by Finder, the `vmark` CLI, a second launch or a file-association double-click are queued until a window's frontend is ready, then delivered to the last-focused ready document window; on macOS, folders open as workspaces and a dock click with no windows reopens the most-recent workspace.
- capabilities: cold-start queue drained by `get_pending_file_opens`; readiness and queue insertion under one mutex; hot opens target the last-focused ready document window via a label-filtered broadcast; paths grouped by workspace root; per-path fs-scope read grant before delivery; macOS `RunEvent::Opened` handed as one batch to the blocking pool (`off_event_loop`) so a stale mount cannot freeze the event loop, then partitioned into dirs / openable files / skipped, and a Finder folder is granted recursively and recorded as a user-chosen workspace root (`workspace::grants::grant_chosen_root`) before its window opens; macOS `RunEvent::Reopen` recreates `main` (or a `doc-N`) at the most-recent workspace; Windows/Linux first launch scans `std::env::args()`; the Windows/Linux single-instance callback reuses `route_file_opens`
- status: shipped-on
- gate: always on; `handle_reopen` and `handle_finder_opened` are `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`; `partition_opened_urls`, `open_finder_directory` and `off_event_loop` compile only on macOS and in tests
- surfaces: Finder / Explorer double-click, Open With, `vmark` CLI, second launch, dock icon click (macOS)
- code: `src-tauri/src/files/open.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_setup.rs`, `src-tauri/src/single_instance.rs`
- rust: `files::open::{get_pending_file_opens, route_file_opens, handle_finder_opened, open_finder_directory, handle_reopen, partition_opened_urls, off_event_loop, record_document_window_focus, record_ready_document_window, remove_document_window}`
- docs: `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Opening a file from outside the current workspace", §"Shell CLI Command"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/files/open.test.rs` (13 tests, including the Finder folder grant and the off-event-loop hand-off)
- notes: Only existing files with a registered extension pass (`is_openable_supported`); others are logged and skipped. On Windows and Linux a directory argument is dropped by that filter, so a folder cannot be opened as a workspace from the command line or a second launch there.

### Single-instance guard (Windows & Linux)
- id: single-instance
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: On Windows and Linux a second launch forwards its file arguments to the VMark already running and surfaces a window, instead of starting a second process that would share and corrupt the first one's session.
- capabilities: plugin registered first; `argv[0]` dropped before the extension gate; forwarding runs off the callback thread so a slow stat cannot freeze the running app; a bare launch still surfaces a window (reveal an existing one or create `main`); keyed on the bundle identifier, so a `tauri dev` build is a separate instance; Linux registers the guard only when `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` is a `unix:path=` or `unix:abstract=` address the plugin's bus library can parse, and logs a warning when it skips the guard
- status: shipped-on
- gate: `#[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]` in `app_plugins.rs` plus a target-scoped Cargo dependency; Linux additionally gated by `session_bus::should_register_single_instance`
- surfaces: Explorer / desktop launcher double-click, second CLI invocation
- code: `src-tauri/src/single_instance.rs`, `src-tauri/src/session_bus.rs`, `src-tauri/src/app_plugins.rs`
- rust: `single_instance::{handle_second_launch, second_launch_with, openable_files_from_argv, surface_a_window, session_bus_present, warn_if_unguarded}`, `session_bus::should_register_single_instance`
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"One VMark at a Time on Windows and Linux"; `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Opening a file from outside the current workspace"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/single_instance.test.rs` (12 tests, mock app), `src-tauri/src/session_bus.test.rs` (12 tests); both modules compile on macOS so the tests run on the dev platform
- notes: macOS does not need it (one process per bundle id, opens arrive as `RunEvent::Opened`). On Linux with no usable session bus the app starts unguarded and a second launch opens a second VMark; troubleshooting.md says so.

### Minimize to tray on close (Windows)
- id: close-to-tray
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: An opt-in Windows setting makes closing the last document window park VMark in the system tray instead of quitting; left-click restores it, right-click offers Show VMark and Quit VMark.
- capabilities: only the LAST document window hides (others still close); nothing is torn down, so no save prompt runs; never hides during a quit; `start_quit` restores parked windows first so an unsaved-changes prompt is answerable; turning the setting off restores the window before removing the icon; tray install/remove idempotent on one tray id; setting pushed from the webview on mount and on change, Rust state starts disabled
- status: shipped-off
- gate: `general.closeToTray` = `false`; effective on Windows only (`cfg!(target_os = "windows")`); tray code needs tauri's `tray-icon` feature, enabled for the Windows target only
- surfaces: Settings → Files & Images → Quit Behavior (row rendered on Windows only); tray icon and its menu
- code: `src-tauri/src/close_to_tray/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/close_to_tray/tray.rs`, `src/hooks/useCloseToTraySync.ts`, `src-tauri/src/window_manager/window_events.rs`
- rust: `close_to_tray::{set_close_to_tray, decide_close_action, effective_enabled, hides_to_tray, CloseToTrayState}`, `close_to_tray::tray::{apply, restore_hidden_windows}`; consulted from `window_manager::window_events` and `quit::start_quit`
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Quit Behavior"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/close_to_tray/mod.test.rs` (7 tests: decision table, quit wins, never effective off Windows), `src-tauri/src/window_manager/window_events.test.rs`, `src/hooks/useCloseToTraySync.test.ts`, `src/pages/settings/FilesImagesSettings.test.tsx`, `src/hooks/lifecycle/__tests__/useWorkspaceLifecycle.test.ts`; `tray.rs` has no unit test and the tray was never exercised on a real Windows desktop
- notes: Windows only; #1419 (closed). The tray tooltip is `menu.app` and Quit reuses `menu.app.quit`; `tray.show` is the only new Rust locale key.

### macOS Dock recent documents
- id: dock-recent-documents
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: Every file added to Recent Files is registered with `NSDocumentController`, so it appears under Open Recent when the user right-clicks the Dock icon.
- capabilities: path must exist and be a regular file (typed `CommandError` otherwise); AppKit call dispatched onto the main thread through the event loop; main-thread refusals logged
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` on the module and its registry entry
- surfaces: Dock right-click; automatic on `recentsStore` add
- code: `src-tauri/src/dock_recent.rs`, `src/stores/workspaceStoreHelpers.ts`, `src/stores/recentsStore.ts`
- rust: `dock_recent::{register_dock_recent, register_recent_document}`
- docs: `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"macOS Dock Recent Documents"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/dock_recent.test.rs` (9 tests, injected registrar); the AppKit registrar itself runs only in a live app
- notes: The frontend invokes `register_dock_recent` on every platform and swallows the rejection off macOS, where the command is not registered.

### macOS quarantine clearing on workspace open
- id: mac-quarantine-strip
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: When a workspace is opened on macOS, VMark strips `com.apple.quarantine` from the folder and its direct children with a registered extension, so later Finder double-clicks are not silently dropped by Launch Services.
- capabilities: root + depth-1 only; registered-extension filter; per-entry failures counted, never fatal; returns `{stripped_count, error_count}`; one-time pinned toast the first time a strip clears something; IPC payload validated before use
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `advanced.clearMacQuarantineOnOpen` = `true`; command body `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`, returns empty stats elsewhere
- surfaces: automatic on workspace open; Settings → Advanced → Platform-Specific
- code: `src-tauri/src/quarantine.rs`, `src/services/macos/macQuarantineNotice.ts`
- rust: `quarantine::{strip_workspace_quarantine_cmd, strip_workspace_quarantine}`
- docs: `website/guide/settings.md` §"Platform-Specific"; `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"File Won't Open"
- tests: 6 Rust tests inline in `src-tauri/src/quarantine.rs` (`#[cfg(all(test, target_os = "macos"))]`), `src/services/macos/macQuarantineNotice.test.ts`
- notes: The `maybeStripMacQuarantine` docstring says "direct .md children"; the filter is every registered extension.

### macOS App Nap opt-out

### macOS smart-substitution suppression
- id: macos-smart-substitutions
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: Before any webview input, VMark writes `false` for the three smart-substitution keys in its own defaults domain, so `-->` in a Mermaid block is not turned into `—>` and straight quotes stay straight.
- capabilities: disables `NSAutomaticDashSubstitutionEnabled`, `NSAutomaticQuoteSubstitutionEnabled`, `NSAutomaticPeriodSubstitutionEnabled`; idempotent; first statement of `run()`
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`
- surfaces: automatic (before plugin registration)
- code: `src-tauri/src/text_substitution.rs`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`
- rust: `text_substitution::disable_smart_substitutions`
- docs: `website/guide/troubleshooting.md` §"Smart Quotes and Dashes on macOS"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/text_substitution.test.rs` (1 test)
- notes: Input methods and user text replacements are unaffected.

### macOS native menu polish (Help/Window registration + SF Symbol icons)
- id: macos-menu-polish
- feature: Native menu bar & menu events
- summary: On macOS the Help menu gets the native search field, the Window menu is registered with AppKit, and menu items get SF Symbol icons, all resolved by menu ID or position rather than translated title.
- capabilities: positional Help/Window lookup (last and second-to-last top-level menus); ID→symbol table plus a title table for muda predefined items; per-submenu fallback icons for dynamic items (recent files, recent workspaces, genies); re-applied after every menu rebuild and genie refresh, before conditional items are re-hidden; ends with a traffic-light repair
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`
- surfaces: menu bar
- code: `src-tauri/src/macos_menu.rs`, `src-tauri/src/macos_menu_icons.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/commands.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/dynamic.rs`

- docs: none
- tests: `src-tauri/src/macos_menu.test.rs` (14 tests: icon table and positional premise checked against the builder sources)

### Native edit actions bridge (cut/copy/paste/select-all)
- id: native-webview-edit
- feature: Platform integrations (macOS/Windows/Linux)
- summary: On macOS the editor context menu sends Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All down the native responder chain, so a menu-initiated paste keeps full clipboard fidelity (HTML, images).
- capabilities: exact-match action parsing (`cut`, `copy`, `paste`, `selectAll`); focus gate on the main thread, atomic with the send, so a background window cannot copy from or paste into another; 2 s responder timeout; distinct errors for unhandled selector and unfocused window; other platforms fall back to `document.execCommand` or a plain-text paste
- status: shipped-on, windows-linux-gap
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` for the real dispatch; the frontend invokes it only when `isMacPlatform()`
- surfaces: editor context menu, toolbar clipboard actions
- code: `src-tauri/src/webview_edit.rs`, `src/services/editor/clipboardBridge.ts`
- rust: `webview_edit::{trigger_webview_edit, EditAction::parse, outcome_to_result}`
- docs: none
- tests: `src-tauri/src/webview_edit.test.rs` (4 tests)
- notes: Still returns `Result<(), String>` (on the command-error ratchet).

### `vmark` CLI installer
- id: vmark-cli-installer
- feature: CLI installer
- summary: A Help-menu item installs or removes a `/usr/local/bin/vmark` launcher, so files and folders can be opened from the terminal.
- capabilities: single toggling item; exact-content ownership check (a foreign file at the path is refused, never overwritten); admin elevation via `osascript … with administrator privileges`; POSIX single-quoting of every interpolated path; post-install verification (regular file, exact content); localized result dialog (Installed / AlreadyInstalled / Removed / NotInstalled), none on user cancel; installed script is `open -b app.vmark "$@"`
- status: shipped-on, macos-only
- gate: `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` module and menu item `install-cli`
- surfaces: Help → "Shell Command: Install 'vmark' in PATH…"; the installed `vmark` command
- code: `src-tauri/src/cli_install/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/cli_install/dialog.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/localized/window_help_menu.rs`, `src-tauri/src/menu/events/dispatch.rs`
- rust: `cli_install::{cli_install, cli_uninstall, cli_install_status}`, `cli_install::dialog::run_install_toggle`
- docs: `website/guide/workspace-management.md` §"Shell CLI Command"
- tests: `src-tauri/src/cli_install/tests.rs` (14 tests)
- notes: Not a Tauri command — reachable only from the native menu dispatcher. `dialog.rs` must spawn with `tauri::async_runtime::spawn`, not `tokio::spawn`, from the menu callback (#1030). `run_admin_shell` must only receive app-constructed commands. The installed script's comment still says "Toggle via: VMark > Help > Install/Uninstall 'vmark' Command", which is not the shipped label. The script targets `app.vmark`, so it always opens the release app, never a dev build.

### Frontend platform detection
- id: frontend-platform-detection

## Maintaining this ledger

- **When a feature ships, moves or dies**, edit its block in the same change, and bump the area's `Verified:` line only if you re-read the whole area. `pnpm lint:feature-map` fails on a block citing a deleted file, a feature with no block, or a block naming a feature the spine does not have.
- **When you add a production file**, give it an owner in `scripts/feature-map.json` — a feature's `paths`, or `infrastructure.paths` if it has no user-facing role. CI fails on an unowned file.
- **Re-read by freshness, not by calendar.** `dev-docs/feature-metrics.md` lists commits per feature since its blocks were verified; the top of that list is the next re-inspection. Re-verify a whole area at once, then move its `Verified:` line to the commit you read.
- **Regenerate the numbers, never type them**: `pnpm gen:feature-ledger`. Counts, the at-a-glance index and the freshness table live there, not here.
- **What this ledger deliberately does not contain**: scores, grades, priorities, target dates or owners — the same rule as the metrics table, for the reason recorded in `scripts/baseline-review-schedule.json`. Findings are evidence with a path attached; what to do about them is a maintainer decision made elsewhere. Decisions from the first edition's fix plan are tracked in the maintainer-local `dev-docs/plans/20260907-feature-ledger-fixes.md` (`bash scripts/check-feature-ledger-phase.sh <0-7|all>`); the current fix plan is `.claude/tdd-guardian/20260927-ledger-findings-fixes.md`.
- **What was and was not verified**: every path cited was read or grep-confirmed at the area's `Verified:` commit; every `docs` claim was grep-confirmed against the page. Nothing here was verified by running the app.
